Section 18/19/20 hooks from earlier builds: `eventRoutines`, `callOwed`, `eventGuestDays`, `occasionDoorOpen`, `affinityPlan`, `eventPlan`, `eventsOn`.

## Machines and wear (section 17, the systems engineer, 0.33.x)
Pillar 2, Half B (`plans/pillar2-plan.md`, "Machines and jobs"). Section 17 sits right after section 14 (and after 15 and 16 once they're in). B1 is data and logic only: nothing in it draws anything or calls `R()`, and nothing in it runs unless something asks (a game with `?nosave` never touches it), so cadet_test is ALL IDENTICAL.

- **The registry.** `machineList()` gives every machine: `{ id, kind, at, room, face, panel, deck, seed }` (and `tier` if the machine sets its own), from `DECKS[n].machines` (the architects add these with the utility rooms, A5), plus the stand-ins in `STUB_MACHINES` (44 since 0.33.2; Decks 1 and 3 use the bridge architect's agreed ids, like `3-filter-1`, `1-databus-1`). A stand-in's `at` and `panel` are its room's middle tile (the lift's is the lift). A stand-in drops out as soon as its deck has a real machine with its id or in its room, so the galley's ovens, the washers and the lift stay until someone adds them. A save's ids that are gone are dropped; new ids start fresh.
- **Kinds (`MACHINE_KINDS`, 29 since 0.33.2).** `oven`, `range`, `galleyplant`, `extractor`, `chiller`, `washer`, `lift`, `lights` (a deck's light relays), `junction` (a power junction), `scrubber`, `heater`, `recycler`, `filter`, `pump`, `uv`, `irrigation`, `nutrient`, `airplant`, `fan`, `manifold`, `gasplant`, `battery`, `relay`, `databus`, `array`, `doorgear`, `hullseal`, `reactor`, `coil`. Each has its system, `life` (days from 100 to 0 on an average day, never serviced), `time` (the share of its wear that comes with time; the rest comes with use), `use` (what wears it), `scope` (where its effects are felt: its own deck, or the whole ship), `w` (its weight in its system), `tier` (who may work on it) and `part` (what a repair needs). An architect adding a machine of a new kind tells the systems engineer, who adds the kind.
- **State.** `SHIP.machines[id] = { cond, state, lastService }`. `cond` runs 0–100; `machineState(cond)`: healthy 75+, worn 50+, faulty 25+, failing 10+, broken under 10. `lastService` is absolute ship minutes, or null. Always read through `machinesNow()`, which runs every ship hour up to and including the current one first (since 0.33.1; each hour's wear is applied at its start).
- **Wear, on paper.** One ship hour at a time: `life`'s share of the hour, split between time and use, up to half as fast again as the machine wears down, times a little variety from `schedU(machine seed, hour, 7)`. Use comes from the schedules (`machUseDay(d)`, from `shipDay(d)`, cached for a few days):
    - ovens, the range and the extractor: people cooking that hour (`galleyCooks(t)` from section 16, summed minute by minute), or before section 16 meals that hour and the next; the galley plant and chiller: meals;
    - washers: laundry visits, plus the uniforms of everyone who worked the day before, washed 06:00–10:00 (about six to a load);
    - the lift: every ride (`commute`) on the ship;
    - scrubbers, heaters, light relays, the gas plant and the bridge relays: person-hours on their deck;
    - the recycler, water filters, pumps and UV: water use (a shower 1; a basin, hand-wash or toilet .25); irrigation and nutrient dosing: time in the garden;
    - the air plant, fan, manifold, battery, power junctions, data bus, array, door gear, hull seals, reactor and coils: time only.
  **Every counter is measured against an average day** (`machNorm()`: the mean of days 1–7, worked out once, 0.33.2). So a machine lasts its `life` on an average day however the schedules change; only busier and quieter hours and days differ. (0.33.0 used fixed numbers, and 0.26.1's extra washing wore the recycler out in two days.)
  Because every hour's wear depends only on the hour, the schedules and the condition, the result is the same whether time moves minute by minute, by odd jumps or in one leap (machine_check proves it). Wear numbers today (no repairs, from a fresh ship): ovens faulty at about day 3½ and broken at 6½; scrubbers 3 and 6½; the reactor still worn at day 14.
- **A fresh ship** starts every machine at 70–100 (`machFresh`, from its seed), so a few are already worn on day 1. The clock going back (New game) starts the machines afresh.
- **Servicing.** `serviceMachine(id, to = 100)` puts a machine back to `to` and sets `lastService`. B2's jobs call it; so does machine_check's ideal crew.
- **Systems (`shipSystems()`).** Air, water, power, heat, food, waste (and laundry), hull, comms (and sensors), propulsion and transport (the lift and doors, 0.33.1), 0–100 each. A machine counts by how well it works (`machWorks`: fully while healthy, 85% at the worn/faulty line, 50% at faulty/failing, 20% at failing/broken, 0 at 0), weighted by `w` and pulled toward the system's worst machine (`.6 × average + .4 × worst`). Dependencies, only once the feeding system is below 75: power feeds everything but the hull (down to half); water feeds food (the garden feeds the galley) and waste. `shipHealth()` blends the systems (air and power 3, water, heat, food and hull 2, the rest 1).
- **Consequences (`shipEffects(deck)`).** What the machines do to people on a deck now, as the design doc's stages (`MACHINE_FX`: a faulty machine counts .35 of its full effect, failing .7, broken all of it; worn nothing; several of a kind count by their average):
  ```js
  shipEffects(deck) -> {
    restore: { hunger, energy, hygiene, fitness, social },   // x what an action gives back (1 = normal; a weak shower 0.4)
    drain:   { hunger, energy, hygiene, fitness, social },   // x how fast a need runs down (1 = normal)
    walk,      // x walking speed
    cook,      // x cooking time (the galley engineer)
    lift,      // x lift ride time
    mood,      // points off a 0-100 mood, 0 to -30
    health,    // shipHealth()
    notes: [{ machine, kind, state, stage: 'minor'|'noticeable'|'serious', text }],   // "Showers run weak"
  }
  ```

  The table: the recycler weakens showers ship-wide (hygiene restore down to ×.4, mood −5); Deck 4's scrubbers make the air stuffy (energy drains up to ×1.4, mood −6); the air plant and fan do the same ship-wide, more gently; the heating plant gives cold cabins on Deck 4 (sleep restores energy down to ×.7, mood −5); washers send uniforms back grubby (hygiene drains up to ×1.3, mood −4); ovens, the range and the galley plant slow cooking (up to ×2.6 together) and give smaller meals (hunger restore down to ×.77); a deck's light relays dim it (walking down to ×.88; Deck 3's are `3-lights-1` in the recycler room, Deck 6's hang off its power junctions `6-junction-1` and `-2`); the lift runs slow (rides up to ×2). On top: power under 50 slows walking everywhere, and a ship under 70 health takes up to 10 more off everyone's mood. Cached until anything changes (`MACH_REV`). Since 0.33.2: water filters, pumps, UV, the extractor (like the galley plant), nutrient dosing, the bridge relays and data bus (Deck 1 only) and a chiller (`spoil`: how fast the cold store's food spoils, for the galley engineer).
- **`shipEffectsAt(deck, t)`** (0.33.2): the effects at ship minute t, read from `MACH_HIST` (each hour's conditions once run, kept 8 days). It's pure: it never runs the machines. Hours after the last one run use the latest, hours before the record use its first, and with no record at all (under `?nosave` until something asks) everything is as new. It's for anything replayed over past times: the crew's paper needs (section 14) and the galley's minute-by-minute food (16). `shipEffects(deck)` is the effects now; it brings the machines up to the present first, so it must never be called while `shipDay` is planning (the plans feed the wear).
- **`machineInfo(id)`** gives a machine's definition, kind and state together, for the job board and the device.
- **Saving.** Section 13 now has `SAVE_EXTRA`: functions that add their part to the save just before it's written (one line in `saveGame`, inert while nothing is registered). Section 17's adds `ship.machines = { id: [cond, lastService] }` (cond in full, so a reload wears on exactly as before) and `ship.machineHour`. `machinesLoad(SAVED)` reads it back at load; a save without machines (any save before 0.33.0), or with nonsense, means fresh machines worn from the start. It works in `v: 1` saves and carries over unchanged when the player engineer bumps the save to `v: 2`.
- **machine_check.py.** `python3 machine_check.py BUILD.html [DAYS]` (DAYS defaults to 14; about 15 seconds). With `?nosave`:
    1. A while of play never runs the machines ahead of the clock (since 0.40.2 the player's needs read `shipEffects` every ship minute, so play does run them), and `shipEffects` is cheap to read.
    2. No repairs: conditions only fall; nothing faulty in the first two days; something broken by day 14 but not the slow kinds; ship health falls but stays above 0; every deck's effects stay in range. It prints ship health by day, the median day each kind turns faulty, failing and broken, and Deck 4's and Deck 3's effects at the end.
    3. The same wear every way: twice, in one jump, at odd minutes, with the schedule caches cleared.
    4. An ideal crew (the day shift services the 3 most worn machines that aren't healthy, every hour 08:00–17:00): nothing gets past worn and ship health stays at 90 or more.
    5. The save hook and `machinesLoad` round trip, and junk saves are refused.
- **machine_check numbers (0.33.0, stand-ins):** no repairs, ship health by day 98 96 91 85 76 66 55 46 39 33 27 24 21 19; at day 14, 21 of 35 broken, the reactor and hull seals still worn. Ideal crew: 106 jobs in 14 days (about 7½ a day), ship health 99–100.

### The job list and supplies, on paper (B2 and B3, 0.33.1)

The lead's go (2026-10-15): on paper only. There's no board drawing and no device section until the player engineer's device and the utility rooms land.

- **The hour (`jobsHour(h)`).** `machinesNow()` now runs every ship hour up to and including the current one. Each hour, first the jobs, then that hour's wear (worn ahead, at the hour's start):
    1. Crew jobs that are done put their machine right (service to 100, repair to 95, overhaul to 90) or move their cargo.
    2. On Wednesdays at 10:00 the delivery lands in the cargo hold (`SHIP.hold`). It holds whatever tops storage back up to `STOCK`, plus a week of meals (1,050) once the galley keeps a cold store.
    3. Jobs are posted, one per machine at most, and kept up to date while nobody has claimed them.
    4. The crew on shift claim what they can.
    5. Jobs that are done stay on the list for two days, as history.
- **Jobs (`SHIP.jobs`).** Each job is `{ id, machine, deck, kind, urgency, posted, found, claimedBy, parts, status }`, plus `start`, `segs`, `done` and `end` once claimed, and `cargo` for restocks.
    - **Ids** come from the machine and the hour (`4-scrubber-1@83`), so the same ship posts the same jobs.
    - **What a machine's state posts:**
        - worn: `service`, urgency 0, 1 part. It's `found` 2–24 hours later (on rounds) and only goes on the board then: the design's sharp-eyed tech.
        - faulty: `repair`, urgency 1, 2 parts.
        - failing: the same repair, urgency 2.
        - broken: `overhaul`, urgency 3, 3 parts.
    - **When things change:** an unclaimed job is upgraded as its machine gets worse, and closed if the machine is put right some other way.
    - **Status:** `open`, `waiting` (needs a part nobody can make: `why: 'needs filter'`), `claimed` or `done`.
    - **Restocks:** a `restock` job (`machine: null`) moves the hold into storage (`cargo: 'stores'`, 30 minutes on Deck 5). A second kind carries 150 meals up to Deck 3's cold store when `SHIP.galley.cold.meals` is under 150 (`cargo: 'meals'`). The meals run, and the week of meals in the delivery, happen only while section 16 says carts carry the food (`FOOD.restock === 'carts'`; while it's `'rule'` the galley tops itself up at 10:30). One owner for the rule, so meals never arrive twice; the switch is one line in section 16 when B3's carts are drawn (0.33.3).
- **Who claims what.** It's worked out at the start of each hour. The most urgent jobs go first, then the longest found. Each goes to the free crew member with the lowest tier who may do it.
    - **Works crew** (`work:works`) take everything but the reactor and coils, by tier (`CREW_TIER`: junior 2, officer and senior 3, chief and up 4).
    - **Engine crew** (`work:engine`) take the reactor and coils, whatever the tier.
    - **A claim must fit inside the work block** the person is in (not mid-ride, not on a tea break), from the hour to their return to their desk.
    - **Easy routine jobs wait for you.** Jobs at your tier (`PLAYER_TIER` 1), below urgent, wait `CREW_LEAVE` (180) minutes of the day shift (08:00–17:00, `DAY_SHIFT`) after they're found, so the crew leave them to you first; one found in the evening waits for your morning (`leaveEnd(j)`, since 0.33.16; before that it was 3 clock hours). From 0.33.32 a job is held on to the end of the shift its wait runs out in, at most `HOLD_MAX` (6) at once, and never on your days off (`playerShiftOn`). Food runs that aren't urgent wait an hour. About 3.7 machine jobs a day are left for you this way (machine_check, "jobs left for the player"), plus a daytime food run.
- **A claim on paper** is a run of segments shaped like `planDay`'s:
    - to Deck 5 (lift, 2 min), fetching parts (10 min), or making missing parts in the machine shop (60 min each);
    - the lift to the machine's deck;
    - the work at its panel: 20 / 45 / 90 minutes for service / repair / overhaul, 25% longer per tier above 1. The place is `panelPlace(id)`, the place with `machine: id`, or null until the rooms land;
    - back to their desk's deck.
  Parts come off the shelf when it's claimed.
- **Food by cart (0.33.13, with the galley engineer).** One rule, `cartClaim(t, cold, { replay })`, gives `null` or `{ meals, arriveAt }` for the hour of `t`:
    - a run sets off whenever the cold store is under `CART_URGENT` (90), and on the day shift (09:00–17:00) whenever it's under `CART_DAILY` (250);
    - it carries `min(CART_MEALS, cap − cold)` (150, never past `FOOD.coldCap`);
    - it lands `CART_RUN` (40) minutes after the hour, later when the lift is slow.
  - **Replays** (`replay: true`) are pure and assume the hold is stocked.
  - **A live call** is limited by `SHIP.hold.meals` and remembered for its hour (`CART_RUNS`), so asking again gives the same run. It takes the meals out of the hold and queues the run (`CART_PENDING`). `cartPost` (from `machinesNow`) puts it on the job list, pushed by the free Works crew member on shift with the lowest tier, or unseen if nobody's free.
  - **The galley lands the meals** at `arriveAt`; section 17 never writes the cold store.
  - **Your cart run (0.33.16).** A run that isn't urgent (the cold store at `CART_URGENT` or more) and is set off on the day shift is left on the board for you for an hour (urgency 1); then the crew take it at the next hour. Urgent runs (urgency 2) and night runs go to the crew at once. One nobody takes within `CART_GONE` (240 minutes) goes unseen. Yours ends with `finishJob`; the galley's books have landed the meals at `arriveAt` either way (an open question: whether your run should land them when you unload).
  - **The hold** starts with, and is topped up every Wednesday to, `DELIVERY.meals` (1,200). All of this only while section 16's `FOOD.restock === 'carts'`.
- **Docked carts (0.33.15).** The `cartDock` kit hands each parked cart's group to `cartDockLive(group, dock)` as Deck 5 is built (the Deck 6 architect's inert hook, 0.30.10). `worksTick` hides a dock's cart while it's out on a run (`cartsOut(t)`: from taking it at `5-cart-N` to putting it back), checked once a ship minute. A run takes and returns the same dock, one whose cart isn't already out.
- **Cart runs on the deck.** A restock job is a hover-cart run: a cart from a dock (`CART_AT`: `5-cart-1`, `-2`), loading in the cargo hold (`HOLD_AT`: `5-hold-1`, `-2`), then up the lift to Deck 3's cold store (`COLD_AT`: `3-cold-1`, `-2`) or to the parts shelves, and back to the dock. Segments carry `hold` (since 0.33.14): `'cart'` from taking the cart at the dock until it's loaded and again on the way back, `'cartfull'` from loaded until unloaded. `jobWhere` passes it on. The performance engineer's crowd (0.27.5) draws the cart when section 10 adds `'+' + w.hold` to the pose (the sim engineer's 0.26.7). The places are asked of the architects; until they exist, those steps are spent where the tech stands.
- **Supplies (`SHIP.stores`, `SHIP.hold`).** `STOCK` is what storage keeps of each part, for example 8 filters and 6 relays and elements. When a part is out, filters, membranes, cells and control rods (`NO_MAKE`) leave the job waiting for the delivery. Any other part gets made in the machine shop, which is slower.
- **The overlay (`jobWhere(person, t)`).** It returns what `whereIs` would, plus `job`, while a crew member is on a job, else null. **Wired in since 0.33.7:** `whereIs` (section 14) asks `jobWhere` first (one line, inert until section 17 has run: `jobWhere.on`), so the crew on the deck walk to their jobs: to Deck 5 for parts, then the lift to the machine's deck, through its service hatch to the panel place (`panelPlace`, the place with `machine: id`), working there, and back to their desk. Claiming reads the plain plan (`planWhere`), never `whereIs`, so there's no loop. Fetching parts happens at the Deck 6 architect's `storage` places in main storage (`5-parts-1`..`4`, `FETCH_AT`; since 0.33.11 the shelf whose `stock` lists the job's part comes first, `shelvesFor`) and making them in the machine shop (`5-make-1`, `-2`, `MAKE_AT`): the first one no other job is using for those minutes, else nowhere (they stand where they are). Since 0.33.10 section 14 owns `planWhere` (the plan alone, with every field section 10 needs) and `whereIs` (jobs first, with a guard against re-entry); section 17's own `planWhere` is gone. `machinesNow` has a fast path for the common case (already up to date), since `whereIs` now asks it on every call.
- **The Works bay job board (0.33.8).** The `jobBoard` kit hands its canvas texture to `jobBoardLive(t)` as Deck 5 is built (one inert line in section 4b), and `worksTick()` (one line in the main loop, after `galleyTick()`) redraws it, in the kit's own look, whenever the job list changes or the hour turns. It shows up to six cards, most urgent first: the machine (numbered when a deck has several of a kind), its deck and room, and the state (`service due`, `repair`, `failing: urgent`, `broken: overhaul`, `needs filter`), plus who's on it. Then the count of open jobs and the shift. No new geometry, textures or `R()`; Deck 5's draw calls are unchanged. The deck's static cards are only seen if section 17 is missing.
- **The device's jobs section (0.33.9).** `device.addSection('jobs', 'Jobs', jobsSection)` (section 15's placeholder device). It lists up to five open jobs from the board. Each one at your tier and not engine work has a "Take it" button. The job you've taken shows its parts (with what's in storage), where it is, and "Drop it". You hold one job at a time (`claimJob` refuses a second). A job you don't finish goes back on the board after `YOU_KEEP` (12 ship hours) for the crew to take. Doing the job at the machine is the player engineer's repair activity, still to come; it ends with `finishJob(id)`.
- **Your side**, for the device and board later:
    - `jobsBoard()`: jobs found and not done, most urgent first;
    - `claimJob(id)`: yours, if it's at your tier and not engine work; the crew leave it alone;
    - `dropJob(id)`;
    - `takePart(part)`: off the shelf; carrying it is the player engineer's;
    - `returnPart(part)`: back on the shelf (0.33.12);
    - `touchJob(id)`: progress on your job (a part fitted, repair minutes done); the 12-hour lapse counts from the last progress (0.33.12);
    - `finishJob(id)`: puts the machine right now.
- **Saving.** The save hook also writes `ship.jobs`, `ship.stores` and `ship.hold`. A save from 0.33.0 loads with an empty job list and full shelves.
- **`JOBS_ON`** (default true) is off only in machine_check's no-repair and ideal-crew runs.
- **machine_check (0.33.1) adds:**
    - the real Works crew over 14 days: nothing breaks, ship health 85 or more, shelves never below 0, deliveries carried to storage, easy jobs left 3 hours for you, nobody on two jobs at once, every job inside its person's work block, `jobWhere` agreeing, and the same twice, in one jump and at odd minutes;
    - your own job claimed, left alone by the crew and finished;
    - jobs and supplies in the save round trip, running on the same after a reload.
- **Numbers (0.33.1, 36 stand-ins).**
    - **No repairs:** ship health by day 98 96 91 85 76 66 54 45 37 30 24 20 18 16.
    - **The real Works crew:** 101 jobs in 14 days (99 services and 2 restock runs, about 7 a day), 13 parts made in the machine shop, at most 4 jobs on the board at once. Nothing gets past worn, and ship health stays 99–100.
    - **What that means:** the crew catch everything at the service stage, so the player will see few faults. That's a tuning question for when the player's loop lands: a longer `CREW_LEAVE`, slower rounds, or more of the Works crew busy on other work.

### Looks, the Ship health board, mood and the chiller (0.33.17)

Answers 2, 3, 7 and 22. Section 17 gives the numbers; others draw and use them. All of them read the machines now (`machinesNow`) except the two pure ones, and are cached until anything changes (`MACH_REV`). Ask once a ship minute or on a deck build, not every frame, and never from inside `shipDay` planning.

- **`machineLook(id)`** → `{ state, blink, dim, drip, flicker, running, cond, kind }`, or null for an unknown id:
    - `state`: `'fine'` (healthy), `'worn'`, `'poor'` (faulty) or `'failing'` (failing or broken);
    - `dim` 0–1: how far its screens and lamps dim (worn .15, poor .35, failing .65);
    - `blink`: a failing or broken panel's warm-red blink;
    - `drip`: a water machine (recycler, filters, pumps, UV, valves, irrigation, manifold, heating pumps) that's poor or worse, or a worn valve;
    - `flicker` 0–1: how often the lights it feeds flicker (light relays 1, junctions .6, bridge relays .5, the data bus .4, times how bad it is);
    - `running`: started by a tap (`machineUse`, 0.33.18) and still going.
- **`deckLook(deck)`** → `{ dim, flicker, spray, wilt, drip }`: the signs a whole deck shows. `dim` from the deck's walking effect (lights and power), `flicker` the worst on the deck, `spray` the shower's strength (1 full, from the hygiene restore), `wilt` 0–1 from the deck's irrigation and nutrient dosing, `drip` any dripping machine on the deck.
- **`shipHealthRows()`** → `[{ key, name, value, state, worst }]`: the "Ship health" board for the bridge (Deck 1) and the Works bay (Deck 5). The first row is the ship (`key: 'ship'`), then the ten systems, worst first. `state` as `machineLook`'s (90+ fine, 70+ worn, 45+ poor, else failing); `worst` is the system's worst machine that isn't healthy (`'Washer 1, Deck 4'`, `machineLabel(m)`), or null.
- **`shipMoodAt(deck, t)`** → `{ mood, health, about }`: for the sim engineer's `crewMood` and `crewFeeling` (answer 22). `mood` is `shipEffectsAt`'s (0 to −30); `about` is what the crew grumble about (`'the showers'`, `'the stuffy air'`, `'late meals'`…, from the most serious note on the deck, then whichever upsets most; `'the state of the ship'` below 70 health), or null while all is well. Pure.
- **`chillerAt(t)`** → `{ cond, state, spoil }`: the cold store's chiller for the galley engineer (answer 7). `spoil` is the share of the cold store's meals lost each ship hour: 0 while the chiller works at 85% or better (healthy or worn), rising to `CHILL_SPOIL` (5%) broken. Pure, from the hour-by-hour record (`histHour`). The galley bins the food and sends the Mail.

### Tapping machines, tier locks and short supplies (0.33.18)

Answers 5, 4 and 6.

- **`machineUse(id, who = 'you')`** → `{ text, ran, until }`. What a tap on a machine does; the player engineer's tap calls it, after the architects' own item text.
    - Every machine gives a line: its name and condition (`'Washer 1: showing its age, due a service.'`), plus "It's on the job board." or "It's your job." when there's a job for it.
    - A machine with a use (`MACHINE_USES`: washer 45 minutes, oven 20, range 15, extractor 15, main fan 30, lights and the lift's call panel 1) starts: `ran` is true and `machineLook(id).running` is true until `until`. Faulty and failing ones say so ("It sounds rough.").
    - Your use wears it a little (a washer load .3 condition points), applied with the next ship hour's wear (`MACH_EXTRA`, saved as `ship.machineUse`). A crew member's use (`who` = their id) adds nothing, since their use is already counted from the schedules.
    - A broken machine doesn't start ("Nothing happens."). An unknown id gives `{ text: null, ran: false }`.
- **Tier locks.** The format is `service: ['h,21,4', { key: 'h,20,16', tier: 2 }]`: a plain key, or a key with a tier (closets 1, plant rooms 2, batteries and junctions 3). Section 4 reads either form (two inert lines). `hatchPassable` lets through only those whose `hatchTier(who)` is at least the hatch's tier: you are `PLAYER_TIER` (1), Works crew `CREW_TIER` by rank (junior 2, officer and senior 3, chief and up 4), the engine crew 4. Untagged hatches behave as before. When tagging, check that every Works place and machine panel behind a tier-3 hatch is only for crew of that tier (`deck_check`, `day_check`).
- **Supplies short, told by Mail.**
    - The weekly delivery is late about one week in five, by one or two days (`deliveryLate(d)`, from the week's Monday; Friday at the latest).
    - Stores sends Mail (`mailSend({ at, from: 'Stores', subject, body })`, section 15's, guarded until it's there):
        - when the delivery is late, on Wednesday at 10:00;
        - when a part nobody can make (`NO_MAKE`) runs out, once until it's restocked;
        - when the hold's food falls under `FOOD_LOW` (300 meals, two days) while carts carry the food.
    - Parts the machine shop can make don't mail (they're never short for long). `SHIP.short` remembers what's been told and is saved.
    - machine_check, 28 days with the real crew: 12 mails, one late week, landed on its late day.

### Chores and openJobs (0.33.19)

The user asked for more jobs on the board.

- **`CHORES`** is a table of job types that don't wait for a machine to wear. For each type: `tier`, `per` (how many a day), `parts`, `text`, and `where()`, which lists the candidates `{ key, title, deck, room, machine?, tier?, bring?, stops: [{ deck, placeId, mins, tag }] }`. Optional fields: `helps` (what finishing does), `finds` (a round), `leave` (its own wait for you, in minutes of the day shift).
    - **Tier 1, for you:**
        (`per` a day: lamp .5, vent .4, calibrate .7, delivery .5, round .4, fault .3; crew filter 1, seal .7.)
        - `lamp`: replace a lamp in a lift lobby or the laundry, 15 minutes, brings a `lamp` (new in `STOCK`);
        - `vent`: clean a vent, 20 minutes; the deck's scrubbers, air plant and fan get +4;
        - `calibrate`: a galley machine or a cart dock, 15 minutes, +6;
        - `delivery`: pick up on Deck 5, drop off (`DELIVERIES`: linen to the laundry, medical supplies to the infirmary, store stock, spares to the bridge, dry goods to the galley);
        - `round`: an inspection round of three stops on Deck 3, 4 or 5; worn machines' service jobs on that deck are found at once;
        - `fault`: a tripped breaker or a jammed cart dock, 10 minutes, waits only 30 minutes of the day shift for you.
    - **Tier 2–3, for the crew:**
        - `filter`: filter swaps, +10, uses a filter;
        - `seal`: pressure-seal checks, +5;
        - `calibrate`: the bridge relays, data bus, array and gas plant.
- **Posting.** `choresOfDay(d)` picks each day's chores from `schedU` (the type's seed, the day), each at an hour from 07:00 to 18:00. `choresHour` posts them, one open chore per place, and never while their parts are under half of `STOCK` (repairs come first). A claimed chore whose unmakeable part has run out is dropped, not left waiting.
- **Doing them.** The crew walk the stops (`jobRun`). You take one with `claimJob` and finish it with `finishJob`; `choreDone` then does its work. Chores are `kind: 'chore'`, with `chore`, `target`, `title`, `bring`, `tier`, `stops` and `leave` on the job. `JOB_TYPES.chore` exists so section 15's repair loop can time a chore on a machine.
- **Tiers.** `jobTier(j)` is the job's own tier or its machine's, never below the tier of the rooms it goes into. `roomTier(n, room)` reads the deck's tagged service hatches; a hatch's tier belongs to the room behind it (the `still` utility room, else the side that isn't a hall).
- **`openJobs(forWho = 'you')`** → `[{ id, title, kind, deck, at, room, place, tier, mins, parts, bring, urgency, state, where }]`: what forWho may take now, most urgent first. `deck`, `at`, `room` and `place` are the main stop, for the interactions engineer's arrow. Machine jobs' titles start with a verb ("Service: Oven 2"). `jobStops(j)` lists the stops in order: chores give their own tags (`pickup`, `drop`, `inspect`…), after `fetch` if they need parts.
- **The crew stand at a stop's place** only if it's a machine panel or a storage spot no other job is using; anywhere else they work where they are, so they never take someone's desk or seat (day_check caught a delivery at Dr Oduya's desk). `jobStops` and `openJobs` still give you the real place.
- **machine_check, 14 days:** chores lamp 5, vent 4, calibrate 9, delivery 9, round 4, fault 2, filter 14, seal 10. The real crew do 210 jobs (48 chores), ship health 97–100, and about 5.9 jobs a day wait for you (the lead's target, 5–6).

### Events and favours (Pillar 3, C7, 0.33.24)

The hooks the plan (plans/pillar3-plan.md, "How events tie into what's already built") gives section 17. All of them are inert while `eventNow()` is null (or not there yet) and nobody has posted a favour.

- **Wear.** `eventWear(sys, h)` reads `eventMod('wear', sys, h * 60)` (section 18). A number between 0 and 20 multiplies that system's wear for the hour; anything else means no change. The minute is passed so an `eventMod` that knows the event's timetable can answer for past hours as the machines catch up.
- **Event jobs.** An event's data may carry `jobs: { <phase>: [spec, ...] }`, where a spec is `{ id?, kind, title?, where?, place?, machine?, deck?, tier?, mins?, parts?, urgency?, leave?, bring? }`. Each hour, `eventJobsHour` posts the current phase's specs once (id `event:<event>:<phase>:<spec id>@<day>`) as chores with `event: <id>`:
    - **A kind from `CHORES` with no place** picks one of that kind's own places (seeded by the job's id).
    - **Otherwise the job goes to:** `place` (a place id), `machine` (its panel), or `where` (a word in a room's name, or a place type). It prefers a standing place there, then falls back to the room's middle tile (`at`).
    - **Titles** come from `EVENT_JOB_TEXT` and the room, unless the spec gives one. Tier defaults to 1.
    - **Timing:** small ones at your tier wait `leave` (default 60) minutes of the day shift for you; the crew take the rest at once.
    - **`postEventJob(spec, { event, forYou })`** posts one straight away, and claims it for you when a meeting's choice gives it to you.

    `eventDef(id)` reads `SHIP_EVENTS` whether it's a list (today's traditions) or keyed by id (the plan).
- **Favours** (for the social engineer, section 20).
    - **`favourPost({ from, title, kind?, place? | machine? | where?, deck?, stops?, parts?, mins?, bring?, expires? })`** returns an id. The favour is offered, never on the board, and never taken by the crew.
    - **`favourAccept(id)`** makes it yours; you can hold it alongside a board job. **`favourDecline(id)`** turns it down.
    - **Its stops** are done with `jobStepDone`. `jobStops` ends with `{ step: 'return', person: from }`, which `jobStepDone` won't take.
    - **Once the stops are done** its work is done (`jobEffect`; a `kind` from `CHORES` does that chore's good). **`favourTurnIn(id)`** at the person completes it. A favour with no stops (a word, a hand) is done when it's handed in.
    - **Lapsing:** an offered or accepted favour not done by `expires` (default a day and a half) lapses (`lapsed: true`).
    - **Lookups:** `favoursOf(crewId)` and `myFavours()`. Favours are saved with the jobs.
- **`openJobs`** items carry `event` (null for ordinary jobs) and fall back to a stop's own `at` tile.
- **Cold cabins (0.33.31).** `coldCabinsHour(h)`: guests aboard (`eventMod('effect', 'guests')`) and Deck 4's heating faulty or worse. Scored hourly as `eventScore('coldCabins', 1)`.
- **Fitted to section 18's ion storm (0.33.26).** Wear asks by system and by machine kind. Event jobs come from `eventMod('jobs')`, posted in the hour of their `at` minute, once per run, with section 18's stop shapes (`eventStopOf`). Finishing one yourself calls `eventMine('jobs', 1)`. Blackouts (`blackoutHour`, `blackoutAt`, `deckLook(deck).dark`) are scored as the `blackout` thread.
- **Trays by cart (0.33.25).** `cartTrays({ trays, at, to, event? })`, called by the galley when an event asks for trays. It's a job from the servery to the bridge (or `to`), once a day, left for you 30 minutes, then pushed by the crew on a full cart. `traysArrivedAt(day, to)` (0.33.28) gives when they arrived, for the galley's bridgeHungry score.

**Still to come (after the device and the utility rooms):**
- wiring `jobWhere` into `whereIs`;
- the Works job board drawn on Deck 5 and the device's jobs section (`device.addSection('jobs', ...)`);
- the player's loop (inspect at the panel, carry two, hold to repair);
- visible faults from `shipEffects` notes (cheap flickers and emissive changes, no new draw calls);
- carts as props on the crowd.

- **The galley rota (0.26.2).** Agreed with the galley engineer: `galleyDay(person, day)` → null | { role, wake, bedBy, blocks: [{ from, to, placeId, task }], meals: { breakfast: [from, to], dinner: [from, to] } }, in minutes from midnight (section 16). `galleyPlan(p, d)` reads it for day-shift galley staff, or falls back to the older `galleyShift`.
    - **Days off:** role null is a day off. The rota decides galley staff's days off (`schedFrame`'s `works`), not `rota.offDays`.
    - **Wake and bedtime:** `wake` is used as given (else 35 minutes before an early first block). `bedBy` caps that night's bedtime, and tomorrow's early start pulls tonight's bedtime earlier too.
    - **Stints:** each block is its own work segment at its station (`workAt`), with the task as the tag and anim `cook` unless the place has one. Someone early waits for their stint, since the station may still be in use.
    - **Meals:** a meal the rota gives is a normal meal (queue, then a seat) at those times, even outside the window. A meal it doesn't give is the normal one, in its window, around the work. A late hand has a normal morning.
    - **Between stints:** free time, ending a lift ride before the next stint.
    - **Desks and stand-ins:** under the rota, galley staff have no desk and galley stations aren't offered to stand-ins (`galleyRotaOn()`).
    - **Queue hook:** `galleyHold(person, t)` (section 16) can hold a crew member at the servery queue while it's empty. It's read in `CrewAgent.update` after the goal is worked out; undefined means no change.
- **Habits for the rooms (0.26.2).** The crew designer's six (0.16.4), as `specials`:
    - runner: before dinner, 25–40 minutes on a treadmill (`gym-run`: gym places with `anim: 'run'`), else any gym place; tag `run`.
    - shopper: shopping on 35% of work days and 80% of days off.
    - musician: 55% of evenings, the music room (tag `play`); the Captain every Thursday.
    - gardener: 60%, the garden's standing places (`garden-tend`); tag `tend`.
    - bar-regular: 75%, after dinner, the bar (`bar`: `lounge-seat` with `anim: 'drink'`), else the lounge; tag `bar`.
    - stargazer: 50%, Deck 2's observatory (`observatory`: seats in a room named Observatory); juniors too.
    These pseudo-types are extra lists in `schedData().shared` and don't change `roomsInUse`.
- **`placeBusy(id)` (0.26.2).** True while a crew member is settled at that place on the current deck, or the player is using it (`PLAYER_PLACE`, for section 15). A Set rebuilt once a frame in `updateDeckCrew`. The Deck 6 architect's treadmill and rower read it.
- **Work in blocks (0.26.3).** `shiftBlocks(p, d)` (was `galleyPlan`) gives a work day in blocks:
    - Galley staff: from `galleyDay`.
    - Anyone with `rota.hours` (`[[from, to], ...]` on their work days): those blocks at their own desk. These are the storekeepers, 08:00–14:30 and 14:30–21:00 at `work:store`.
    - `schedDesks` lets people with `rota.hours` share a desk when their hours don't overlap.
    - A first block before 07:00 is an early start: up 35 minutes before (or at `wake`), with a quick wash. A later one gets the normal morning.
    - A meal window covered by work, with no time given, is a staff meal at the desk or station.
- **Activities from the plan (0.26.3).** On the deck, the pose at a place comes from the segment's `anim`, not only the place's, so a stint's task shows: tea on Brennan's stool takes the stool's own pose (sit), and the dish pit is `wash`.
- **Service hatches (0.26.3).** `crewPath(s, g, rooms, who)` passes the CrewAgent to `findPath`, which asks `hatchPassable` (section 4, the Deck 6 architect) at hatches marked `service`. Works crew get through; nobody else does. `placeSpot` asks as a Works person when picking a `work:works` place's walk-to tile.
- **Waiting for food (0.26.3).** When `galleyHold` sends a crew member to a queue spot another crew member is already heading for, they stand where they are instead, so two people never share a spot. day_check names the place and the people when two share one.
- **The servery line and trays (0.26.30).** Replaces the above: crew queue in `LINE` (section 10) and carry a tray only from the counter to their mess-hall seat and on to the tray return. See the 0.26.30 row; day_check fails on a tray outside the mess hall or anyone standing still a ship minute with one.
- **Ship effects on needs (0.26.3).** `crewNeeds` reads `shipEffectsAt(seg.deck, seg.from)` (section 17, pure, typeof-guarded) for each piece of a plan. Each need's drain is multiplied by `drain[k]`, and each restore by `restore[k]` (a restore moves toward its target by that share, so a weak shower gives back 40%). The plans themselves don't read the machines: the machines' wear is read from the plans, so that would be a loop.
- **Machine panels.** Places with `machine` (B1) are never a desk or a stand-in station; they're for servicing (B2).
- **dayReport's station check** looks for two people at one work station at overlapping times, so the galley's shared, time-booked stations pass. Beds are still checked as one owner's.
## Player needs and the device (section 15, the player engineer, 0.28.x)
Pillar 2, workstream A3 (plans/pillar2-plan.md, "Needs", "The device", "Activities on places"). Section 15 sits right after section 14, like 13 and 14, and touches the player, the deck and the HUD only at run time. Nothing in it calls `R()`.
- **Warnings, the toilet and tips (0.28.41-0.28.42, the user's after 0.60.3; `plans/tips-plan.md`).** Bathroom 0: the `'+go'` pose (8b `HOLDS.go`, no prop) and 70% walking (`GO_SPEED`). A need under 25 / 10: one gentle / firm toast each (`NEED_WARN`, `needWarnTick`), re-armed at 40. 33 first-time teaching tips (`TEACH`, `teachTick`) through the device's tip card, one every 3 minutes at most. Both wait for quiet moments (`popupWatch`, `POP`). See the 0.28.41 and 0.28.42 rows.
- **Needs.** `NEED_KEYS = ['hunger', 'energy', 'hygiene', 'fitness', 'social']` (the plan's `NEEDS`; section 14 declares that name), in `SHIP.player.needs`, 0–100, 100 = fully met. The device labels hunger "Food" so a full bar reads as good.
    - **Drain per ship hour, awake / asleep (`NEED_DRAIN`):** hunger 4 / 2, energy 4.8 / 0 (3.75 before 0.28.9, 4.4 before 0.73.0), hygiene 2.5 / 1, fitness 0.6 / 0.3, social 1 / 0.25. Asleep, energy rises 12.5 an hour (`SLEEP_GAIN`), so 8 hours fills it from anywhere.
    - **Gains (`PLAYER_ACTS`), spread over the activity's ship minutes:** a meal at a mess seat +55 in 20 min; the grab shelf +25 in 3; a shower +80 in 10; a workout +30 in 30; a call home +40 in 15. While an activity runs, its need doesn't also drain. Browsing (8 min) and paying (2 min) restore nothing yet: shopping is a nice moment until items come.
    - **What a normal day does (player_check, on paper):** with a shower, breakfast, dinner, 8 hours' sleep and the gym and a call home on alternate evenings, the lowest each need reaches over a week is food 55, energy 40 (at bedtime), hygiene 52, fitness 62, social 39.5, and you never slow down. With nothing at all, fitness falls under 25 on the fourth day.
    - **Starting values:** a new game 60 / 100 / 60 / 70 / 70 (just woken, before breakfast); a `v: 1` save 80 each.
    - **Bathroom (0.28.19, the user's).** `bathroom` drains 12 an hour awake, 3 asleep; a toilet in any public washroom fills it (+100 in 3 minutes, sitting; `PLAYER_ACTS.toilet`). High: tasks × 1.05; low: tasks × 0.9. New game 60; a save without it 80. Cabins' toilets and wash pods are private (`PRIVATE_TYPES`).
- **Bonuses and penalties (0.28.9, the user's rule).** `needEffects(needs = SHIP.player.needs)` returns `{ walk, task, drain, socialGain, notes: [reason], marks: { need: ['up' | 'down', reason] } }`. A need at `NEED_HIGH` (75) or over gives its bonus, under `NEED_LOW` (25) its penalty, in between nothing; effects multiply. The table (`NEED_FX`), tuned from the lead's:

    | Need | High (75 and over) | Low (under 25) |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Energy | task × 1.15 ("Well rested") | walk × 0.8 ("Tired") |
    | Fitness | walk × 1.05, energy drain × 0.94 ("Fit") | walk × 0.8, energy drain × 1.06 ("Out of shape") |
    | Food | energy drain × 0.96 ("Well fed") | task × 0.85 ("Hungry") |
    | Hygiene | socialGain × 1.5 ("Fresh and clean") | socialGain × 0.5 ("You could do with a wash") |
    | Social | task × 1.1 ("In good spirits") | energy drain × 1.05 ("Lonely") |

    The lead's table had fitness 0.85 / 1.15, food 0.9 and lonely 1.1 on the drain: tuned down so the user's 16 to 18 hours hold (with 4.4 an hour: 16.1 unfit, 17.1 average, 18.2 fit). **task** is how fast tasks go: activity minutes (`A.done` counts `dm × task`, and the gain goes with it), repairs and making parts all run faster or slower, and the toasts' "about N minutes" divide by it (`taskMins`). Activities that end on a condition (the queue) ignore it. **drain** multiplies energy's awake drain only; `needsDrain` steps in half-hours so a long skip feels a bonus end. **walk** is `playerSpeed()`. **socialGain** multiplies social from chatting and company (0.28.10). The device marks a need ▲ (teal) or ▼ (red) beside its name, with each reason as a line under the bars, and says "Walking at N% speed" whenever it isn't 100.
- **Seeing it work (0.28.20, 0.28.21).** A need that rises floats a "+N Need" note over your character and pulses the Device button (`needsSeen`, `needFloat`, `needPulse(k)`); see the build rows.
- **Mail (0.28.10).** `mailSend({ at, from, subject, body, quiet })` → id. `at` is absolute ship minutes (now if left out); `quiet` skips the toast. `SHIP.mail = [{ id, at, from, subject, body, read }]`, newest first, at most `MAIL_MAX` (60), saved as `save.mail` through `SAVE_EXTRA` and read back defensively (`mailLoad`). New mail puts a count on the Device button and a toast, one per batch (several sends in one moment, such as a sleep's catch-up, make one toast "N new messages"; none while asleep). `mailUnread()`. A fresh start (no save) and New game send the welcome (`mailWelcome`, quiet so the first toast stays). Safe to call at any time after section 15 has run; sends before it are kept.
- **Device tabs (0.28.10).** `device.addSection(id, title, render, tab = 'status')`; tabs Status and Mail along the top (`device.show(tab)`), the Mail tab showing its unread count. Status: now, wallet, needs, then section 17's jobs. Mail: the inbox; tap a message to open it and mark it read.
- **Company and chatting (0.28.10).** `playerChat(person)` (section 10's crew tap): `CHAT_GAIN` 6 social for the first chat with someone each ship day, `CHAT_AGAIN` 1 after, times `needEffects().socialGain`; adds "You chat for a minute." to the tap's toast (and, unwashed, a wrinkled nose). `SHIP.player.chats` (person → day, today's and yesterday's) is saved. Eating: each ship minute at a mess seat, `COMPANY_GAIN` 0.2 social for each crew member settled at a mess seat within `COMPANY_NEAR` (3 m), up to `COMPANY_MAX` (3), times socialGain; the meal's end toast says "Good company, too."
- **The store and credits (0.28.10).** `STORE` lists snacks, drinks and comforts with price and gain; browsing a shelf picks the next one of its kind (`storePick`, by locker size, purchases and day: no `R()`), `basket = { kind, name, price, gain }`. Paying takes the credits; snacks and drinks raise food at once, comforts go in `SHIP.player.locker` (40 at most) and raise social. Not enough credits: a toast, no sale. `SHIP.player.credits` starts at `CREDITS_FRESH` (40); each finished job pays `JOB_PAY` (15). Saved with the locker.
- **Machines (0.28.11).** `machineTap(it, at)`: after the cart run, the job loop and the activities have passed on a tap, the nearest non-stub machine on this deck within `MACHINE_NEAR` (1.2 m) of the thing tapped; you walk up to it (`approach`) and the toast is `machineUse(id, 'player').text` (section 17, the systems engineer's; it may also start the machine). Without `machineUse`, or no machine there, the tap does what it always did. The machines' `walk` effect is still never applied to you.
- **The cart run (0.28.11).** A restock job (section 17's kind `'restock'`, cargo `'stores'` or `'meals'`) you hold is done by hand, with the crew's step times (`jobRun`): `cartTake` at a Cart dock (2 min; not a dock whose cart is out, `cartsOut`), `cartLoad` in the cargo hold (10 min, at a free `5-hold-N`), `cartUnload` in main storage (20 min, `5-parts-N`) or the cold store (15 min, `3-cold-N`), `cartBack` at a dock (2 min) → `finishJob` (which moves `SHIP.hold` onto the shelves for stores) and `JOB_PAY`. `SHIP.player.cart = { job, stage: 'empty' | 'full' | 'done', dock }`, saved. Loading and unloading start from a tap in the room or on their own when you stand still there with the cart (`cartAuto`, each ship minute): the cargo hold has nothing tappable. `touchJob` at each step. A dropped job: tap a dock to put the cart back. You can't take a cart with parts in your hands. Meals: unloading calls section 16's `galleyCartLand(hour)` (the hour from the job id `restock-meals@<hour>`), which lands that run's meals in the cold store; it's safe if section 17 calls it too (a second call lands nothing). Known gap: the dock's parked cart doesn't hide while you have it (`cartsOut` reads crew runs' segments; the systems engineer could add the player's).
- **Sit anywhere (0.28.12).** `sitTap(it, at, point)`, after the job taps and 15b's `PLAYER_TAPS` and before `machineTap`, when no `ACT_TAP` rule matched: the nearest place within `SIT_NEAR` (1.3 m) of the tap point with a type in `SIT_TYPES` and pose `sit`, not `privatePlace`, not `heldBy` crew (else "X is sitting there"). Activity `sit` (`until: () => false`: it lasts until you walk away); the stand-in takes the spot's pose and height. While sitting: energy's drain × `SIT_REST` (0.6, through a copy of the ship's fx), and `SIT_GAIN` (0.1) social a minute for each crew member settled in a sit pose within `COMPANY_NEAR`, up to `COMPANY_MAX`, times socialGain (`sitCompany`). Chosen interaction: one tap sits you and shows the description in the same toast; no second tap.
- **Squeezing past (0.28.12).** Settled crew hold their walk-to tile (section 10's `occupiedBy`), which could close the only way through. Section 10's `goTo` now asks, for the player only and only when there's no path: `squeezePath(walker, goal, avoid)` retries with those crew tiles allowed (walking crew and the goal tile still never). `squeezeStep()` each frame slows you to `SQUEEZE_SPEED` (0.5) on or just before such a tile, if that crew member is still there, and toasts '"Excuse me, Chef." Brennan shifts aside to let you by.' once per person per walk.
- **Shuffling aside (0.28.70).** The user's note on 0.60.5: "walking into people shuffles them aside". `shuffleTick(dt)` (from `playerUpdate`, after `squeezeStep`): while you're walking (press-and-hold or a path, `player.vel` at least 0.15), any crew member `shuffleable` (not settled at a place, not sitting or lying, not your escort, not stopped talking to you, not you) within `SHUFFLE_NEAR` (0.45) and ahead of you takes a step at `SHUFFLE_V` (2 tiles a second) across your way: `SHUFFLE_STEP` (0.5 tiles) to the side they're already on, else the other side; in a one-tile corridor, where half a tile would put them in the wall, `SHUFFLE_SHORT` (0.25) to either side; else 0.5 straight on (a step back). A way counts only if `shuffleClear` passes from them to the end of the step, and from there to their next path tile, every 0.2 tiles: open tiles, no wall edge between (`edgeOpen`; a diagonal must be open round one corner, `shuffleStep`), and `collide` doesn't move a body there. No way: they don't move and you pass anyway. The step is added to their `x`/`z` (`SHUFFLE`, crew agent -> what's left of it); their own update walks them on from there, so they carry on with their plan. Section 10's press-and-hold push (`Walker.update`, "nor through crew on their feet") skips `shuffleable` crew for the player, so they never hold you up. Once their step ends, nobody is stepped again for `SHUFFLE_COOL` (1.5 s, `SHUFFLE_DONE`), so following someone close doesn't make them wobble. The polite word is a 2.5 s speech bubble over them (section 8b's `bubbleSay`, a line from `SHUFFLE_WORDS` by `idHash`), never a toast, so it never stamps `TOAST_AT` or holds up the need warnings; at most once a person per `SHUFFLE_SAY` (60 s of `performance.now()`, `SHUFFLE_SAID`), and never over a bubble they already have. No `R()`.
- **Crawlways (0.28.11).** `crawlTiles()` reads `deckDef.crawl` (`['i,j', ...]`, cached per deck). On one: `player.maxV × CRAWL_SPEED` (0.5) on top of the needs' walk, `player.crouch = true` (a one-time toast), and Walker.update poses you `'crouch'` (+ what's in hand). No deck has a crawl list yet; player_check tests with a stand-in.
- **Going to bed (0.28.9; the alarm clock from 0.73.0).** Tapping your own bunk from 20:00 to 05:30 opens the alarm clock (section 13's `#sleepAsk`: pick the wake time, the energy you'll wake with, a warning at or after your shift's start; `SHIP.player.alarm`, saved), then fades out and wakes you then (06:00 by default; see the 0.73.0 row); before 20:00, "Too early to turn in". Asleep, energy rises 12.5 an hour, so 20:00 to 06:00 fills it from anywhere.
- **Walking speed (the user's rule).** `playerSpeed()` is 1, times `SLOW_SPEED` (0.8) if fitness is under `SLOW_BELOW` (25), times 0.8 again if energy is under 25: so one low need walks at 80%, both at 64%. `playerUpdate` sets `player.maxV = PLAYER_V0 * playerSpeed()` every frame (`PLAYER_V0` is the maxV the Walker was built with), so at full speed it's exactly the old number. The first time either drops under 25 a toast says why. Nothing else is ever punished.
- **Time.** `playerUpdate` drains by the ship minutes since the last frame, so a hidden tab (no ship time) drains nothing. A time skip (`SIM_ADVANCE`) drains the whole gap at once: asleep if you chose Sleep in your bunk (`sleeping`, section 13) or for the debug Day button (any skip starting at night), otherwise awake (the debug Night button). Any activity stops at a skip. 20 hours awake leaves energy at exactly 25; 21 hours slows you.
- **Activities (`playerTap`, `actStart`, `actUpdate`).** The item tap in section 11 asks `playerTap(item, itemPos, tapPoint)` first. `ACT_TAP` picks the activity from the item's title (treadmills, rowing machine, weights, mats → gym; booths 1–4 → call; shelves, drinks fridge, window display → browse; counter → pay; servery → a tray; grab and go → grab; a table → eat; shower → shower), then the places of its types within `ACT_NEAR` (2.4 m) of the item on this deck, nearest the tap first. No such place nearby (the lounge's booths and high tables, Deck 5's counter, someone's wash pod) and the tap does what it always did.
    - You walk to the place's walk-to tile (`placeSpot`, section 10), then a stand-in body slides onto the spot and takes the pose, and time runs. The stand-in is a `FullPerson` (section 10) built from `PLAYER_SPEC`, posed by the crowd's own code, while your Walker waits hidden on the walk-to tile; the camera, doors and crew paths see you there. It's disposed when you're done, so it costs the same draw calls as you.
    - **Poses:** treadmill `run` (a gym place's `anim`, or the treadmill's title until the treadmill places carry `anim: 'run'`), weights `lift`, the rower `sit` and the mats and bench `lie` (their place's pose), a booth `call`, shelves `browse`, the counter `pay`, a mess seat `sit` (the crowd's eating), a shower `stand`. Until section 8b has the activity poses they fall back to stand.
    - **Held places:** a place is held if a crew agent on the deck is at it or on the way (`goalId`). The nearest free place of the kind is used; if they're all held, a toast says who's on it. Checked again when you arrive: if someone got there first, you move once to the next free place of its kind nearby, else a toast says so. `playerHolds()` gives the place you're using, for the schedules to keep crew off it (not wired yet).
    - **Eating:** the servery: you take a tray at a free queue spot (1 min), then walk to the nearest free mess seat and eat; a mess table: straight to its nearest free seat. Food comes from `foodFrom(where, peek)`, which asks the galley's `galleyServe(where, peek)` (section 16, to come) and until then serves breakfast 06:00–08:00 and dinner 17:00–20:30, and the grab shelf any time. Between meals the servery says it's closed.
    - **Shopping:** browse at a free shop place, then the counter (the `work:store` place must be within reach) to pay; a clerk at the counter rings it up. With nothing picked out, the counter just shows its text.
    - **Showers:** the public ones (Deck 4's port and starboard washrooms, Deck 5's Works washroom). A shower in a room with a bed, or named after a bed (`4-05-wash`), is someone's own and isn't offered.
    - **Stopping:** walking away (a tap elsewhere, steering) stops at once, keeping what you'd gained; a skip or a deck change too.
- **The device (a placeholder).** A small Device button under the clock (an amber dot when a need is under 25) opens `#device`, a plain panel in the lift panel's style. `device.addSection(id, title, render)` adds or replaces a section; `render(el)` fills a fresh `el` each time the panel is drawn (on opening and every ship minute while it's open). Built in: `now` (the day and time, then what you're doing or the status line from `needsLine()`) and `needs` (five bars, amber under 50, red under 25, and "Walking at 80% speed" when slowed). B2's jobs section plugs in the same way. The user is designing the real device; replace the panel, keep the data and `addSection`.
- **Other sections (0.28.2), each looked up at run time, so nothing breaks while they're missing:**
    - `shipEffects(deck)` (17): `restore[k]` multiplies activity gains and sleep's energy, `drain[k]` multiplies drains, read once a ship minute for the deck you're on (`playerFx`). Time skips use the pure `shipEffectsAt(deck, from)` instead, so a sleep never runs the machines. `walk` is ignored: only fitness and energy slow the player. The first two `notes` show under the status line on the device.
    - The galley (16): `foodFrom` asks `galleyServe(where, peek)` if it exists, else `takePortion('player')` (a hot portion, else the grab shelf, else nothing) with `galleyText('servery' | 'grab')` for what's on and the closed text; else the meal windows.
    - Poses (8b): `poseOf(place)` when it exists (booths `sit:call`, the rower `sit:row`, the bench `lie:lift`), except a treadmill place without `anim`, which is `run` by its title. The player's stand-in is a `FullPerson`, so it's posed by the crowd's own code, props and all.
    - **The queue (0.28.3):** with `galleyJoin(who)` and `galleyTurn(who)` (16), a servery tap is the `queue` activity: walk to `j.at` / `j.xy` (the back of the line), and from arriving (`galleyJoin` is asked again then, so the wait starts when you're in line) stand until `galleyTurn('player')`; then `takePortion` serves you (and clears the join), and you go to the nearest free mess seat to eat. Walking away leaves the line. The device shows "In the queue for the servery: about N min." An activity can end on a condition (`until(A)` in `PLAYER_ACTS`) instead of its minutes.
- **Your job (B2, 0.28.6).** The player's side of section 17's job list (`jobTap`, `jobBegin`, `jobEnd`, after `playerNew`). Section 17 has the board and the device's jobs section (take it, drop it), `myJob`, `takePart`, `finishJob`, `panelPlace`; this has the walking, carrying and doing. Everything checks `jobsOn()` (those four functions there), so without section 17 nothing changes.
    - **Fetch:** a tap on anything in main storage (Deck 5, room `r`, not the tarp) while you hold a job that still needs parts: you walk to the nearest free storage place (`5-parts-1`..`4`, the crew's own, so `heldBy` keeps you apart; in front of the shelf if they're all held) and look (`fetch`, 4 minutes), then `takePart` each part still needed, up to `CARRY_MAX` (2) in hand. None left: it says so (the next delivery is on Wednesday). Hands full, or nothing needed: a toast, no walk. With no job and parts in hand, a tap there puts them back (`stow`), once section 17 has a `returnPart(part)`.
    - **Make (when storage is out):** a part your job still needs with `SHIP.stores[part]` at 0, and not in section 17's `NO_MAKE`: a tap on anything in the machine shop (Deck 5, room `f`) walks you to the nearest free `5-make-1`/`2` and makes it (`make`, 60 minutes, the crew's `JOB_MAKE`). Walking away pauses it (`work.make = { part, done }`). The part goes into your hands; section 17's stores aren't touched. A storage trip that finds none says whether the machine shop can make it.
    - **The notice:** each ship minute `jobNotice` looks at `jobsBoard()` for open jobs you could take (your tier, not the engine crew's). When there's a new one and you're free (no job, no activity), a toast: "Your device: New job: …, …. Take it on your device." Jobs posted during a time skip are taken as seen.
    - **At the machine:** a tap on anything within reach of the panel place of your job's machine (on its deck) walks you to the panel place (`job`, pose from `poseOf`, else `work`). On arrival (`jobBegin`) you fit what you carry that the job needs; then, if every part is in, you repair for `jobMins(job)` less the time already done; if not, the first visit is a look over (3 minutes, then it says what it needs), and later visits just say what's missing. Walking away pauses: fitted parts and repair minutes stay in `SHIP.player.work` (`{ job, fitted, done, seen }`). When the repair's done, `finishJob(id)` closes the job and puts the machine right.
    - **The device:** the 'now' section adds "Carrying: …" and your job's progress (parts fitted, repair minutes done). The job list itself is section 17's 'jobs' section.
    - A job you drop, or one that goes back on the board after 12 hours without progress (`jobTouch` calls section 17's `touchJob` on each part fitted and each minute of repairing or making), clears `work`; parts you carry stay in hand, and go back on a shelf with a tap in main storage (`returnPart`, 0.33.12).
- **Saving.** `SAVE_V = 2`: `player.needs` is saved with the rest, through `SAVE_EXTRA`; from 0.28.6 also `player.carry` and `player.work`, read back defensively (strings only, two at most). `loadGame` accepts `v: 1` and `v: 2`; `needsLoad` clamps each saved need to 0–100 and fills anything missing or junk with 80. New game resets the needs (`playerNew`). `?nosave` still neither reads nor writes.
- **Hooks outside section 15 (all small):** section 10 (0.28.8): `Walker.update` calls `animateActivity(this, holding ? 'stand+carry' : 'stand', …)` once `player.holding` has been set, else `animateRig` as before; section 13: `SAVE_V = 2`, `SAVE_EXTRA` (the systems engineer's lines), `loadGame` takes `v: 1`, `playerNew()` in New game; section 11: one line at the top of the item tap; section 12: `playerUpdate(dt, t)` after the walkers; the header's file map; the device button, panel and CSS in the HTML.
- **player_check.py (0.28.1).** `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 player_check.py BUILD.html` (about 4 minutes): a week of normal days and a week of neglect on paper; the speed rule (the factor, the top speed and the speed actually reached); sleep and awake skips; one player day on the real decks by tapping the real things (shower at 10:30 on Deck 4; grab shelf, treadmill, weights, booth, shelves, counter mid-morning on Deck 3; the servery and a mess seat at dinner), each need rising by its gain, each pose taken, you back on your feet; a private wash pod not offered; booths held by crew; the servery closed at 12:00; walking away mid-workout; draw calls under 450; saving (v 2 kept, v 1 at 80, junk clamped, `?nosave`).

### The work steps (Pillar 5, seat P, from 0.28.71; `plans/mechanics/foundation.html` section 15)

What section 17's `jobKindAdd` kinds and section 24's mechanics give a stop, and what comes back. One runner, `workStep(job, stop, k)` (section 15), started where a stop starts today (15b's `ixChoreTap` for chores and registered kinds, 15's `jobBegin` at a machine's panel).

- **The step's name travels as `how`** on each entry of `jobStops(j)` (`'hold'` when missing: today's behaviour, untouched). `step` on those entries is already the stop's tag (`'fetch'`, `'lamp'`, `'pickup'`, `'job'`…), so it can't carry it. On a kind's own stop list (`def.stops(j)`, or `j.stops` entries) the page calls the field `step`; section 17's `jobStopsOf` copies it to `how` (and `opts` as is). `opts` may hold functions: it's made fresh by `def.stops(j)` each time, never saved on the job.
- **`hold`**: stand and work `mins` (exists). On a machine kind, the minutes are times `careerSpeed(kind)`.
- **`look`**: `opts = { secs: 4, marks: [{ id, label }, ...] | none, text }`. Stand by it `secs` real seconds (the progress bar), then, if `marks`, a small card to mark it ("Fine", "Worn", "Something's off"). Result `{ pick: id }` (none without marks). Pose `look` (falls back to `stand`).
- **`choose`**: `opts = { title, text, options: [{ id, label }, ...] }` (2-6). A choice card in the device's style (`deviceChoose`; buttons 44 px+). Result `{ pick: id }`. "Not now" leaves the stop undone.
- **`turn`**: `opts = { label, v: start, min, max, by: 1, onTurn(v) -> text | null, ok(v) -> true when right, gauge(v) -> text | null }`. A two-button card (− / +) with the setting's name and, only if the machine has one, its own gauge's words (`gauge`). Each press moves `v` by `by` (within min/max) and calls `opts.onTurn(v)` (its text shows on the card). When `opts.ok(v)`: `jobStepDone(job.id, k, { value: v })`. Pose `tune` (falls back to `work`). The value so far is kept if you walk away.
- **`wait`**: `opts = { mins | until, label }`. The first tap starts it (a toast: "ready at 14:20"); `until` is a ship minute (`shipNow()` scale), else start + `mins`. A tap after that: `jobStepDone(job.id, k, {})`. `workWaitAt(jobId)` gives the minute it's ready (for the job card's "ready at"), or null.
- **`carry`**: exists (two slots, the cart); not run by `workStep`.
- **Saved:** the steps under way are `SHIP.player.wsteps = [{ job, k, how, v, at, done }]` (save `player.wsteps`; one per stop, at most 8, so a wait runs on while you do the job's next stop; cleaned on load by `wstepsLoad`; a job that isn't yours any more isn't saved; New game empties it). Walking away pauses a step: a look keeps its seconds (`done`, ship minutes), a turn its value (`v`), a wait its start (`at`); a choice card just closes, the stop undone.
- **Hooks it uses:** 15b's `ixChoreTap` calls `workStep(j, st, k, spot)` just before a hold stop starts (no `careerSpeed` there: those jobs have no machine; a machine job's minutes get it through `jobMins`, reviewer R's LOW on 0.28.72, removed in 0.28.73); 15b's `ixChore` also takes a kind in `JOB_KINDS` (section 17's `jobKindAdd` registry) with no machine; 15's `jobBegin` hands the panel's `'job'` stop to `workStep` when its `how` isn't hold (after fitting the parts); `actEnd` calls `workStepEnd(A, abort)`. A look counts real seconds (`def.real`: your needs don't speed it). Poses `look` and `tune` are used once 8b has them (`WS_POSE`), else `stand` and `work`.
- **For section 17:** `jobStepDone(id, k, res)` gets `{ pick }`, `{ value }` or `{}`; the job card can show `workWaitAt(jobId)` ("ready at " + `hhmm`).


### The whole-save budget (the player engineer owns it, from 2026-10-17)

**250 KB for the whole save (`localStorage[SAVE_KEY]`) at any point to day 280.** Checked by `python3 player_check.py BUILD.html --long [DAYS]` (about two minutes; skipped on a branch without section 13's `newGameStart`/`skipTo`): it starts the real arrival (`newGameStart(true)`), skips ten hours at a time to each day's 06:00, saves, and keeps the largest size; every 10 days it prints the size and the biggest parts (top-level keys, `ship.*` and `player.*` split out), and fails above 250 KB. First run, on combined 0.60.2 (142b503), to day 280: **largest 149.9 KB on day 257** (social 53.4, ship.jobs 49.5, mail 21.2, notes 8.5, ship.events 7.0, habitLog 3.8, ship.machines 2.1, crew 1.7, galley 0.5); 70.7 KB on day 10, 107.6 on day 140, 124.6 on day 280. `ship.jobs` swings with the job board (25 to 50 KB); `social` is the one that keeps growing (17 KB on day 10 to 53 on day 280, about 0.13 KB a day), then `habitLog` and `ship.events`. A section adding to the save should check `--long` before and after.

## Player interactions (section 15b, the interactions engineer, 0.37.x)

Section `// 15b. PLAYER INTERACTIONS` sits right after section 15. It owns more things to use, the job board's list and the goal pointer. It reuses section 15's activity loop, so every rule there (the minutes, the gains, walking away to stop, `needEffects` and the ship's effects) applies to these too.

- **The hook.** Section 15 declares `PLAYER_TAPS = []` above `playerTap`, and `playerTap` tries each `f(item, itemPos, tapPoint)` right after `jobTap`. Agreed with the player engineer; the line is the same on both branches. 15b pushes `ixTap`.
- **Parity (what a place is for).** `ixKind(place, item)` maps the crew's place types to activities, registered into section 15's `PLAYER_ACTS` (`IX_ACTS`, kinds named `ix…`):
  - the bar and Deck 2's tea table (`lounge-seat` with `anim: 'drink'`): `ixDrink`, food +10 and a little social, 20 min, `sit:drink`;
  - the kettle, the hot water, the Captain's tea (standing places in lounges and common rooms): `ixTea`, food +5, 6 min;
  - reading seats (`anim: 'read'`): `ixRead`, energy +5, a little social, 20 min;
  - other lounge and common-room seats (talk, plain): `ixLounge`, social +4, energy +4, 20 min; `sit:talk` in company, `sit:read` alone;
  - benches, garden seats and the music room's beanbags: not 15b's since 0.37.2; they're section 15's sit-anywhere (`sitTap`);
  - observatories (rooms named "Observ…": Deck 2's observatory, Deck 1's observation room) and the telescope: `ixStars`, energy +6, 15 min;
  - the garden's standing places: `ixGarden`, social +6, a little fitness, `water` pose;
  - the music room's piano, guitars and record player: `ixMusic`, social +8, `play` / `sit:play`;
  - the laundry: `ixFold`, hygiene +12, 15 min, `fold`;
  - basins (public washrooms): `ixBasin`, hygiene +6, 2 min;
  - the gym's water cooler: `ixWater`; any other gym place tapped by a name section 15 doesn't know uses section 15's own `gym`.
- **Company.** Crew settled within `IX_COMPANY` (3.5 m) or in the same room add `with` more social (scaled by `needEffects(needs).socialGain` once section 15 has it). Side gains (`also`) come in from 15b's own frame loop (`ixSide`, from 0.37.1), following `A.done`, so section 15's task speed (`needEffects().task`) speeds them up or slows them down like the main gain. (0.37.0 used the activity's `until`, which section 15 runs at plain speed.)
- **What it leaves alone.** A tap section 15's `ACT_TAP` would take (treadmill, shower, mess table…), a real machine within section 15's `MACHINE_NEAR` (its `machineTap` runs it through section 17's `machineUse`), washers, places in another room than the thing tapped, private places (`privatePlace`: a room with someone else's bed), beds and bunks, notices, rotas, boards, screens, charts (`IX_SKIP`; garden beds are fine). Places crew hold (`heldBy`, `placeBusy`) say "In use".
- **Reach.** A place counts if its spot is within `IX_NEAR` (2.4 m) of the tapped thing's outline (its hit box), and the nearest to your tap point wins. A few seats have no tappable thing near them (two Deck 2 benches by the sample bench, one bow-lounge booth seat, one sofa end, one nutrient-tank garden place, one infirmary waiting bench); they're reached by tapping their neighbours.
- **The job board.** Tapping anything titled "Job board" (the Works bay board on Deck 5, and any later ones) walks you to it and opens a panel (`ixBoard`, made in 15b's DOM): your job first with Drop it, then section 17's `openJobs('you')` (from 0.37.2; before it, the device's rule), most urgent first, with Take it for the jobs section 15 can walk you through (machine jobs and chores on a machine, cart runs); chores without a machine (lamps, vents, deliveries, rounds) say "not yet" until someone builds their walk. Then the rest of the board, each with why it isn't yours (tier, crew only). Take it calls section 17's `claimJob`; the panel closes and the pointer appears.
- **Chores and turning in (0.37.3).** Chores without a machine (section 17's `CHORES`: lamp, vent, delivery, inspection round) are walked in 15b (`ixChoreTap`, before the place taps): the next stop is the first of `jobStops(myJob())` without `done`. A `fetch` stop is a tap in main storage (Deck 5, room `r`): 4 minutes, then `takePart` for what's needed and `jobStepDone`. Any other stop is a tap on something within reach of the stop's place (or in its room) on its deck: you stand at the place's spot (not the place itself, so a lamp over a busy lift queue is still yours) for the stop's minutes (`ixStop`, pose by tag), then `jobStepDone`. Walking away pauses; a stop only counts once its minutes are in. After the last stop section 17 does the work and sets `workDone`; the parts are used. The board's row for your job then says "done: turn it in" with Turn it in (`turnInJob`); chores are paid `JOB_PAY` there. With section 17's `TURN_IN` on, machine jobs and cart runs land in `workDone` too when section 15 finishes them, and complete at the board the same way (section 15 still pays them when it finishes them). Chores on a machine (calibrate, faults, filters) go through section 15's repair loop.
- **The marker and the progress bar (0.37.8).** `ixFrame` hides `#ixGoal` while `actProgress()` (section 15) returns something, i.e. while the progress bar is over you, so the two don't stack over your head.
- **The ship health panel (0.37.6).** A tap on anything titled "Ship health" walks you to it and opens `ixHealth` (`.ixpanel`, the job board's family): the first row of `shipHealthRows()` (the ship) then each system worst first, as a bar, number and word (`IX_STATE`: Good, Worn, Poor, Failing), then each system's worst machine when it isn't fine, then the jobs on the board. Redrawn each ship minute while open. Both panels close with Close, Escape, or a tap outside (a capture `pointerdown` that goes no further, so it doesn't walk you).
- **What you carry (0.37.5).** `ixHold()` returns `'carry'` (the crate) for a delivery chore between its pickup and drop stops, `'toolbox'` for any other job you hold whose work isn't done, else null. Section 15's `playerUpdate` asks it last when choosing `player.holding` (`... ? 'tray' : typeof ixHold === 'function' && ixHold() || false`), so a cart, parts in hand or a tray come first. The props are 8b's (the performance engineer's).
- **Tracking (0.37.4).** `SHIP.player.track`: `null` means the device's Jobs app has Track switched off, so `ixFrame` shows no job step (only an explicit `setGoal`, e.g. Maps' Go there); a job id or `undefined` means on. Taking a job at the board sets it to the job's id. Saved through `SAVE_EXTRA` as `player.track` and read back defensively. The device calls `clearGoal()` when Track is switched on, so the job step shows again.
- **First tips (Pillar 3, C13).** 15b says when; the device draws them through `tipShow(id, text)` (shown once each, saved there). Ids: `board` (within 3.5 m of a job board), `taken` (a job taken at the board), `machine` (within 2.5 m of your job's machine panel), `turnin` (work done). All inert until `tipShow` exists.
- **The goal pointer.** `setGoal({ deck, at: [i, j] | place: id | room: key, name or RegExp, label })` and `clearGoal()`, for any section (the Pillar 3 map will reuse it). With no goal set, your job's next step (`ixJobStep`): main storage for parts, the machine shop for a part storage is out of, the machine's panel to look it over or repair it; a cart job points at the cargo hold. Drawn in DOM (`#ixGoal`), no draw calls, hidden with no goal: a bobbing marker over the place when it's on screen, a round arrow at the edge of a frame clear of the HUD (top 130 px) and the toast (bottom 110 px) when it's off; on another deck it points at the lift and says "Take the lift to Deck N: …". It runs on its own `requestAnimationFrame`, a frame behind the camera (not visible).
- **The Tracked button and panel (0.72.0, HUD U, the playtest update; since 0.72.4 also a device app, `deviceApp('tracked')`, first in the list).** `#trkBtn` and `#trkPanel` (a `.lift` dialog), code right after `requestAnimationFrame(ixFrame)`. `trkItems()` lists your job (`myJob()`, sub = `ixJobStep()`'s label), then devDay's items for the rest of today and tomorrow until noon (not 'off'), then `TRK_PLACES` (job board, your bunk via `playerBed()`, the call booths `3-callhome-1`). A tap: a place or item gets `SHIP.player.track = null; setGoal({ ...goal, trk: key })` (the `trk` key is how the panel knows which row is on; setGoal's spread must keep extra fields); your job gets `SHIP.player.track = id; clearGoal()`; a tap on the row that's on stops it. Goals: `tradGoal(x)` first for a tradition when the traditions seat defines it (tap-to-join's leader), else the Schedule's `devItemGoal(x)`; items with no goal show disabled ("no arrow for this one"). Placement (`trkPlace`, every 400 ms and on resize): on phones (pausePlace's media query) right-aligned under the needs strip, clear of the activity bar on its left; on wide screens after the Device button (or Pause beside it), wordless if it would touch the round buttons. `.on` (gold dot) while `goalNow()`. Nothing saved. The device app tiles' labels are 13px/.03em (were 15px/.05em) so none wrap. interactions_check's "== tracked" part (320 x 640) covers it.
- **interactions_check.py (0.37.0).** `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 interactions_check.py BUILD.html` (about 6 minutes): fifteen things tapped on Decks 1, 3, 4 and 5, each starting an activity with a pose and raising its need; a bar with every stool held says "In use"; the treadmill stays section 15's; a notice reads as before; a seat in someone's cabin is left alone; walking away stops it; the board opens, lists, takes and drops a job; the pointer's job steps (storage, the lift, the machine), `setGoal` / `clearGoal`, the arrow off screen and the marker on; draw calls under 450; no console errors.

## The arrival (section 21, the onboarding engineer, 0.42.x)

Pillar 4, Part 1 (plans/pillar4-plan.md). Section 21 sits right after section 20 and, like it, waits for first use. **Wave 1a (0.42.0) is on paper only: nothing calls into it yet, and test URLs never start the intro.**

- **The state:** `SHIP.intro = { on, step, since, day, done: { stepId: shipMin }, missed: [stepId], settle: { itemId: shipMin }, skipped, ended, escort, flags }` (`since`: when the current step became yours), saved as `save.intro` (`introSave`/`introLoad`). Day 0 is the arrival Sunday, so its minutes are below 0 (`introAbs(day, min)`). `flags` holds the week's flags only until section 13's flag store exists.
- **The evening:** `INTRO_STEPS`, one entry per row of the plan's A0-A13 table: `{ id, day, at, who, mode: 'lead'|'wait'|'meet'|'free', where, meeting, checkpoint: { deck, tile, room }, rel: { id: pts }, flags, fallback: { wait | until, mail, flags, later } }`. `introAdvance(t, here)` moves it on: a step is done when it's due and you're in its checkpoint's room, at most `INTRO_REACH` (2) tiles away; otherwise, after the fallback's wait (counted from when the step became yours; only 5 minutes while you're missing steps, so the evening still ends by 20:00), its Mail goes out and it moves on to `later`. Two steps in a row never have checkpoints within reach of each other (intro_check). `INTRO_FLAGS` lists every flag the intro sets: `introBegin` clears them (section 13's store outlives a game), and `introEnd`, however the intro ends, leaves the device, Calder met and every week's gate set. The points add up to the social plan's end state (Torres 10, Tomas 10, Pell 5, Calder 3, Brennan 3). Checkpoint tiles are provisional until wave 2 walks them.
- **The week:** `INTRO_WEEK`, beats by the clock: `{ id, day, at, until, who, also, where: { deck, room } | 'seek', settle, meeting, job, appt, escort: 'lead'|'follow', flags, mail, invite, end }`. `week1.d2` ... `week1.d7` at 06:00 on their days, `week1.done` at Day 8 00:00; Tomas's `socialInvite({ trad: 'sowing', day: 6, from: 'tomas' })` on Friday at 18:00. Missing the bunk or dinner on Sunday: the Monday briefing hands you the device and you meet Calder there.
- **Settling in:** `SETTLE = [{ id, title, teacher, place, done(kind, data) }]`, 15 items; `settleNote(kind, data)` ticks them from the ship notes (see the hook register for who sends what). All done sends Calder's note.
- **For other sections:** `introOn()`, `introStep()`, `escortPlan(p, t)` (section 10's walker: `{ mode, to }` or null), `introBeats(id, d)` (section 14's `introPlan`: `[{ id, at, until, where }]`), `introEnd()`, `introSkip()`, `introArrival()` (Day 0, for section 18's "after arrival" rhythms), `introBegin(name)` (the name goes to section 15's `SHIP.player.name` through `playerNameSet`). Escort: `INTRO_ESCORT = 'torres-zeke'`; flag `escort.torres-zeke`.
- **Hash keys:** 700-749 (none used yet; 700 is set aside for your table at dinner).
- **intro_check.py:** `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 intro_check.py BUILD.html` (about 5 minutes).
