## Lines (section 22, the dialogue engineer, 0.43.x)
Pillar 4's dialogue engine and the writers' kit (plans/pillar4-plan.md Part 2; plans/social-deep-plan.md Parts 6 and 9). Writers fill plain text files in `content/` (the guide: `content/README.md`); `content_gen.py` bakes them into generated blocks in section 22; `lines_check.py` checks them.
- **Where:** section 22 sits after section 17's end and before `// 18. SHIP EVENTS`, so sections 18 and 20 can read the baked data at load (no temporal dead zone). Its code is lazy: it never touches ROSTER or SHIP at load.
- **Generated blocks** (never hand-edit; re-run `python3 content_gen.py`; a second run is byte-identical; `--check` exits 1 when stale): `// ---- BEGIN LINES` (`VOICE_TEMPLATES`, `VOICE`, `LINES`, `CONVOS`, `MOMENTS`, `MOMENT_THEMES`; since 0.43.20 `VOICE` and `LINES` are `JSON.parse('...')` strings, for load speed), `// ---- BEGIN MEETINGS` (`MEETINGS_BAKED`), `// ---- BEGIN TRADITIONS` (`const TRADITIONS = [ ... ]`, the only one in the build: one tradition a line, exactly what the traditions engineer's `plans/trad_parse.py` reads (`trad_parse.read_all`; the interface is theirs and frozen); texts `{ text, tone? }`; without that module and with .trad files present, content_gen stops). Shapes are in the README's "For the engineers". On a merge conflict inside a block, re-run the generator.
- **The Talk a while pack (talk engineer TE1, claude/talk-wire; plans/talk-pack-plan.md):** `content/talkpack/*.talk`, read by `parse_talk_file` in content_gen.py into the generated block `// ---- BEGIN TALKS` (`TALKS = { pool: [talk], own: { crewId: [talk] }, small: { small, done, small.<id>, done.<id>: [{ text, tone? }] } }`, a talk `{ id, topic, when?, lines: [{ text, tone? }], ask, replies: { listen, share, cheer: { text, ans } } }`, `when` a `linesOk` condition object). Ids are written by hand and never reused (a save's fired list holds them). New condition word `thread:` (the speaker's story theme: `voiceOf(id).thread`, else the roster's). **The picker (T2):** `talkPackPick(p, t)` -> `{ talk, src: 'own'|'pool', said: { lines, tones, ask, askTone, replies: { listen|share|cheer: { text, ans, tone } } } }` or `{ small: [{ text, tone }], done }`; the run-out order: their own talks in file order (any whose `when:` holds), then the pool (never for guests), a topic not had with them in `TALK_TOPIC_DAYS` (14) first, picked by schedU k 755, then small talk (3-4 lines; `small.<id>`, Veyli guests `small.saruth`; a `done` opener `TALK_DONE_ODDS` .25, never twice running). A talk with a placeholder that can't be filled is skipped (`{trad}` = `talkPackTrad(t)`, the next tradition, not an honoree's, no rec-deck one while `REC_DECK` is off). `talkPackFire(p, pick, t)` notes it. Saved: `SHIP.talks` (`talksState()`, lazily from `SAVED.talks` through `talksClean`) as `save.talks = { fired: { id: [day, crewId] }, small: { crewId: [day, done 0|1] } }`, written only once anything's noted (about 7.4 KB with the whole pack fired); `linesNew` clears it. Checked by `talk_check.py`. The run-out order is a list, `TALK_SOURCES` (`{ id, talks(p), pick: 'file'|'seeded', k }`), with **work pools (T4, the user's answers to plans/work-pools-plan.md):** `content/talkpack/work-<pool>.talk` (`@talks work:<pool>`, one of works watch survey bridge comms infirmary galley garden store; topic always `work`, no `topic:` line; ids `work.<pool>.<slug>`, unique across the pack), baked as `TALKS.work = { <pool>: [talk] }`; `TALK_WORK_POOLS` maps a speaker's dept to their pool at talk time (helm and command: bridge; guests and civilians none). The order is own, then the work pool and the shared pool taking turns (`turns: true` sources; `talkPackOrder`: the one had last with them goes behind, work first if neither), then small talk; work talks fire once a save like the rest. Switched on by content: with no work files the picks are as before. **Envoy Saruth's 4 own talks and `small.saruth` wait** for guests to become talkable (the chat card is crew only; backlog #15, for the ship-wide events build). **In the game (T3):** section 20's `talkStart` asks `talkPackStart(C)` (switch `TALK_PACK_ON`), which picks and fires the talk as it starts (walking off or "got to go" still uses it up: lead 20's OK) and keeps it on `C.talk.pack`; `talkNext`, the ask in `talkStep`, `talkReply` and `talkAnswer` ask `talkPackNext` / `talkPackAsk` / `talkPackReply` / `talkPackAnswer` first (typeof-guarded hooks, hook register). The card: opener (after the `talk.start`/`talk.sit` line), the more lines every `TALK_LINE` minutes, the question at `TALK_ASK` with the talk's three replies as buttons (the +2 for the one that suits them as before), its answer, then filler to the end. Small talk sets `asked`: no question, no buttons but Stop. `C.talk.topic` is null for a pack talk, so the old `talk.<topic>.*` lines aren't used (to be parked by the writers); the memory is still `talk.<topic>`. A filler line that's also one of the talk's lines isn't said again.
- **`lineFor(who, kind, ctx)`** → `{ text, id, kind, tone }` or null. Pool: the person's own lines (`VOICE[id].lines[kind]`, score 300), their template's (`VOICE_TEMPLATES[tpl]`, 200), the shared bank (`LINES[kind]`, 100), each +10 per condition; only lines whose conditions all hold (`linesOk`). Recently said: -15 if this person said it in an earlier hour (their last 6), -8 if someone else said it among the last 12 (`LINE_RECENT`, not saved). The top score wins; ties by weight `w`, picked with `schedU(p.seed, hour, 750)`, so a second tap in the same hour gives the same line. Placeholders and voice tokens by `linesFill` (a line that can't be filled is dropped before the pick). A `told:` line calls `memTold`. `ctx`: `{ t, other, room, thing, trad, machine, dish, deck, act, place, topic, mem, peek }`.
- **Conditions** (the frozen vocabulary, proposed to the social designer for wave 1; `COND_KEYS` in content_gen.py, value lists in lines_check.py): social ones read section 20's and 13's queries through `lineQ` (each `typeof`-guarded; a missing query fails the condition, a negated one holds); the engine's are `time weekday season place deck event trad dept rank trait habit act birthday pair topic`. Agreed with the social designer (2026-10-17) with additions: `week1` reads section 20's `inWeek1()`; `beat=arc.n` (exactly n, baked `beatIs`); `aff:<crewId>:<band>`; `leaving:soon|you` (`leavingSoon`, `ctx.goodbye`); `newhere` (`newHere`); `moved:<id>` (`movedOff`); `guide:true` (`flag('guide.active')`); `place:calm` reads section 20's `CALM_TYPES`; `{thing}` is `memThing(key)` of the line's `mem:`/`told:`. `odds:` uses `schedU` keys 770-797 (by line id).
- **`overheardFor(a, b, ctx)`** → `[{ who, text }]` (with `.id` and `.tone`) or null: `CONVOS` whose `pair` and conditions hold for speaker A (B as `ctx.other`), the most specific, picked with `schedU(a.seed ^ b.seed, hour, 751)`; `ctx.skip` lists ids not to repeat, `ctx.noSerious` leaves out `#serious` ones.
- **Overheard, played (0.43.12):** `linesTick()` every frame (from section 20's `socialTick`); `OVH` is the conversation playing. Constants `OVH_ODDS` .3 a ship minute, `OVH_GAP` 20 s, `OVH_STEP` 3.2 s a line, `OVH_PAIR` 2.5 tiles. The voice budget (`linesHush`: CHAT, DIALOGUE, sleeping; section 20's `CHATTER_AT`/`CHATTER_GAP` shared both ways) and the saved ring (`linesState`, `linesRing(day)`, `linesHeard(id, day, tone)`; `SHIP.lines` in save.lines; an entry dated after today, from an earlier game, doesn't count).
- **`linesLegacy(kind)`**: section 20's old lists in their old shapes from the lines marked `old` (`content/banks/old.lines`), so section 20's picking (and every check) is unchanged until it asks `lineFor`. **`linesMeetings(into)`**: merges `MEETINGS_BAKED` into `MEETING_SCRIPTS`, turning `{me}` texts into `c => ...c.you` (for section 18 to call once).
- **Voice tokens** `{hi} {bye} {yes} {thanks} {wow} {sigh}` come from the card's `words`, else the template's, else `LINE_WORDS`, picked with `schedU` keys 760-769; `{nick}` by tier (null: `{me}`).
- **schedU keys:** 750-799: 750 the pick, 751 overheard, 752 overheard's odds, 753 its pair order, 754 reactions' odds (0.43.26: the tries are worked one a frame), 755-759 the Talk a while pack (755 the pool pick, 756 small talk's order, 757 a "done" opener's odds, 758 three or four lines, 759 which "done"), 760-769 voice tokens, 770-797 `odds:`, 798 the work pool's pick (the Talk a while pack); free: 799.
- **Hooks outside section 22** (for the hook register): section 20, five `const CHAT_* = linesLegacy(...)` lines and one in `chatGreet` (`typeof lineFor === 'function' && VOICE[p.id] ...`: a person with their own lines greets with them; it peeks when asked about another hour, so section 20's look-ahead never counts as said); and (0.43.12) `if (typeof linesTick === 'function') linesTick();` after `chatTick();` in `socialTick`. Section 13 (0.43.14): `if (typeof linesNew === 'function') linesNew();` in the New game handler, after `socialNew`. Section 22 also sets section 20's `CHATTER_AT` while a conversation plays. Proposed, not yet made: section 18, `linesMeetings(MEETING_SCRIPTS);` after `const MEETING_SCRIPTS = {};`.
- **lines_check.py:** `python3 lines_check.py [GAME.html] [--strict] [--quiet]` (no browser, a second). Errors: unreadable files, unknown tokens or condition values, lengths (card 90, bubble 60, beats/moments/meetings 140; a placeholder counts 9), unknown crew ids, the banned list, unearned `#serious`, stale baked blocks. Warnings: unknown names, duplicates, American spellings, habits the person lacks, reading ease under 60. Reports coverage per person against the plan's targets (`--strict` makes it an error: from wave 2) and the voice budget's rules and supply. `--day` plays the voice budget in the game (0.43.19): run it on a build with the writers' content baked, whenever section 22's playing code, section 20's chatter or bubbles change.

## Your device (section 19, the device engineer, 0.35.x)

Section `// 19. DEVICE` sits **before** section 15 (after 14), so sections 15 and 17 can register at load. It's the game's one menu (Pillar 3 plan, Part 1). Two layers:

- **Apps describe.** `deviceApp({ id, title, icon, order, badge, view, act })`. `view(ctx)` (ctx: `{ t, wide }`) returns a VIEW: `{ title, blocks }`, each block one of `{ kind: 'text', text, mark }`, `{ kind: 'list', rows: [{ text, sub, mark, action, button }] }`, `{ kind: 'bars', items: [{ label, value, mark, note, level }] }`, `{ kind: 'buttons', items: [{ label, action }] }`, `{ kind: 'chips', items: [{ label, on, action }] }` (0.55.0: big toggles crossed out while `on` is false; build one with `devChips(items)`, which falls back to buttons marked "✕ " on a skin without chips; `DEVICE_SKIN.has(kind)`). An action is `{ app, do, arg }`; `do: 'open'` opens an app, anything else goes to that app's `act(verb, arg)`. Marks: `up`, `down`, `new`, `urgent`, `hot`, `big`. Apps never build DOM. `badge()` returns a number or null.
- **The skin draws.** `DEVICE_SKIN` (open, close, draw, notify, button, wide) is the only code that touches the device's DOM (`#device`, `#devBody`, `#devBtn`, `#devBack`). It draws the home screen's tile grid with badges.
- **Other calls:** `deviceShow(id)`, `deviceClose()`, `deviceRefresh()` (section 15 calls `device.refresh()` every ship minute), `deviceNotify({ app, text, urgent })` (a badge on the app until it's opened; a toast if urgent), `deviceOn('open' | 'close', fn)`. Errors from apps go to `DEV.errors` and the app shows "(this app failed to draw)".
- **The shim.** `device.addSection(id, title, render)` registers a legacy app drawn by `render(el)`; an id a native app already has is ignored (so section 17's `jobsSection` registration is a no-op). `device.open(id)`, `close()`, `show(id)` (`'status'` is home), `refresh()`, `isOpen`.
- **Apps at 0.35.4:** as below, plus Memos, Ship, Record, and the Crew and Appointments shells. **At 0.35.2:** home (time, where you're expected and what's next, today's traditions, what you're doing, carrying, job progress, ship notes), Schedule, Calendar, Needs, Jobs (tracks only), Mail, Wallet, Settings (screen colour, screen effects, tips).
- **Your day (0.35.2).** `devDay(d)`: breakfast and dinner (`MEALS`), the day shift (`DEV_SHIFT`, every day: the player has no rota of their own yet), the week's shape (`DEV_WEEK`: Monday's briefing, Wednesday's drill, Thursday's inspection and the Captain's piano) and the traditions. `devTraditions(a, b)` reads section 18's `upcomingTraditions(a, b)` when it exists, else `eventsOn(d)`.
- **Reminders (0.35.2).** `deviceTick()` runs from `deviceRefresh` once a ship minute (not while loading or asleep): each tradition in the next `ahead` minutes gets one `deviceNotify` on the Calendar; urgent (a toast) unless it's during the day shift, where only `per[kind] === true` ("Remind me") chirps; `per[kind] === false` ("Don't") is silent. A new morning puts a badge on the Calendar. `SHIP.device.calendarRemind = { on, ahead, morning, per }` is saved.
- **Tips (0.35.4).** `tipShow(id, text)` → false if tips are off or that id was ever shown (`SHIP.device.tipsSeen`, saved). One card at a time (`TIP_Q`, `tipDone()`), above the sheet when it's open. Section 15b decides when (`ixTip`).
- **Camera framing (0.35.4).** `deviceFrame(on)`; `deviceFrameStep` (its own `requestAnimationFrame`) eases `DEV.fr` 0 → 1 and moves the camera's `top` and `bottom` down by a third of the view, in whole pixels, so the player sits in the visible top third. At 0 it never touches the camera, so builds with the device closed render identically. Only for the phone sheet; the wide side panel needs none.
- **Dialogue and chat (0.35.5).** `deviceDialogue(view, onTap)` with section 18's view `{ title, name, who, text, to, pose, choices, i, n }` (null closes it). Section 18 runs the flow (advancing after 7 s, replies, closing when you walk out); this draws and calls `onTap()` / `onTap(k)`; the X calls `dialogueClose()`. `body.dlgon` while it shows: tips wait. `DEVICE_SKIN.chat` becomes section 20's `CHAT_SKIN.draw` once the page has loaded.
- **Maps (0.35.3).** The VIEW block `{ kind: 'map', grid, rooms: { key: { name, kind } }, you, pick, goal, action }`; kinds `room`, `hall`, `service` (still or spine rooms), `locked`, `void`, `lift`. The skin draws one `<rect>` per run of a room's tiles in a row and one `<path>` for all the walls, with the pattern `#devhatch` for locked rooms.
- **Jobs and Track (0.35.2).** The device no longer takes or drops jobs (the board does). Track sets `SHIP.player.track` to null (off) or the job's id; it starts undefined, which counts as tracked. Section 15b decides what the arrow shows from it.
- **The skin (0.35.1), the old-school screen.** CSS tokens on `.device` (`--crt-ink`, `--crt-dim`, `--crt-bg`, `--crt-hot`, `--crt-glow`, `--crt-scan`, `--crt-radius`, `--crt-font`, `--crt-size`, `--bezel`), with `data-ph="amber" | "green" | "cyan"` choosing the phosphor. The dim colours are the plan's, brightened just enough for 7 : 1 (amber `#d09142`, green `#42b567`, cyan `#4ab1a3`). The scan lines and vignette are one `.crt::after` overlay; the roll is `.crt::before` moved with a transform; both animations only with `.fx` and never under `prefers-reduced-motion`. `.wide` (wider than 720px and landscape) docks it on the left; otherwise it's a bottom sheet and `body.devsheet` lifts the toast above it. A capture listener closes the sheet on a tap on the ship (the tap doesn't walk you) and opens it on a swipe up from the bottom 28px.
- **Saved:** `SHIP.device = { settings: { phosphor, fx, tips } }` in `save.device` (SAVE_EXTRA), read defensively. `SAVE_V` stays 2 until memos and appointments arrive.
- **device_check.py (0.35.0):** `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 device_check.py BUILD.html` (about a minute) at phone (390 × 844) and wide (1280 × 800) sizes: every app opens from its tile and draws without errors and keeps the look rules (15px+, 44px buttons, no blur, 7 : 1), Back and Close, mail and notify badges, the shim, the panel on screen, nothing added to the 3D scene, `deviceOn` once per open and close.

## Deck 2 (builds 0.9.x, the Deck 2 architect)

Built from plans/deck2-plan.html (Sketch 8) on branch `claude/deck2-command`, alongside the Deck 1 architect (0.8.x). Everything for Deck 2 sits in one block in section 4d, between Deck 3 and Deck 6: the `// ---- Deck 2 kit ----` (materials `D2`, new pieces added to `KIT` and `WALL_KIT` with `Object.assign`, nothing calls `R()` at load) and then `DECKS[2]`. No shared engine code was changed.

- **Turn 1, the shell (0.9.1).** The plan's data went in as it was, with two fixes:
    - The plan listed `v,23,9` as Watch office glass, but that edge is between the lobby and the science corridor (both halls), so it would have walled off a row of the lobby. Dropped. The Watch office's glass is `v,23,6` and `v,23,8` either side of its door `v,23,7`.
    - The plan's Watch office plate was on `v,23,4`, which faces into the Second Officer's cabin. The plate is on `v,23,5` (now plain wall) instead.
    - **Bedroom plates** are brass plates on the header above each door (`nameplates`, wall kit `brassPlate`), not the teal plates beside it: Vance's and the Envoy's corner cabins have no wall beside their doors, and a plate over the door can't be mistaken for the neighbour's. Names: Capt. Marrow, Cdr. Vance, Works Chief, Infirmary Chief, Dr. Sato, Second Officer, Envoy Saruth, Chief Holt, Visitors, Senior Helm, Comms Chief, Counsellor, Watch Officer, Survey Deputy, Visitors. The six new officers are placeholders, by role.
    - **Mood light:** `sky #e9e6ff`, `sun #fff0dc`, `hemi .94`, `sunI .96`: a touch cooler in the shade and softer in the sun.
    - About 220 draw calls by the lift (empty shell).
- **Turn 2, the bedrooms (0.9.2).**
    - **`d2Suite(room, doorKey, o)`** furnishes a bedroom from its door. Bedroom doors are `closed`, and `roomFrame` only finds `door` and `open` edges, so `d2Frame` takes the door key instead (same frame: u along the door wall, v into the room). Layouts are drawn with the door in the left half and mirrored otherwise. Three layouts: hull cabins 4 × 5 (bed under the windows, desk on the far wall, en-suite in the front corner, wardrobe and armchair by the door, bookcase and plant at the back), the 3 × 5 cabins by the lift (V, H), and inner 4 × 4 cabins (`inner`, with a desk or `armchairs`). Options: `fab` (headboard, throw and armchair), `rug`, `papers`, `kettle`, `model`, `swans`, `trophy`, and `bed` / `desk` tap text.
    - **Corner cabins** X (Vance) and E (Envoy Saruth) are placed by hand, clear of the rounded stern corner.
    - **Taps:** every bedroom has one or two `see` items (bed, desk, the Envoy's tea table): tap them through the cutaway and the text shows without walking, since the doors stay shut. That's how the player sees into the bedrooms.
    - **Cost:** all bedroom kit uses shared `D2` materials and the rooms are `NO_FADE`, so they merge with the rest of the deck: about 250 draw calls by the lift, 290–310 over the quarters with walls down, about 540 zoomed right out (Deck 3 is 520–560 at that zoom). No glow sprites in bedrooms; the lamp shades are emissive.
- **Turn 3, the Captain's quarters and the corridor (0.9.3).**
    - **Captain's quarters `C`** (room-local 5 × 12, stern windows on the west wall at x 0, door `v,7,10` at z 5.5): wide `suiteBed` (`wide: true`) at (2.3, 1.2) with the en-suite and wardrobe by it, `alcoveCurtain` across z 3.0 (x 0.15–3.35, open on the door side), telescope at (.7, 4); `diningTable` at (2.3, 6.4) along the room with `light: true` (the deck's one real lamp; `LAMP_SLOTS` is 2), window seat on the stern wall, sideboard by the door; study: `captainDesk` at (2.25, 9.4) facing the room, low `suiteShelf`s along the south and east walls (tall bookcases hid the study from the usual camera), a globe and an armchair by the windows.
    - **Corridor:** `brassSconce` on ten wall edges (two flanking the Captain's door), `portrait`s on `v,7,7`, `v,7,13`, `v,9,8`, `v,9,13` (tappable: Varga, Moro, Brenner, Marrow; the three paintings are canvases drawn at load, no `R()`), `consoleTable`s (snug) under the gallery's east portraits.
    - About 255 draw calls by the lift, 330 over the Captain's quarters with walls down.
- **Turn 4, science, the Watch office and the array (0.9.4).**
    - **Kit:** `chartTable` (its top is `D2.chart`, a star chart drawn at load from a fixed hash), `chartRack`, `labBench` (`scope`, `centrifuge`), `labIsland`, `sampleScanner`, `specimenShelf`, `brassTelescope`, `commsDesk`, `signalBoard` (`D2.wave`), `fumeHood`, `gloveBox`, `probeCradle`, `launchTube`, `signalRacks` (`D2.rack`), `mastBase`, `sensorDish`, `humJar`; wall kit `starChart`. Reused: `controlBoard`, `desk`, `locker`, `dispenser`, `crate`, `coldShelf`, `windowSeat`, `stool`, `beanbag`, `clocks`, `msgBoard`, `notices`, and the `washroom` template.
    - **The dish** (`sensorDish` in `A` at (3.2, 10.5), turned to face the bow): a turntable group built, then merged on its own with `mergeMeshes(tt)` and marked `noMergeAll`, so it turns as a few meshes (`SPINS`, 0.12 rad/s). The feed's material and glow are made at dress time and breathe (`PULSES`). `light: true` adds the deck's second real lamp and tints it blue (`LAMPS[...].l.color`), so Deck 2 uses both `LAMP_SLOTS`. The humming sample in the specimen store pulses the same way.
    - **Locked rooms** F, P and A are `NO_FADE`; their taps are `see` items.
    - **Memory:** Deck 2's shared textures (rugs, portraits, charts, waveforms, rack lights) are made at load and upload on the first visit, then stay, like Deck 5's hatch textures. deck_check's Deck 4 round trips stay steady (496 geometries, 34 textures).
    - **Cost:** about 285 draw calls by the lift (Deck 3 326, Deck 4 332), 230–300 over the science rooms with walls down, 540–580 zoomed right out over the quarters.
- **0.9.5:** `hull.byFace: true` on Deck 2 (the shared splitter edit from the bridge branch, opt-in per deck; Decks 3–6 keep the old split).
- **Deck 2 is done (0.9.4, hull fix 0.9.5) and waiting for the user's review.**
- **Places (0.19.1, Pillar 1 workstream 3c).** `DECKS[2].places` lists 162 places, made from the furniture's real positions (a script logs every `place()` on Deck 2, then puts a place on each seat, bed and workstation) and stored as fixed data.
    - **Ids:** beds `2-<room letter>` for all fifteen bedrooms, visitors' rooms included. Everything else `2-<what>-<n>`: `shower`, `basin`, `toilet` (en-suites, numbered in placing order), `wctoilet` and `wcbasin` (the public washroom), `desk`, `armchair`, `cushion`, `windowseat`, `beanbag`, `captable`, `command`, `survey`, `comms`, `watch`, `bench`, `liftwait`.
    - **Types:** desk chairs and armchairs in bedrooms are `lounge-seat`; the Captain's desk chair is `work:command`. Rule proposed to the lead: a place in a room that has a bed place belongs to that bed's owner, so nobody is sent into someone else's cabin.
    - **Watch posts:** the roster puts 8 Watch crew on Deck 2 by day and the office has 4 work spots (duty chair, status board ×3), so four standing posts make up the rest: the lockers, the lobby by the office glass, outside the Captain's door, and the end of the science corridor at the array window.
    - **Locked doors:** every bedroom door is `closed`, so today's pathfinding can't take anyone to a bed. Raised with the lead (proposal: a walker may pass a `closed` door whose `sealed` room holds their own bed).
    - **Layout fixes (0.19.2):** some places were boxed in by furniture: S's and R's interiors, Vance's cabin, the Captain's en-suite, two of the Captain's table seats and two of the public washroom's three toilets. Fixed by moving S's and R's armchair, Vance's wardrobe and seating, and the Captain's wardrobe, and by laying the washroom out by hand (2 toilets, 1 basin). The Captain's window seat stays (the lead's call), so the two table seats behind it aren't places. 159 places, all reachable by the official place check.
- **Activities (0.32.1, Pillar 2 A5c).** `anim` on 55 places: `read` 35, `talk` 16, `drink` 4. The chart room's and observatory's public seats are `common-room` (8), for free time; the telescope and the lab and Watch spots stay work-only.
- **Utility rooms (0.32.2).** Room-local layouts: `g` (2 × 9): `gasRack` at x 1.675, z .5–2.5 and 5.5–6.5 against the observatory wall, `gasManifold` (1.7, 3.5), `extractor` (1.625, 7.5), `conduits` (1.8, 8.5) as the lines going down; the aisle is column 39. `u` (2 × 5): `powerCabinet` (1.65, 1.5), `relayRack` (1.65, 3.5), conduits at both ends; the aisle is column 23. `n` (7 × 1): `cableTray` (w 5) along the hull, `signalRepeater` at the aft end, `arrayFeed` at the bow end. Machine kinds come from `MACHINE_KINDS` (section 17), so the registry keeps them: gasplant, scrubber (the extractor), powerjunction (the breakers), junction (the light relays), relay (repeater, feed, racks), array (the dish). Ids keep the old stand-ins' `2-gasplant-1`, `2-junction-1` and `2-array-1`, so saved wear carries over. Machine panels face away from the default camera (their service side is the aisle).
- **The answers pass (0.32.3).** Door plates name every officer (`d2Plate`, from `ROSTER` by bed id, at dress time). Observatory night: the gathering is every Deck 2 place with `event: 'observatory-night'` (11: bench ×3, beanbags ×2, cushions ×2, standing ×4); new ones are type `event`, so only the scheduler that asks for the event uses them. `window: true` marks seats and spots by a window. Lived-in touches in `g`, `u`, `n` (wall pieces sit on each room's far walls, so they show with walls on Auto).
- **Machine wear:** drawn centrally by section 8b (0.27.9) from each `machines` entry's `at` and `face`; add `look: { lamp, screen, drip, light }` to a Deck 2 machine only where the default spot is hidden. Deck 2's own kit names are `crawlCableTray` and `lightRelayRack` (0.32.5; don't reuse other decks' kit names, the last definition wins).
- **Seats (0.32.6):** every sit place carries `xy` and `y` measured against its furniture (check with `?seats`, section 8b). Move furniture, re-measure its seats. The en-suite (`ensuite`) has a toilet at local (.24, .42, .2), facing -x.

## The crew roster (builds 0.16.x, the crew designer)

Pillar 1, workstream 2. Everything is in section 9, after `CREW`, between the `BEGIN ROSTER` and `END ROSTER` markers, plus two lines below them.

- **`ROSTER`:** 74 of the 75 crew in the format of plans/pillar1-plan.md "The roster". With you, that's 1 Captain, 1 XO, 4 Chiefs, 6 Senior Officers, 16 Officers and 47 Juniors; 60 on days, 15 on nights. Each entry has `id`, `name`, `story` flag (and `player` on yours), `rank`, `dept`, `uniform`, `role`, `shift`, `rota.offDays`, `bed`, `work: { deck, place }`, `habits`, `traits`, `looks`, `bio`, `life` and `seed`.
    - `dept` is the job's department: command, helm, comms, works, watch, survey, infirmary or galley. `uniform` is the uniform it wears, one of `makeCharacter`'s five `DEPT` keys (helm and comms wear command red, the galley wears works gold). `rosterSpec(p)` turns an entry into what `makeCharacter` takes.
    - Story crew keep `CREW`'s looks and bios exactly. The Works Chief (Chief Ines Calder), the Infirmary Chief and the six senior officers are written by hand too; the user approved their names (0.16.6), so nothing is provisional any more.
    - Workplaces use the place types `work:command`, `work:helm`, `work:ops`, `work:comms`, `work:watch`, `work:survey`, `work:galley`, `work:infirmary`, `work:works` and `work:engine`.
    - Days off: two in a row, staggered round the week within each department (20–22 people off on any day).
    - Night shift: the 6 juniors in Bunk 8 (the night-shift room), the Second Officer, the Senior Watch Officer and 7 officers (in the quiet inner cabins).
- **`ROSTER_GUESTS`:** people who live aboard but aren't crew, so not in the 75: Envoy Saruth (bed `2-E`).
- **`ROSTER_PLAYER`:** you. You're one of the 75 (one of the 47 juniors, in Works) but aren't scheduled, so your entry is kept out of `ROSTER`. You sleep in Bunk 1, berth 4 (`4-B1-4`), the top of the second bunk, as Bunk 1's furniture text says (you lost the coin toss for the bottom one); Tomas has berth 1 under his taped star map, Chloe Tran berth 2 above him, and berth 6 is the one spare berth on the ship.
- **`HABITS`:** the shared habit vocabulary, each with what it does to a day. The schedule engine reads it. Since 0.16.4 it has 24: the first 18, plus six for Pillar 2's activities, one room's regulars each: `runner` (gym treadmills, Deck 3), `shopper` (crew store, Deck 3), `musician` (plays in the Deck 4 music room; `music` listens), `gardener` (tends the Deck 3 garden; `garden-lover` sits), `bar-regular` (bow lounge bar, Deck 3) and `stargazer` (Deck 2 observatory). Nobody has more than one of the six. Five story crew got the one their life lines already suggest (Marrow musician, Tomas and Mori stargazer, Nandakumar gardener, Ferreira bar-regular); the generated crew get theirs from a second random stream (`NEW_R`), weighted towards people whose habits go with it and spread across departments, so nothing else in the roster moved. Regulars: runner 10, shopper 10, bar-regular 9, musician 6, gardener 6, stargazer 6. The roster page now shows each person's habits.
- **Work hours and galley teams (0.16.5):** `rota.hours` is an optional list of work blocks, `[start, end]` in ship minutes from midnight, that replaces `SHIFTS[shift]` on work days; only the two storekeepers have it. The galley staff carry `rota.team` (`chef`, `early`, `late`) and the deputy `rota.cover` instead, with no hours: the galley engineer's `GALLEY_SHIFTS` (section 16) is the one copy of the galley's hours. The roster page describes the teams in words, without times, for the same reason.
- **Birthdays and door plates (0.16.6):** every entry (ROSTER, ROSTER_GUESTS and ROSTER_PLAYER) has `birthday`, a ship day of the year 1–364 (52 weeks of 7 days; day 1 is the game's Day 1, a Monday; the day of the year is `(day - 1) % 364 + 1`). All 76 fall on different days, every week has one or two, and the first is Day 4 (Chloe Tran). Everyone above junior, and the Envoy, has `plate: { rank, name }`, the two lines for their cabin door: rank words as RANK_NAME says them (`Captain`, `Executive Officer`, `Department Chief`, `Senior Officer`, `Officer`; the Envoy's is `Veyli Envoy`), and the name with rank titles moved to the rank line ("Chief Bram Holt" gives `Bram Holt`; "Dr." and "Chef" stay). The plate's door is the room of their `bed`. Both come from their own random stream (`BDAY_R`) or plain rules, after everything else, so nothing else moved.
- **Habits that change (0.16.7):** every ROSTER and ROSTER_GUESTS entry has `habitShifts`, 1 or 2 items `{ add?, drop?, why }` (HABITS keys; `why` is a whole sentence naming the person, shown as it is). Each is valid against the person's habits as generated (adds only what they lack, drops only what they have, no clashing pair, `common-room` for juniors only). The hand-written crew and the Envoy have hand-written ones from their life lines (`SHIFTS_HAND`); the rest are drawn by `SHIFT_R` from swaps, adds and drops. The sim engineer's `habitWeek` (section 14) picks who changes and when, applies one item, logs it in `SHIP.habitLog`, and skips items that have stopped being valid. You (`ROSTER_PLAYER`) have none: your habits are yours.
- **Visitors (0.16.8, Pillar 3 C8):** inert data in the ROSTER block, after `ROSTER_PLAYER`.
    - `GUESTS`: 60 entries in roster shape (`rank: 'guest'`, `habits`, `bio`, `life`, `seed`, optional `work`) plus `template`, `cabin`, `arrive` and `leave` (ship days of the year, 1–364, like birthdays) and `spec` (ready for `crowd.add`/`makeCharacter`: specialists wear their service's uniform; everyone else has `dept: 'civilian'` and `outfit`, a colour, since makeCharacter has no civilian outfit yet and falls back to the survey colour).
    - Event guests carry `event` and `stay` (days) with `arrive`/`leave` null: the events engineer sets the day. The Veyli delegation of four for `diplomatic` (Speaker Teshavi in Deck 2's second visitors' cabin `2-U`; Kesrin, Moleth and Ithra in `4-01`–`4-03`), Dr. Soraya Lindgren for `nebula` (`4-04`), Taavek for `veyliVessel` (`4-04`), and Mara Quill and Jonah Petrie for `salvage` (`4-01`, `4-02`). Event guests use only `2-U` and `4-01`–`4-04`, one event at a time.
    - Everyday visitors: 52, one arriving each ship week (specialists for 4–9 days, passengers and transfers for 2–6), in `4-07` and `4-08` by turns, so they never meet an event guest or each other. The first arrives on Day 5. The visitors' suite `2-B` is left free for dignitaries.
    - `GUEST_TEMPLATES`: `scientist`, `delegate`, `passenger` and `survivor`, each `{ label, day, evening, meals, wake, bed }` (and `first`/`firstDay` for survivors, `formal` for delegates), with places as `[deck, type, weight]` like LEISURE, plus `['cabin', w]` and `['work', w]`. The sim engineer turns them into days.
    - Their own random stream (`VIS_R`) and name pools (`VIS_FIRST`, `VIS_LAST`), so the crew didn't move.
- **What the story crew tell you (0.16.8):** the story crew and the Envoy have `about: [{ tier, topic, text }]` (0.16.9: tiers and topics agreed with the social engineer), things they tell you as you get to know them: `tier` is section 20's relationship tier (0 stranger, 1 acquaintance, 2 colleague, 3 friend, 4 close friend, kept for a real confidence) and `topic` is `home`, `work`, `hobby`, `past` or `secret`, for the social engineer's chat lines and the Crew app: 7 each for Tomas, Pell and Rina, 4 for Marrow, Vance, Sato, Holt, Calder, Brennan and Saruth. They add to the bios and life lines, which are unchanged.
- **The garden (0.16.11):** three crew with the gardener habit now work the hydroponics garden (dept `garden`, uniform `survey`, work `{ deck: 3, place: 'work:garden' }`, the stations the deck architect adds in 0.38.6; the sim engineer's `gardenDay` (0.26.20) runs their day). Tove Cruz, Senior gardener (`team: 'senior'`, 08:00–17:00, off Sat and Sun); Ravi Hart, Gardener (`early`, 06:00–14:00, off Mon and Tue); Imani O'Neill, Gardener (`late`, 13:00–21:00, off Wed and Thu). New optional `rota.hoursOn: [{ days, hours }]`: on those days the hours replace `rota.hours`; Tove takes the early hours on Mon and Tue and the late hours on Wed and Thu, so every day has someone from 06:00 and someone until 21:00 (the generator asserts it). Ranks, beds, habits and the 60/15 split are unchanged; Works has two fewer technicians and Survey one fewer officer.
- **Pillar 4, seat 9 (0.16.12):** all in the generator, after everything else, so nobody's name, looks, bed, habits or birthday moved.
    - **Varied days off** (`days_off`): two in a row for everyone. 22 of the 59 day crew (about 37%, after the couples' adjustment in 0.16.13), and you, are off Saturday–Sunday; the rest are spread across the week by department; the night shift has its own spread (`NIGHT_PAIRS`). Fixed: Torres Thu–Fri, Halloran Sat–Sun, Calder Sat–Sun, and the galley, store and garden rotas as agreed. The generator asserts that every department with two or more people on a shift has someone on every day (lone night officers in command, comms and the infirmary have days off like anyone). 52 of 76 people's days off changed; the list is in the 0.16.12 report to the lead.
    - **Social fields** (the social plan, Part 9) on every crew member, Saruth and the reserve: `tpl` (one of the ten voice templates, from their traits), `openness`, `needs` (the reply flavour), `loves` and `likes` (gift tags: tea, sweet, plant, garden, made, book, music, star, game; from their habits and life lines), `joy`, `thread` (`arc:<id>` for the ten story arcs, Torres's being `arc:torres`, otherwise one of the twelve struggle themes: homesick, exams, family, stuck, notgood, lonely, loss, talent, burnout, leaving, rivalry, startover; these spellings are frozen with social B and the generator asserts them), `mentors` (0.16.13: true for patient, kind, steady, gentle, warm, calm or generous people, plus Torres, Calder, Oduya and Brennan; social B's crew-to-crew mentor bonds), `keepsake`, `home` and `family`. The story crew, the seniors, Torres and Saruth are hand-written (`SOCIAL_HAND`); the rest come from rules and `P4_R`.
    - **`about`:** six or seven details for everyone (tiers 0–4, one tier-4 secret each, topics home, work, hobby, past, secret). Hand-written for the seniors and Torres, two more each for the story crew who had four; generated from templates (their work place, home, family, joy and thread) for everyone else.
    - **Couples:** four established couples, crew only and never story crew: `partner` both ways (Mateo Navarro and Elif Yoon, Farah Rahman and Ravi Hart, Marta Soto and Rosa Abara, Carmen Chen and Karin Yilmaz). A couple shares `transferTo`, and (0.16.13) at least one day off: the partner who's free to move takes the other's days where department staffing allows (Mateo Navarro, Farah Rahman and Carmen Chen moved). Leavers are never half of a couple.
    - **Transfers:** `stays: true` on the Captain, the Chiefs, Torres, Tomas, Pell, Rina, Brennan and Saruth; everyone else has `transferTo` (the ship they'd go to, for letters). `aboard: null` (always) for all crew who stay; the twelve leavers have `aboard: [null, rotationDay]` (0.16.13).
    - **`ROSTER_RESERVE`:** 12 future crew (4 new cadets, `cadet: true`), in roster shape with every field above, `reserve: true`, `aboard: [rotationDay, null]` and (0.16.13, agreed with the sim engineer) `replaces: '<leaver id>'`: each arrival copies the leaver's bed, work, shift and rota (galley team included), so nobody else's desk or bunk moves. Leavers are picked by the generator now, deterministically: the same role and shift as their replacement (a Works cadet replaces a Works technician), never anyone with `stays` or a partner, never twice. **`ROSTER_ROTATIONS`:** six Sundays (Days 35, 77, 119, 161, 203, 245, every six ship weeks from week 5), each `{ day, out: [leaver ids], in: [arrival ids] }`: 1, 2, 2, 3, 2 and 2 each way; the first brings a new cadet. Leavers are fixed in the data, so social can't steer a rotation away from someone mid-thread; if that's wanted, the sim would skip or postpone that person. The reserve runs out after Day 245.
    - **Hobby and since (0.16.14):** `hobby` (an -ing phrase) and `since` (time aboard, a duration phrase; for the reserve, from the day they arrive) on every crew member, Saruth and the reserve, added last by `hobby_since` with no random draws (hand-written in `HOBBY_HAND` and `SINCE_HAND`; the rest from `HOBBY_OF_LINE`, `HOBBY_OF_JOY`, `HOBBY_OF_TAG`, `SINCE_NEW` and `SINCE_OF` by `stable(id)`). `family` is said after "my" (no article, no third person; asserted); `FAMILY` keeps both forms, and the about templates use the "Has a ..." form through `FAMILY_HAS`.
    - **Kin, lost and hobbyNoun (0.16.15):** short fill-ins for the theme lines, added last by `kin_lost` with no random draws. `kin` ("my gran"; "" if none living and not a partner; `KIN_HAND`, else `KIN_OF[family]`), `lost` ("my wife"; only Oduya (`LOST_HAND`) and people whose about lines say "Lost someone close", from `LOST_OF` by `stable(id)`; never kin), `hobbyNoun` (`HOBBY_NOUN[hobby]`, a bare activity noun). A new hobby phrase needs a `HOBBY_NOUN` entry (the generator fails without one); a new family phrase needs a `KIN_OF` entry.
- **Beds:**
    - Deck 2: the 12 senior crew, one per room (`2-C` Captain, `2-X` Vance, `2-O` Works Chief, `2-I` Infirmary Chief, `2-V` Sato, `2-T` Holt, `2-S`, `2-H`, `2-R`, `2-N`, `2-G`, `2-D`); visitor rooms `2-B` and `2-U` are empty.
    - Deck 4 cabins: the 16 officers in `4-05`, `4-06` and `4-09` to `4-22` (none in the guest cabins or spares).
    - Deck 4 bunks: the 47 juniors (you included) in 8 rooms × 6 berths (`4-B<n>-1` to `-6`), with `4-B1-6` spare. As of 0.14.0 the rooms hold 4 berths each (2 double bunks); the user chose bigger rooms with three ordinary bunk beds, which workstream 3a is building.
- **Fixed data:** `plans/crew-roster-gen.py` is seeded, so re-running it without edits writes exactly the same block. Edit it (names, habits, who's where) and re-run to change the crew; it rewrites the block in place and `plans/crew-roster.html`. Nothing in the roster calls `R()`.

## How decks work (section 4)

Unchanged from DEV_NOTES_2 in outline:

- `SHIP` holds state that outlives a deck.
- 4a is the current deck: per-deck `let` tables, replaced by each build.
- 4b is the shared kit, made once at load.
- 4c is `buildDeck(def)` and `unloadDeck()`. A build reseeds the random generator with `def.seed`, and unloading frees everything made after the build's id watermark.
- 4d holds the deck definitions in `DECKS`.

**Deck definition fields:**

- **Required:** `num`, `name`, `seed`, `lift` (the car's centre in tile units), `map`, `doors`, `rooms`, `windows`, `closedDoors`, `hull` and `dress(d)`.
- **For the lift:** `blurb` (subtitle in the lift panel) and `arrive` (toast text on arrival).
- **Optional:**
    - `hazard` paints hazard bands at doorways onto steel floors.
    - `markings` gives keep-clear outlines: `[i, j, w, d]` in tiles.
    - `plates` and `sconces`, plus Deck 4's `cabins`.
    - `lockedRoom(r)` gives tap text for locked rooms.
- **Deck 5 also has:**
    - `sealed`: door key → room letter. Its `closedDoors` are built from the rooms' `tap` text.
    - `workLights`: wall lights listed in data and placed by its `dress`.

**Door types** in `doors`:

| Type | What it is |
| --- | --- |
| `door` | Slides open for anyone nearby. |
| `closed` | Shut; tap text comes from `closedDoors`. |
| `hatch` | A shut service hatch with hazard leaves. Counts as closed everywhere. |
| `glass` | An inner window: wall-textured sill, pane and header, lowering with the cutaway like a wall. |
| `lift` | Walkable lift doors. Opens for anyone near, unless a ride holds it with `d.force`. |

Two types are generated, never listed: `open` (between two `hall` rooms) and `hull`. An edge between two solid (void) rooms doesn't exist, so a door listed there does nothing.

**Room flags:**

| Flag | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `name` | Shown in the HUD. |
| `floor` | Floor type (below). |
| `hall` | Hall rooms join each other with no wall. |
| `void` | A solid block with hatch texture. |
| `tap` | Text for sealed rooms. |
| `pad: [i, j, w, d]` | The shuttle pad marking. |
| `spine` | The floor draws nothing from the random sequence. |

**Floor types:** `warm`, `wood`, `tile`, `plate`, `corridor`, `lobby`, `carpet`, `mess`, `steel` (diamond plate), `haul` (plate with lanes), `bay` (darker plate), `grate`, `lift` (ribbed rubber), `deck` (the promenade's pale planks, with guide stripes and an inlaid light line along the walls, for hall rooms), `gym` (flecked rubber), `garden` (green pavers). `carpet` only suits hall rooms: it draws a guide stripe wherever the next tile isn't a hall.

**Ship frame.** Every deck is placed so its lift centre sits at `LIFT_AT` (world −4, 0). The car and ladder therefore sit at the same world spot on every deck:

| Piece | Rows | World position |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Lift car `@` | 2 × 3 | x −5…−3, z −2…1 |
| Ladder row `Z` | the row below the car | z 1…2 |

The trunks run up behind the car's back wall. A deck with room above the car (Deck 4) shows them behind glass in a `Y` row there.


**Changing decks.** `goToDeck(n, at)`:

1. Hides the crew and unloads the current deck.
2. Builds deck `n` and indexes its room tiles.
3. Places you at world point `at` if that tile is free (a ride passes where you stood in the car), otherwise at the nearest open tile to the lift.
4. Activates the crew whose `deck` is `n`.
5. Refreshes what taps can hit and updates the HUD.


## Cutaway and sky (0.5.8)

- **Hull pieces.** Each room's hull run is split again wherever the outline turns to face a new way (bow, stern, either side). Each piece in `HULL_RUNS` has `face`, its outward axis. A piece is low when `face · camera > 0`, which on screen means the lower and right walls are low and the upper and left walls stay up, in every Walls mode. Hull edges follow the nearest piece facing their way.
- **R() is still drawn once per window span**, even when a span crosses pieces. Splitting must never change the count, or every later texture shifts.
- **Walls modes.** `wallsMode` is `'auto'`, `'down'` or `'up'` (`wallsLow` is true in `'down'`). Up keeps inner walls and solid spine blocks at full height; tall furniture still fades.
- **Sky.** `SKY` is a screen quad just inside the far plane, with additive stars, so it only shows where nothing else drew. It has its own random sequence, lives in the scene (not a deck), and costs one draw call. Two layers drift aft on screen at different speeds, with slight parallax as the camera moves. Night brightens it (`uK`). With reduced motion it drifts at a quarter speed.
- **Windows** (0.5.9) have nothing behind the glass, so you see the sky through them. `nebulaTex` and `starTex` are still drawn at load, and `R()` is still called once per window span, to keep the random sequence.

## Curved inner walls (0.6.4)

- **A deck lists them in `curves`.** Each has an `arc`, `[cx, cz, r, a0, a1]` in tiles and degrees (0 points to the bow, 90 to starboard), or `pts`, `[[x, z], ...]` in tiles, for a smooth Catmull-Rom line. `out` (1 or −1) names the customers' or front side; `it` makes it tappable at its middle.
- **Kinds:**
    - `wall`: full height, wall texture, end posts. It joins `RUNS` (with `curve` set) and drops with the cutaway like an inner wall. Tested, not used yet.
    - `glass`: a planted base 0.8 m high with a glass screen and a brass rail to 2.1 m.
    - `bar`: a counter 1.1 m high with a wooden top overhanging the `out` side, a brass foot rail and an amber under-glow.
    - `planter`: a low planted wall 0.7 m high.
- **Built by `buildCurves(def)`,** after `dress`, into `deckRoot`. `curveStrip(P, y0, y1, o0, o1, mats)` makes any strip along a line, like `hullStrip` does for the hull.
- **Walking:** tiles whose centres are within the curve's half-width + 0.3 of the line are added to `BLOCKED`, and `collide` pushes walkers off each segment in `CURVE_SEGS`.
- **Not yet:** curved walls don't replace grid edges, so a room's shape is still its tiles. Rounding a room's corner (the promenade's corners at the plaza, say) would also need the floor painter to repaint the sliver of tile left outside the curve with the neighbouring floor, and the two grid edges to stop drawing while still blocking. That's the next step when we want it, and the bridge's command ring will want it.
- **Materials:** `curveWallMat` (the wall texture, repeating along the curve) and `CURVE_MAT`. No `R()`.

- **New kit:** `raisedBed`, `growRack`, `trellis`, `citrusTree`, `bush`, `pond`, `bigTree`, `groundCover`, `stepStone`, `moss`, `gardenLamp`, `pollen`, `medBed`, `medDesk`, `medCabinet`, `examBed`, `treadmill`, `rower`, `weightRack`, `weightBench`, `gymMats`, `shopShelf`, `drinksFridge`, `shopDisplay`, `callBooth`; wall kit `herbWall`, `gymMirror`, `clocks`, `msgBoard`. Materials `GD`, `MED`, `GYM`, `SHOP`, and `LEAF_GEO` with `leaf()` for low-poly foliage. None call `R()` at load.

## The promenade (0.6.8)

- **Planter islands** (`planterIsland`, 2.48 × 1.34, `snug`) sit on rows 10–11 at cols 28–30 (lemon tree, `fruit`) and 34–36 (marigolds). Trees: a 1.9 m trunk with the crown at about 2.5 m (0.7.0). No lamp posts (taken out at review, 0.6.9). Rows 9 and 12 stay clear as lanes, and so does every column with a door: the mess doors (25, 26, 31, 32), gym (27), store (33), galley back door (38) and booths (38). Each island has its own fade group (`'=:a'`, `'=:b'`), so only the one you're behind fades.
- **Route kiosk** (`routeKiosk`) at col 24, rows 10–11 (blocked with `block`), screens on both faces. Its canvas stars come from a fixed hash, not `R()`.
- **Garden-door end:** a bench on each wall and four small potted plants (the `plant` kit, not `bush`, which draws from `R()` and would reshuffle the garden).
- **Materials** in `PROM`. Nothing calls `R()`, so Deck 3's garden, lounge and infirmary are pixel-identical to 0.6.7, and Deck 4 is ALL IDENTICAL.
