## Snug collision (0.6.9)

- `place(..., { snug: true })` still adds the piece's tiles to `BLOCKED`, so paths and the crew brain avoid them as before. It also records the piece's real footprint in `SNUG` (tile → boxes), and `collide` pushes walkers off those boxes instead of the whole tile. You can walk right up to a bench against a wall.
- Used on the lobby benches on Decks 3 and 4, the garden-door benches and the planter islands. Worth using for any thin piece against a wall.
- 0.6.9 against 0.6.8 on Deck 4: only 21 of the 3000 collision probes move, all beside the two lobby benches; everything else is ALL IDENTICAL.

## The music room (0.5.9)

- Room `n` on Deck 4, no longer a hall. Kit: `MUS` materials, `pianoSlab`, and `KIT` pieces `grandPiano`, `pianoBench`, `drumKit`, `guitarStand`, `amp`, `micStand`, `stageLight`, `beanbag` and `recordCabinet`, plus `WALL_KIT` pieces `acoustic` and `instrumentHooks`. None of them call `R()`.
- The stage is an extruded slab following the bow curve, an inset in from the hull. Its tiles are added to `BLOCKED`, and stage pieces are raised by the stage height after `place`.
- One stage light is a real lamp, within `LAMP_SLOTS`. The Captain's haunts already favour `n`, so she visits.
- 0.5.9 against 0.5.8: the random seed matches. Paths, the Captain's late goals, and screenshots showing windows or the bow differ as expected. Every room tile and every music-room item can be reached; the 96 unreachable items are locked cabins, the same as 0.5.8.

## The lift (section 11)

- **The car** is room `@` on each deck's map (2 × 3, mid-spine, big enough for a hover cart). `buildDeck` fits every car with a panel and handrail on its north wall and a light strip on its south wall.
- **Lift doors sink into the floor** (0.6.9): `leaf.position.y = 1.1 - open * 2.3`, hidden once fully open, so they never stick out past the frame. Other doors still slide.
- **Lift doors** are one wide door per side: lift edges in a line (three per side now) share a single pair of leaves, built on the first edge, and the corners between them have no posts (`liftJoint`). A door object carries its own `cx`, `cz` and `span`.
- **Lift doors** are walkable, so you can walk straight through the car. Crew sometimes cut through it.
- **Opening the deck list:**
    - Tap a lift door or the car panel (an item with `lift: true`) and `enterLift()` walks you to `panelTile()` (the car's back row, east column), faces you to the panel, then opens the deck list. If someone's standing there, you go to the nearest free car tile instead.
- **Pressing:** choosing a deck calls `pressPanel(n)`. You walk back to the panel if you'd moved, then `player.press` plays a 0.8 s reach with the right hand (`g.reach`, blended in `animateRig`). At the top of the reach `startRide(n)` fires. Moving cancels the press.
    - From out in the lobby the panel is usually hidden by the cutaway, so tap the doors.
- **The panel** lists Decks 1–6, with unbuilt decks greyed and your deck marked. Escape, "Stay here" or a tap outside closes it.
- **A ride** (`RIDE`, about 1.8 s, on the game clock):
    1. The doors close.
    2. The view fades to black.
    3. `goToDeck(n, at)` runs behind the black, keeping your facing.
    4. The view fades in and the doors open.
    5. The doors go back to proximity behaviour, and the arrival toast shows.
- **Taps and pointer input are ignored during a ride.**
- **`LAMP_SLOTS = 2`.** Each deck is padded with dark point lights under the floor up to this count, so changing decks never recompiles shaders. Raise it if a deck needs more real lamps.


## The spine

- **Deck 4:** rows 6–7 are `Y` (Spine trunks) and rows 11–13 are `Z` (Spine ladder). They're glassed onto both lobbies and the corridors. A hatch at `h,20,14` leads down the ladder (tier 2). The lift sign (row 7) and call panel (row 11) stay on solid wall either side of the lift doors.
- **Deck 5:** one `Z` row (9). The car's back wall faces the Works bay; there's no `Y` row.
- **`spineTrunks` and `spineLadder`** each fill exactly one row beside the car (on Deck 4 the trunks sit in row 7, just above it), placed with their back to the car (`'n'` for both). That's why they line up. Decks with more spine room add their own fittings around them, as Deck 4 does with `trunkJunction` and `harnessRack`.
- **Tap text** is shared from `SPINE_IT`.
- **`#` rooms are still solid spine** wherever a deck has spine beyond those rows.


## Kit added in 0.5.x (4b)

- **`WM`, the Works materials:** steel, paint, screens, hazard (texture), hull, cockpit, engine, nav lights, door steel, light strip, tool board and more. They're made once at load. Repeated pieces share these so merged meshes stay few; for example, all work lights share `WM.lightStrip`.
- **`KIT` additions:**
    - Works complex: `workbench`, `lathe`, `fabricator`, `weldBench`, `counter`, `aisleSign`, `tarpHeap`.
    - Shuttle bay: `shuttle`, `bayDoors`, `doorGear`, `bigFan`, `boothConsole`.
    - Spine: `spineTrunks`, `spineLadder`, `trunkJunction`, `harnessRack`.
- **`WALL_KIT` additions:** `workLight`, `jobBoard` (static cards in `o.cards` for now), `toolWall`, `carPanel`, `handrail`, `deckDirectory`.
- **`gearGeo(r, teeth, depth, holes)`** makes an extruded gear. Teeth of the same pitch mesh at any size (big gear 0.9/24 teeth drives small 0.45/12 at twice the speed).
- **Turning parts.** Push `{ o, ax, w }` onto `SPINS` and the loop turns `o.rotation[ax]` by `w` radians per second on the game clock. Mark spinning meshes with `noMergeAll(o)` so merging leaves them alone. `SPINS` is reset with each deck.
- **Fade groups.** `place(..., { fadeGroup: 'r:Lights' })` puts tall pieces in their own fade group instead of the whole room's. Storage aisles use it.
- **Pieces against the hull that must lower with the cutaway** go in `WALL_DECOR` with their hull edge, so they hide when the hull run is cut. The bay doors do this with `v,1,6`. Don't do it for things you watch through glass (the door gear): its hull run wraps round to the near side and would hide it just as you walk up.
- **`placeOnEdge(kind, key, toward, { it })`** now takes tap text, like `placeWall`.
- **Taps:**
    - `item.see` shows the text without walking, for things behind glass.
    - `item.lift` opens the lift.
    - Sealed doors and lift doors use `reachableSide(e)` and `doorSides(e)`.


## Crew

- **Chef Brennan and Rina are `deck: 3`** (0.6.7). Rina keeps her bunk on Deck 4 in the story, but has no `home` until shifts move people between decks. 0.6.7 against 0.6.6: Deck 4's crew paths and crew screenshots differ as expected; the crew-hidden shot differs only by a door still closing behind crew who had just walked past.
- **Pell is `deck: 5`** with Works haunts and favourite spots (benches, job board, tea corner, lathe, storage counter). The game still starts with you on Deck 4, where seven crew wander.
- **The room brain adds `j`, `=` and `+` weights on every deck.** On Deck 5 there's no `j`, which is harmless (that pick just fails and retries). Room letters are per-deck, so `home` and `haunts` only make sense for the walker's own deck.


## Crowd rendering (0.20.x)

Section 8b, by the performance engineer (Pillar 1, workstream 5). The player and close crew keep full `makeCharacter` rigs (16–17 meshes, about 15,000 triangles and 32 draw calls each with shadows). Everyone else goes through `crowd`.

**How it works.**

- `crowd.add(spec)` calls `makeCharacter(spec, finish)` with a `finish` that keeps the queued parts instead of baking meshes. The rig's joints stay ordinary Groups, kept out of the scene, and `animateRig` drives them exactly as it drives a full rig.
- Each person's parts are rebuilt at lower detail (`CROWD_DETAIL`: spheres, caps, cylinders and rings with fewer segments; boxes as they are) and packed once, in their joints' own space, at two levels: near (about 4,100 triangles) and far (about 2,100).
- Up to `CROWD_PER` people (12) share one `SkinnedMesh`. Their joints are its bones (17 slots a person); every vertex follows one bone, and the bone inverses are identity, so a bone's matrix is just the joint's world matrix. One material, `CROWD_MAT` (as `CHAR_MAT`, with `skinning: true`, which r128 still needs). So 40 people are 4 meshes: at most 8 draw calls with the shadow pass.
- A group's geometry is rebuilt only when someone joins, leaves or changes detail. Its bounding sphere is refitted every frame round its members, so a group off screen isn't drawn.
- Far detail: anyone more than `CROWD_FAR` (9) tiles from the camera's target, or everyone when zoomed out past `CROWD_FAR_ZOOM` (0.75), with half a tile of hysteresis.
- Coarse detail (0.27.8), only in a big crowd (more than `CROWD_MANY`, 70, people on the deck): anyone further than `CROWD_FAR2` (16) tiles, or everyone zoomed out, uses `CROWD_DETAIL[2]` (about 1,100 triangles; parts under `min`, 3 cm, left out). Each person keeps their parts (`jparts`) so `packOf(2)` can pack it the first time it's needed. Note a normal day already has up to 67 crew on Deck 3, so a ship-wide event pushing a deck past 70 turns this on by design.
- Without float vertex textures (rare on phones now), `CROWD_PER` shrinks so a group's bones fit the shader's uniforms.
- Nothing in the crowd calls `R()`: each person has their own random stream (`animateRig`'s new `rnd` argument), so adding crowd never shifts anything else. `CROWD_MAT` and the empty geometry are made on the first `add`, so a game with no crowd has none of it.

**Interface** (for the sim engineer):

- `const h = crowd.add(spec)`: `spec` is the roster's `looks` plus `dept` and `rank` (for the uniform).
- `h.set(pos, face, pose, walkPhase)`: `pos` is a world `{x, y, z}`; for `sit` and `lie`, `y` is the seat or mattress top (0 means a usual chair, 0.475, or bunk, 0.5). `face` is an angle like `Walker.face` (0 looks toward +z); for `lie` it's the way from head to feet. `pose` is `walk`, `stand`, `sit`, `lie` or `work`; changes blend over about half a second, except the first `set`, which snaps. `walkPhase` (gait cycles) is optional: leave it out and the gait follows how far `pos` moves each frame.
- `crowd.remove(h)`, `crowd.clear()`, `crowd.near(x, z, r)` (who's at a spot, for taps; the meshes themselves are never hit by the tap raycast), `crowd.visible`, `crowd.stats()`.
- `crowd.update(dt, t)` runs once a frame from the main loop, after the walkers.
- Sitting puts the hips just above the seat and the feet on the floor by leg IK, forearms on the table, a fork to the mouth now and then. Working: hands busy at bench height, head down. Lying: on the back, arms by the sides, centred on `pos`.
- Crowd people don't block walking. Who stands where is the sim's job (workstream 6).

**The demo.** `?crowd=40` in the address (any number up to 76) starts you on Deck 3 by the lift at 18:30 and puts that many of the real crew (from `ROSTER`, day shift first, not Rina or Brennan) on Deck 3's own places: the servery queue (stand), the grab shelf, two galley stations (work), three people walking the mess hall's aisles, the 44 mess seats, then the 22 benches. A line under the clock shows the crowd, draw calls and frame time. They leave when you leave the deck and come back when you return; the Crew button hides them. `?crowd` works as `?nosave`, so the demo never touches a save. Without the flag, `CROWD_DEMO` is `null` and nothing of the demo exists.

**Numbers** (headless, SwiftShader; frame times there aren't meaningful, so the demo's line is the one to read on a phone):

| Deck 3 | Draw calls | Triangles drawn (both passes) | Crowd JS a frame |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| By the lift, no crowd | 326 | 285,000 | |
| By the lift, 40 crowd | 329 | 364,000 | about 2 ms |
| In the mess hall, no crowd | 242 | 240,000 | |
| In the mess hall, 40 crowd | 246 | 401,000 | about 2 ms |

With the real crew and places (0.20.1, on 0.23.0):

| Deck 3 | Draw calls | Triangles drawn (both passes) | Crowd JS a frame |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| By the lift, no crowd | 326 | 285,000 | |
| By the lift, 40 | 328 | 345,000 | about 1.2 ms |
| By the lift, 70 | 331 | 443,000 | about 1.8 ms |
| In the mess hall, no crowd | 242 | 240,000 | |
| In the mess hall, 40 | 246 | 391,000 | about 0.8 ms |
| In the mess hall, 70 | 248 | 486,000 | about 0.8 ms |

Each group of 12 adds at most two draw calls, so the count isn't the limit; triangles are. Seat and bed heights for the sim: see the rule sent to the sim engineer (bunks: odd berths 0.5, even 1.6; mess seats 0.475; benches 0.48; lounge seats vary, so places may carry a `y`).

For comparison, 40 full rigs would add about 1,300 draw calls and 600,000 triangles. Memory is flat over Deck 3 and 4 round trips with the demo on (the four groups' bone textures stay, like kit).

**Testing a crowd turn:** cadet_test and deck_check as usual (the crowd is empty in both). For the demo, open the build with `?crowd=40`; the Game class in cadet_test.py loads `file://` paths, so a script that adds the query to the `goto` is enough.

## Activities (0.27.x)

Section 8b, Pillar 2 workstream A2, by the performance engineer.

**Poses.** `handle.set(pos, face, pose)` takes a base (`walk`, `stand`, `sit`, `lie`, `work`), an activity, or both as `'base:activity'`:

- **Activities:** `run`, `lift`, `call`, `browse`, `pay`, `play`, `water`, `cook`, `wash`, `fold`, `drink`, `read`, `talk`.
- **An activity alone** stands. The exception: `call`, `read`, `drink`, `talk`, `play` and `lift` sit when `pos.y` is 0.3 or more (y is then the seat).
- **Seated and lying forms** are written out: `'sit:call'` in a booth, `'sit:play'` at the piano (keys; standing `play` is a guitar), `'lie:lift'` on the weight bench (a press), `'sit:lift'` on the rower.
- **Standing on something:** a standing pose with `pos.y` under 0.3 stands on a floor that high (a stage, the treadmill's belt, 0.16).
- **Unknown poses** stand.
- **Held things**, as a `+modifier` on any standing pose: `+cart` (pushing an empty hover cart), `+cartfull` (loaded), `+carry` (a crate of parts at the chest). Since 0.27.7 also `+tray` (both hands at the waist), `+toolbox` (right hand, hanging) and `+datapad` (left hand, reading). A HOLDS entry with `one` holds in that arm only (1 left, -1 right), `bone` puts the prop on that hand (R, L) instead of the body, `hx` sets the wrist and `look` bends the head.
- **Seats** (0.27.11): the sit pose puts the hips at the spot, the feet 0.3 forward on the floor; on a seat over 0.62 the feet rest at seat height minus 0.5 (a stool's ring). `?seats` shows every seat place on the deck with a stand-in and its id. A place's seat height is its `y` (section 10's `placeSpot`), else SEAT_Y by type; most mismatches are fixed there.
- **Crouch** (0.27.7): `crouch` is a base pose like `walk` (the gait follows the movement), with the hips lowered, a forward lean, arms forward and the head up. For crawlway tiles: the player's Walker calls `animateActivity(this, 'crouch', ...)`; crew could use it too. It can carry a held thing (`'crouch+toolbox'`). `'walk+cart'`, `'stand+cartfull'`, `'work+cart'` (loading: the work arms win). The prop rides the body bone, counter-scaled so it's the same size for everyone; full rigs get a small mesh on the root.
- **`row`** (the rower) always sits. A seated `gym` place with no `anim` rows.
- **`CROWD_ANIMS`** is the set of activities drawn. The schedules can check it before sending one.
- **`poseOf(place)`** gives a place's pose from `anim`, or from its type (the plan's table: `gym` lift, `callhome` call, `shop` browse, `music` play, `garden` water, `laundry` fold, `work:galley` cook), on its base pose. By type alone, seated places stay plain `sit` except booths (`sit:call`) and the gym (`sit:lift`, `lie:lift` on the bench); a place with its own `anim` (the piano bench: `anim: 'play'`) gets it seated too. Section 10's `placeSpot` uses it, so crew at those places now do the activity.

**Cost (0.27.0 against 0.25.2):** draw calls identical on every deck in day_check (peaks 370, 373, 415, 412, 377, 321). Props add a few hundred triangles (Deck 3 at 18:20: 475,711 against 475,163, with 67 crew). cadet_test ALL IDENTICAL against 0.25.2, also with the `placeSpot` hook reverted (the refactor alone changes nothing).

**How it's drawn.**

- `animateActivity(o, pose, dt, t, sk, y)` does a rig's whole frame: the gait (`animateRig`), the base pose's overlay (sit, work, lie, as before), the activity's overlay, and props. It works on a `CrowdPerson`, a `FullPerson` (which borrows `CrowdPerson.prototype.update`) or a `Walker`.
- Each activity is an entry in `ACTS`: the props it holds, and a function that moves the arms, head and torso with a blend weight. Blends take about half a second, like the bases.
- `run` drives the gait in place (the legs cycle at a jog with no movement).
- **Props** are made from the character kit's primitives, on first use (`PROP_KIT`, no `R()`, colours through `col()`).
  - **On a crowd person** they ride three prop bones (right hand, left hand, chest: `CROWD_BONES` is now 20, joints 0–16, props 17–19), inside the group's skinned mesh. Picking one up rebuilds that group's geometry, so props cost no draw calls.
  - **On a full rig or Walker** each prop is a small mesh on the hand or chest (`rigProps`), its own copy of the geometry so the rig can dispose it.
  - Props appear once the activity is more than halfway blended in.

**The lineup.** `?poses` in the address shows every pose and activity on Deck 3's promenade, labelled (`POSE_DEMO`, like `CROWD_DEMO`).

**For the player (A3).** In `Walker.update`, replace

    animateRig(this.rig, g, dt, t + g.t0, Math.min(1, this.vel / this.maxV));

with

    animateActivity(this, this.act || 'stand', dt, t + g.t0, Math.min(1, this.vel / this.maxV));

- Set `player.act` to an activity (`'run'`, `'lift'`, `'call'`, `'browse'`, `'pay'`, or any other) while the player does it, and back to `null` after.
- With `act` null this is exactly `animateRig` (same `R()` draws, no props), so nothing changes until an activity is set.
- Standing on the treadmill: raise `player.obj.position.y` to the belt (0.16) yourself; the Walker's root isn't moved by `animateActivity`.


## Machine looks (0.27.9, the performance engineer)
- `machineFx.tick` runs at the top of `crowd.update`. When the deck changes it lists the deck's machines (`machineList()`, not stand-ins) and works out each effect's spot; twice a second it asks `machineLook(id)` for `{ state, blink, dim, drip, flicker }` (0..1 or true).
- Effects: `blink`, a red lamp blinking faster the worse it is; `drip`, drops falling from 0.9 m, more often the worse it is; `dim`, a dark see-through plate over the screen (only where `look.screen` is given); `flicker`, a warm glow that stutters (a hash of the time, never R()).
- Spots: default lamp on the machine's front at 1.2 m (the front is the tile edge toward `panel`, or near the back wall when `panel` is its own tile); drips from 0.9 m just in front; glow at 2.1 m. An architect overrides with `look` on the machine entry: `[dx, y, dz]`, dx tiles to the right as you face the front, dz tiles out of the front from the tile centre, y metres; `screen` adds w and h in metres.
- Cost: four `InstancedMesh`es (64 each), made the first time something shows, hidden while empty. `?looks` forces everything on (on Deck 6: lamps 1-4, about 5 drops and 2 glows at a time).
- Wilting: on Deck 3 only, `GD.leaves` lerp toward `#a08a3c` (45% for poor, 100% for failing, times 0.65) and go back when you leave or it's mended. These materials are also the promenade planters', which share the water.
- Lamps (0.27.10): `lamps(t)` runs in the main loop right after `applyLight` (which resets the lamps every frame); while any of the deck's `lights` machines flickers, lamps 0, 3, 6, ... (and 1, 4, 7, ... at full flicker) drop to 15% at moments picked by a hash of the time.
- Shower spray (0.27.10): the deck's `shower` places; for each one `placeBusy`, 12 streaks (3-sided, r .012, .28 long, see-through) fall from 1.9 m round the spot. Weak water: `shipSystems()` water or heat under 50 gives 7 streaks and cuts out 45% of the time; under 25, 4 streaks and 60%. Cabin wash pods are closed boxes, so the spray mostly shows in the open stalls of the washrooms.

## Rules that keep it working

Phones:

- Never let two surfaces share a plane. Overlap solids, or keep faces at least 0.005 apart. The job board's screen sat 0.005 behind its frame's face and vanished; check plane depth against box faces.
- Keep the canvas opaque, keep the camera depth range tight, and snap the camera to whole pixels.
- Avoid per-pixel noise on large surfaces.
- Pass every colour through `col()`.
- 1 unit = 1 tile.
- Only one deck is in memory at a time.

Determinism:

- Shared kit made at load must not call `R()`. It would shift every later kit texture.
- New floor types on an existing deck must not draw from the floor loop's `R()` (see `lift` and `spine` in the floor painter). Otherwise every later tile's grain changes.
- New kit used on Deck 4 should avoid `R()` too.
- Regressions must come out pixel-identical. When they don't, find out why before moving on.

Shared work (step 2, from the roadmap's lessons of 2026-10-19):

- **The crew's planner never reads records built while the game runs.** A day's plan must come out the same whenever it's made, so it may read only what is fixed for that day (the roster, the day number, seeded rolls, records settled at a known hour, like the rec deck's at 22:00 the evening before). Never `MACH_HIST`, a machine's live state, who's on the deck now or anything else filled in as you play. (The MACH_HIST bug: a plan's fallback read the live history, so the same day planned at two moments differed; machine_check and day_check disagreed between runs. Fixed in 0.64.1 by `recPlanShut` / `recSettled`.)
- **Checks never assume an empty deck or a free time slot.** Before a check places the player, books a seat or starts an event at a time, it looks for a free one (or clears it) and says which it used. A new tradition, a new place or a crew change must not break an unrelated check. (Two check fixes on 2026-10-19 were checks that assumed a slot nobody used; and interactions_check's floor tap that hit a crew member.)
- **New features are gated until they're switched on.** A new feature sits behind one named switch (like `REC_DECK`), and with the switch off the game is identical: cadet_test ALL IDENTICAL against the build before it, the same days in day_check. Texts that only make sense with the feature on (like "Seven decks") are gated too. Switching it on is its own reviewed step.
- **One owner per function and table** (`plans/owners.md`). No new top-level name that already exists anywhere in the file (names_check fails it); add to a shared table only through its owner's registry or slot.
- **New code goes in your seat's labelled slot** (between `// ---- slot: <seat> (section N) ... ----` and `// ---- end of slot: <seat> ----`; new lines go just above the end line; the list is in `plans/map.md`), not at the end of a neighbour's section, so two seats never add lines at the same spot.

Extrude geometry:

- `ExtrudeGeometry` bevels widen a shape by `bevelThickness` on each extruded face. Put decals and windows outside that.


## Testing

**The quick check before every push** (step 2): `bash check_fast.sh` (about 5 minutes on 4 cores; `CADET_WORK` must hold `three/` and `acorn/`: `cd $CADET_WORK && mkdir -p acorn && cd acorn && npm pack acorn@8.12.1 && tar xzf acorn-8.12.1.tgz`). It runs, in order, stopping early if a fast step fails: `content_gen.py --check` (the words are baked), `node names_check.js` (the script parses as the browser reads it; no name, shared-table key or registration made twice, gated blocks included; no "Deck 7" in text), then two lanes side by side, each in its own CADET_WORK: deck_check, machine_check, leave_check | device_check. A step passes only if it exits 0 **and** prints its OK line. Prints `QUICK CHECK OK in N s` or `QUICK CHECK FAILED` with the failing lines and the log path. The full checks below are unchanged and still run before every release. **GitHub runs the same quick check by itself** (`.github/workflows/quick-check.yml`, on since 2026-10-20, the user's call) on every push to a claude/ branch or main that touches the game, the checks or the words: a tick or a cross on the commit, logs under the run's "check-logs". A newer push to the same branch cancels the older run.

**Every check exits 1 when it fails** (step 2): deck, machine, day, food, path, perf, disc, arc, social and cadet_test used to print FAILED and exit 0, so a script or GitHub saw a pass. Their printed lines are unchanged.

**names_check.js** (step 2, from the split rehearsal): `node names_check.js [GAME.html]`, 1 second. On a FAIL it names the section and game line of both copies. Repeats that are meant are listed in its `MEANT` set with the reason (section 19's 'crew' app shell, replaced by section 20's; `SHIP.tiles` reloaded from the save); a table key declared `null` and filled once later by its owner is allowed.

**Finding your way:** `python3 tools/section.py` (list), `tools/section.py 19` (print a section), `--where LINE`, `--diff A B` (sections and owners a change touches); `node tools/shared.js` (functions and tables used outside their section; `--md` makes the list in `plans/owners.md`).

**mech_check parts share the game** (build master 8, 0.63.5): each part's `reset` changes `SHIP.works`, the jobs and the machine clock, and the next part (or the next run of the same part) starts from what it left. TPARTS and M12 were fixed for this in 0.63.5. M8, M13, M14, M15, FC and FF still run machinesNew/machinesNow before they rebuild the works, so their machine catch-up reads the last run's works; they pass today, but restoring the works first (as TPARTS now does) changes m8's results, so their expectations would need redoing. If one of them starts failing only on its first run, this is why.

**Parallel runs need their own CADET_WORK** (reviewer R6's find, 0.63.4). Every check copies the build into `$CADET_WORK/test/` (default `/tmp/cadet-work/test/`): cadet_test as `_run_<file name>`, career_check as `_career_save.html`, mech_check as `_mech_save.html`, player_check as `_save_budget.html` / `_save_check.html`. Two checks running at once in one container on different builds can load each other's copy and pass falsely. Give each run its own: `export CADET_WORK=/tmp/work-<tag>; mkdir -p $CADET_WORK/test; ln -sfn /tmp/cadet-work/three $CADET_WORK/three`.

**cadet_test.py hides the player's +N need notes** (`#needFx`) and the device button's glow (`#devBtn` box-shadow) in screenshots, like toasts (the lead's ask, 2026-10-17: before 0.28.23 they faded on real-time timers, so a note like the debug Day skip's "+69 Energy" was in g_nocrew on some runs and not others). Three runs of the same build: identical apart from i_tap1's known 1-level renderer noise.

**cadet_test.py** now clicks Walls until it's back to Auto after the walls shot, so it works with builds before and after 0.5.8. It compares two builds on Deck 4 (deck data, paths, collisions, crew, taps, screenshots).

- **Setup in a fresh container:**

  ```
  pip install playwright
  mkdir -p /tmp/cadet-work/three && cd /tmp/cadet-work/three
  npm pack three@0.128.0 && tar xzf three-0.128.0.tgz
  ```

  `CADET_WORK` (default `/tmp/cadet-work`) sets where three.js and the test copies live. `CADET_CHROME` points at a Chromium binary when Playwright's own browser isn't installed (in Claude Code cloud sessions, `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`).

- **Run:** `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 cadet_test.py OLD.html NEW.html` from a scratch folder (screenshots land in the current folder). It takes about a minute. The old build comes from git, for example `git show HEAD~1:starship-cadet.html > old.html`.
- **Screenshot noise (0.7.0).** In cloud containers the software renderer sometimes rounds a few dozen pixels one colour level differently between runs, even comparing a build with itself. The test treats a difference of at most 1 as noise and says so ("apart from renderer noise"); anything bigger fails. It also keeps toasts out of screenshots (they fade on real-time timers) and turns off CSS transitions; toast text is still compared. Since 0.26.23 it hides the needs' floating notes (`.nf`, "+69 Energy") the same way: they're removed on a real-time timer, so one build's shot could catch one and the other's not (seen comparing 0.26.22 and 0.26.23, flipping between runs).
- **When crew changes are expected,** compare the crew-hidden shot, or make a test copy with the crew change reverted and expect ALL IDENTICAL. That's how Pell's move was proven.

Deck 5 and the lift were tested with small ad-hoc scripts that reuse cadet_test.py's `Game` class:

```
exec(open('cadet_test.py').read().split("a, shots = run(")[0])
```

They checked:

- Every item's approach tile and every room tile can be reached from the lift lobby (skip `lift` and `see` items).
- Rides down and back: the deck, the tile you're on, your facing, the fade and the door states.
- Taps on sealed doors, hatches and glass-backed items.
- Draw calls and `LAMPS.length`.
- `SPINS` angles advance.
- Memory across round trips: Deck 4 must come back to the same counts each time. The first Deck 5 visit uploads shared kit (hatch textures, sill and pane geometry), which then stays, like any kit.

**Screen-space tap tips:**

- Project a door's edge centre at y ≈ 0.08. The cutaway may have lowered the door, and a tap at head height goes past it.
- Things in locked rooms can be hit through a lowered door; that's the test spot, not a bug.

**day_check.py** (0.22.2): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 day_check.py BUILD.html [DAY]` fast-forwards one ship day with `?nosave&debug` (about 4 minutes) and checks the plan's rules: everyone sleeps 6+ hours in their own bed, eats twice and works their shift; no double-booked places, no gaps; the same plans twice; on every deck a whole day of crew logic with nobody stuck over 10 ship minutes, nobody in someone else's bed or place, nobody missing; draw calls peak under 450; Deck 4 run twice comes out identical. Run it with cadet_test and deck_check whenever crew, schedules, places or the roster change.

**talk_check.py** (the talk engineer, T2): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 talk_check.py BUILD.html` (about 10 seconds) steps the Talk a while pack's picker on paper over many days with every crew member and guest: the pack baked; no talk twice in a save, own talks only from their owner; the run-out order checked at every pick against what was ready (own in file order, then the pool, then small talk; guests never the pool); small talk 3-4 lines, "done" about one in four and never twice running; tier, `!who:`, `!thread:`, `rank:` and the arc gates; nothing unfilled; the same pick twice; a recent topic goes behind; the save (none before any talk, round trip, junk dropped, size, New game clears). Run it whenever section 22's pack code or content/talkpack changes.

**join_check.py** (0.70.0): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 join_check.py BUILD.html` checks tap to join (section 18, "Tap to join"). Run it with events_check whenever traditions, section 18's joining, the crew's escort walking or the goal pointer change.

**events_check.py** (0.34.0): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 events_check.py BUILD.html [DAYS]` steps four weeks of ship events and walks each kind through on its deck. See "Ship events (section 18)". Run it whenever events, schedules, places or the roster change.

**machine_check.py** (0.33.0): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 machine_check.py BUILD.html [DAYS]` runs the machines on paper for 14 ship days, with no repairs and with an ideal crew, and checks the wear is sensible and the same every way (about 15 seconds). See "Machines and wear". Run it with the others whenever machines, schedules or places change.

**device_check.py** (0.35.0): the device's apps and skin (section 19); see "Your device". Run it whenever section 19 or the apps' data change.

**puzzle_check.py** (0.55.0, seat H): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 puzzle_check.py BUILD.html` checks the head-scratchers (section 24k, plans/mechanics/case-engine.html). The device's chips block and its buttons fallback; the case engine with a stand-in kind (a sweep of cases through every band, the rate roll, a case in play, the save); about 2 minutes. With 24 core in the build it opens Fault notes as the Works app's tab (checked on a trial combine with C's 2ea7ab2: puzzle_check and device_check OK). Run it whenever section 24k, the chips block or the fault tables change.

**interactions_check.py** (0.37.0): the player's extra interactions, the job board and the goal pointer (section 15b); see "Player interactions". Run it with player_check whenever sections 15, 15b or the places change. Its "tap at the stop" taps the floor at the stop, or within JOB_REACH of it, where no crew member is in front on screen (X3, after 0.71.6: Sol waiting by Deck 3's lift took the tap as a chat; a tap on a person talks to them by design).

**clothes_check.py** (0.42.40): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 clothes_check.py BUILD.html` (about 2 minutes) switches clothes on in a test game and taps through a week's clothes: the arrival's spare, yesterday's clothes, the hamper, Put down, the washers (busy, all busy, done), putting it away, the save mid-wash, new games. Run it whenever section 21b, the bunk rooms, the laundry, Put down or the needs change.

**leave_check.py** (0.61.0): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 leave_check.py BUILD.html` checks leave, missed shifts and being let go on paper (about a minute); see "Leave, missed shifts and being let go". Run it whenever the career section, reputation, `playerShiftOn` or the occasions change.

**career_check.py** (0.44.0, the Works career engineer): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 career_check.py BUILD.html` (about 3 minutes) checks section 24 core on paper: nothing opens with no mechanic in the build; a steady player (every row given a stand-in sub-block) gets each row on its day, at most one big and one small a workday, nothing on days off, each big training as ask, meeting, the teacher's job, yours, trained, tools given, "Relied on" by the end of Day 8; a very busy player not before Day 5; a lazy player gets every row later, none lost; bands, skill words, tools and wear, penalties; the save (worksClean, junk, a real reload mid-training, an old save as Tier 1, under 3 KB by Day 30); the steady run twice. Run it whenever section 24, jobs, `playerShiftOn`, the occasions or a teacher's rota change.

**mech_check.py** (0.44.0, frame by the Works career engineer; one part per mechanic by the mechanic engineers): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 mech_check.py BUILD.html [ID ...]`. For each ladder row whose sub-block is in the build: its training end to end, stage 0 once trained, the same twice; then the mechanic's own part (`PARTS[id]`). Rows not in the build are listed, not failed.

**perf_check.py** (the performance engineer, Pillar 4's phone-speed watchdog): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 perf_check.py NEW.html [OLD.html]` (about 5 minutes per build) measures the busy moments on phone views (portrait 390×844 at zoom 1.5 and 0.7, landscape 844×390 at 1.15): draw calls (budget 450), triangles, milliseconds of game code a frame (rendering skipped: SwiftShader can't say how fast a phone draws) and crew on the deck, then memory over three deck round trips. With OLD it marks regressions (calls up over 10% and 25, game code up over 30%, memory growing). Scenes are a list at the top (`SCENES`); add one when a tradition or moment lands. Baseline at 0.41.10: everything under 450; the busiest is the junior common room zoomed out on a portrait phone (428), then the bow lounge at quiz night (378); game code at most about 3 ms a frame (the mess at Sunday dinner, 68 people).

**path_check.py** (the performance engineer, 0.27.29): `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 path_check.py GAME.html [PAIRS]` (about 5 minutes) checks that `findPath` (the heap search) finds exactly the same paths as `findPathScan` (the old scan), tile for tile, including every failure. It runs PAIRS (default 3000) random searches on every deck, then compares every real call of a played Monday as it happens. Run it whenever section 7, `free`, `edgeOpen` or `edgeBlocks` change. Good is PATH CHECK OK.

**deck_check.py** (0.6.8) does that: `CADET_CHROME=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium python3 deck_check.py BUILD.html` visits every deck in `DECKS` and checks every room tile and every tappable item can be reached from the lift, lists the rooms nobody can get into yet (locked cabins, sealed rooms; not failures), flags any room you can only partly get into, lists the active crew and draw calls, and checks Deck 4's memory over round trips. Run it on every turn alongside cadet_test.py.


## Working method

- Edit the build in place and never rewrite the whole file. Make small exact-string edits, each matching once.
- Work a deck one turn at a time. Each turn ends with a tested build (your area's checks, then `bash check_fast.sh`), a row in `plans/build-table.md`, and a commit pushed to the repo. The build master sets the build number when merging (CLAUDE.md "How we work"). Git is the save point: earlier builds come back with `git show <commit>:starship-cadet.html`.
- When a deck's turns are all done, stop for review: a playable link, screenshots, a summary and the test results.
- Keep these notes current as you go, so any new session can pick up from them.


## Galley and food (section 16, the galley engineer, 0.29.x)

Pillar 2, workstream A4: the galley really cooks. Section 16 sits after section 14 (and after section 15, the player's needs, once that's in), before section 6. Nothing in it calls `R()`; every choice is `schedU(person.seed, day, k)`.

- **The rota (`galleyDay(person, day)`).** For galley staff (`dept: 'galley'`), else null: `{ role, wake, bedBy, blocks: [{ from, to, placeId, task }], meals: { breakfast, dinner } }`, in minutes of the day. `role` is `chef`, `early`, `late`, or null on a day off. `wake` is null when the usual wake is fine. `bedBy` is the latest bedtime tonight because tomorrow starts early (6 h 55 before tomorrow's wake); it's set on days off too. `meals` are the staff's own meal times. The schedules (section 14) ask for it through a hook (the sim engineer's); it never asks them, so there's no loop. The sim engineer's 0.26.2 reads `galleyDay` directly (wake, bedBy, each stint as a work segment at its place, the staff meals at the given times). The older `galleyShift` was removed in 0.29.4: 0.26.1 fell back to it, and the galley staff missed dinner.
    - **Who does what (`galleyRoles`).** The chef is Brennan (ROSTER `galley: 'chef'`, crew designer's 0.16.5), else her deputy (`galley: 'cook'`: varga-tariq, who works Fridays and Saturdays), else the first hand on duty by id. The hands (`galley: 'hand'`) on duty take early and late shifts by turns, late first, so every day has at least one of each. Fixed teams also work (`rota.team: 'early' | 'late'`, with `rota.cover: { for }` marking a deputy) if the roster ever sets them. Without any field, the chef is found by role ("Chef ..."). The galley's hours live only here, never in the roster's `rota.hours`.
    - **The shifts (`GALLEY_SHIFTS`).**
        - Chef: up about 04:30 (±5 minutes); cooks 04:50–08:00; eats 08:00–08:20; cleans down to 09:30; free 09:30–14:30; cooks 14:30–16:30; eats 16:30–16:50; cooks 16:50–20:30; tea on the stool 20:30–20:45.
        - Early hand: up about 05:05; bakes for the grab shelf 05:30–06:00; serves and washes up by turns 06:00–08:00; eats 08:00–08:20; washes up to 09:30; bakes to 10:30; preps to 13:00; cleans to 13:30.
        - Late hand: a normal morning; preps 13:00–14:30; cooks 14:30–16:30; eats 16:30–16:50; serves and washes up 16:50–20:30; washes up and cleans down to 21:15.
    - **Stations (`GALLEY_AT`).** The blocks are cut into 15–30 minute stints at different stations (the line swaps every half hour), so you see people move between the ranges (`3-galley-1..3`), the ovens (`-4`), the prep island (`-5`, `-6`), the dish pit (`-7`), the pass (`3-galley-pass`) and Brennan's stool (`-8`). No station is held by two people at once; when a job's stations are taken, the next job's are tried (`GALLEY_NEXT`).
- **The food (`SHIP.galley`).** `{ at, cold: { meals }, servery: { portions, dish, meal }, grab: { items }, dirty, inUse, pots, wait, back }`. `galleyAdvance(t)` steps it a ship minute at a time (`galleyStep`) up to absolute minute `t`. It's called every frame (`galleyTick`, a one-line hook in the main loop after `tickShip`) and on time skips (`SIM_ADVANCE`). With no state, or when time went backwards (New game), it starts fresh at midnight of the current day and works forward. Whatever deck you're on, the same sums run.
    - **Events (`galleyEvents(day)`).** Read from the plans: each person's first breakfast or dinner segment is when they take food (2 minutes into a `queue` segment, at once otherwise). `grab` at a `grab-shelf` takes a grab item; anything else takes a hot portion. A sit-down meal brings its plate back when the meal ends. From the rota, how many people cook, bake and wash each minute.
    - **Cooking.** A pot is 10 portions and 20 minutes of a cook's time; a baking tray for the grab shelf is 8 items and 15 minutes. From 70 minutes before a window opens, an idle cook starts a pot when the servery and pots hold less than the portions the plans still need for that meal (plus 3, for you), up to the hot line's 50. Bakers, and idle cooks, keep the grab shelf at what's still needed today plus 4, up to 16. Pots take their meals from the cold store when they start.
    - **Eating.** A hot portion needs a clean plate (110 aboard). Anyone who finds nothing waits (`S.wait`) and is served first when food arrives; they give up 30 minutes after the line closes.
    - **Closing.** At 08:30 and 20:45 what's left in the servery is wrapped for the grab shelf; anything over 16 is wasted.
    - **Washing up.** 3 plates a minute for each person at the dish pit.
    - **The cold store.** 300 meals, under two days' food (about 150 a day), topped up at 10:30 each day (B3 will bring it by hover cart from the Deck 5 hold instead).
    - **Menus (`galleyMenu(day)`, 0.29.11, answer 10).** A week's cycle: breakfasts by weekday (`MENU.breakfast`); dinners on Monday, Tuesday, Thursday and Saturday from `MENU.cycle` (9 dishes, moving on 4 a week); Wednesday is a crew member's favourite (a different person each week; their second choice, or the next favourite, if it's already on that week's menu); Friday is the treat (`MENU.treat`) with a pudding (`MENU.pudding`); Sunday is the crew's vote: everyone votes for their favourite (ROSTER `favourite` if the crew designer adds one, else one of `MENU.fav` from their seed), or their second choice if the favourite was on that week, about one in four doesn't vote, the most votes wins. Returns `{ breakfast, dinner, note, pudding, fav, votes }`. `servery.dish` names what's on. No dish name has a full stop (section 15 reads "Serving: <dish>.").
    - **Spoilage (0.29.11, answer 7; 0.29.14 reads `chillerAt`).** `S.spoil` is the share of the cold store lost an hour, read on the hour from section 17's `chillerAt(m).spoil` (0.33.17: 0 while the chiller works well, up to `CHILL_SPOIL` 0.05 an hour broken), else from `shipEffectsAt(3, m).spoil` (1 fine, 2 broken: `FOOD.spoilDay`, 25% a day). The food goes off bit by bit into `S.rot`; the 09:00 and 21:00 checks (`FOOD.binAt`) bin the whole meals in `S.rot` into `S.bin`, and the bin goes down to the recycler at 10:30 (`FOOD.binOut`). With the chiller broken all week at 0.05 an hour: about 260 meals a day binned, the cold store dips to about 30–70, and the urgent cart runs still keep it from running dry (the hold empties faster; that's section 17's shortage to report). Each binning sends a Mail note (`galleyMail`, through section 15's `mailSend` when it's there; never twice for the same minute and subject; only live, never in a report). Nothing spoils with the chiller as new.
    - **Busy windows (`galleyBusy(day)`, 0.29.12, answer 11).** Picked from the day number (`galleyU`, `schedU` hashed twice, because once is streaky from day to day), so a day is always the same. A **rush** after the 17:00 shift change on about half of weekday dinners (17:00–17:45): the line serves `FOOD.rushRate` (1) a minute, so a short queue forms (about 15–25 people wait a day, up to about 4 minutes). A **slow oven** about one day in five, at breakfast or dinner: hot pots take `FOOD.slowOven` (1.75) times as long from 30 minutes before the window to an hour after (waits up to about 20 minutes; 1.9 made people give up at breakfast). Waiters stand at the queue places (`galleyHold`), and `galleyText('servery')` says how many are waiting and why. Calm windows still never queue.
    - **Birthday cakes (0.29.12, answer 17).** `galleyBirthdays(day)`: ROSTER members whose `birthday` (a ship day of the year, 1–364, the crew designer's) is that day; none yet, so no cakes until it lands. Mail "Cake tonight" at 12:00 (live only); baked at 15:00 from 4 cold-store meals (`S.cake`); out on the servery's tray slide from 19:30 (a cake and 5 candles with flames: 2 draw calls, only then); gone at 21:30. The menu board and `galleyText` name whose birthday it is. Birthdays are in since the crew designer's 0.16.6 (all 74 crew, on different days; the first is Tamsin Haddad's on Day 4); yours (`ROSTER_PLAYER.birthday`, Day 50) gets a cake too, worded for you ("It's your birthday", 0.29.13).
    - **Section 18's events (0.29.15, agreed with the events engineer).** One cake a day, the galley's: `galleyCake(day)` -> `{ who, name, gather }` is a birthday (above), else any `eventsOn(day)` entry with `cake: true` (Founding Day, once section 18 flags it). `gather` is when the event's gathering starts (the birthday gathering's `from`, 20:15), so the noon Mail says "The cake's out on the servery from 19:30, and everyone's in the mess at 20:15." Section 18 draws its own table cake only when `SHIP.galley.cake.out` isn't set. On a harvest morning (`eventsOn(day)` has id `harvest`) the dinner gets "with fresh garden salad" in its note (`galleyMenu(day).salad`). All guarded with typeof.
    - **Ship-wide events (Pillar 3, C7, 0.29.18).** Section 18's `eventMod('galley', key)` is read on the hour into `S.ev` (`galleyEvRead`); null, or no section 18, changes nothing. Keys: `menu` (a dish id in `GALLEY_EV_DISH`, e.g. `stormStew` → "storm stew", or a dish name: both meals, the servery text and the menu board); `cook` (hot pots take this many times as long; storm stew .75); `grab` (the grab shelf stocked for this many times the usual, up to its 16); `extra` (more portions at each meal, for guests); `batch` (meals for a dinner away from the mess, a number or `{ meals, label }`, taken from the cold store at 15:00; the servery's text says "Brennan is also cooking for <label>"); `trays` (trays for the bridge: cooked with the dinner, plated at 18:00 (`FOOD.trayAt`) and sent by section 17's `cartTrays({ trays, at, to: 'bridge' })` when it's there). Section 18's `eventMod(kind, key, t)` (0.34.7) answers for ship minute t, so the galley passes the minute and replays see events too (0.29.19); an `eventMod` without the third argument would be read live only.
    - **Your cart run (0.29.13).** Each cart in `S.carts` is `[arriveAt, meals, hour]` (the hour of its run, as in the job id `restock-meals@<hour>`). Live, while section 17's `cartWaiting(hour)` is true (the run is yours, or on the board waiting for you, and not unloaded), the meals wait past `arriveAt`; `galleyCartLand(hour)`, called by section 17 when you unload at 3-cold-1/2, lands them at once and returns how many. Replays never wait. Section 17 has `cartWaiting` since 0.33.19 (agreed with the systems engineer). Meals on their way count toward the cold store when the galley asks `cartClaim` for a run, so a held run never sets off another (0.29.17).
- **For other sections.**
    - `takePortion(who)`: the player eats. A hot portion and a plate if the servery has one, else a grab item; true or false.
    - `galleyJoin(who)` and `galleyTurn(who)`: the player queues. Join gives `{ at: [33, 2], xy, face: 's', ahead, turnAt }`: stand at the back of the line by the tray return (a tile that's no one's place; the crew hold the four queue places). It's your turn a minute from now plus about 45 seconds for each crew member in the line or waiting. `galleyTurn` is true from then while there's food; `takePortion` serves you and ends it.
    - `galleyHold(person, t)`: for section 10. Where someone waiting for food stands: each waiter gets their own spot, the queue places in line order (`GALLEY_QUEUE`), then null (stand where you are). undefined if they aren't waiting. It brings the galley up to t first. (0.29.7: it used to return the queue place from each person's plan, and those are reused every few minutes, so two waiters could get the same spot.)
    - `galleySave()` and `galleyLoad(obj)`: SHIP.galley as plain data for the save, and back (anything malformed means a fresh start). When section 13 has `SAVE_EXTRA` (the systems engineer's), section 16 adds `save.galley` through it and loads `SAVED.galley` at start; both are guarded by typeof, so they're inert until it's merged.
    - `galleyCooks(t)`: how many are cooking or baking at absolute minute t, for oven wear (section 17). `shipEffectsAt(3, m).cook` (section 17's pure, recorded version, safe in a replay), when it exists, scales each new pot's cook time (`galleyCookT`). The rota and `galleyCooks` must never read the effects, because wear reads them and the effects read wear: that would be a loop.
    - `FOOD.restock`: `'rule'` (the 10:30 top-up, today) or `'carts'`. With `'carts'`, the top-up stops and section 17's restock job (150 meals by hover cart from `SHIP.hold` when `cold.meals` is under 150) is the only way food arrives. One owner at a time, so meals never arrive twice.
    - **Carts (B3, 0.29.9).** `FOOD.restock` is `'carts'` (since 0.29.9; agreed with the systems engineer, whose `cartClaim` is in 0.33.13). Until `cartClaim` exists in the build, the 10:30 top-up carries on, so a build without section 17 still eats. With carts, on the hour `galleyStep` asks `cartClaim(t, cold, { replay })`, a pure rule of section 17's: null, or `{ meals, arriveAt }`. It keeps the run in `S.carts` and lands the meals at `arriveAt`, never past the cap. The same happens in the replay (`galleyReport`) and live, so they agree. Section 17's hourly step calls the same rule only to put the run on the job list, move the cart and take the meals from `SHIP.hold`; it never writes `cold.meals`. The rule proposed to the systems engineer: a daily run from 09:00 when under 250, an urgent one whenever under 90; carry at most 150 and never past 300. One missed day dips the store to about 70; two run it dry, and dinner is missed.
    - `galleyText(what)`: a line for tap texts (`servery`, `grab`, `dish`, `cold`, `menu`). The servery's line starts "Serving: <dish>." and adds tonight's note (a favourite, the Friday treat, the vote); `cold` says when it's running warm and what's binned.
    - `galleyMenu(day)` and `galleyBoard(day)`: the day's menu, and the menu board's text (today's meals and the week's dinners).
    - `galleyReport(day, days)`: days from a fresh galley, with each day's figures, for food_check.py.
- **On Deck 3 (`galleyShow`).** Made when you arrive (after the deck is built, so its unload frees them) and redrawn only when the numbers change. Four instanced meshes, no shadows, never tapped:
    - food in the servery's five pans, rising with the portions;
    - steel covers over the pans when the servery is empty;
    - dirty plates stacking in the dish pit's sink (one for every 3, up to 24);
    - the grab shelf's rolls and wraps, one for every 2 items. The shelf kit (the bridge architect's 0.31.2) calls `galleyShelfRows(g, ys)` instead of drawing those two rows, when its place has `live`
    - bin bags of spoiled food against the wall at the dish pit's end, one for every 10 meals binned, up to 3 (0.29.11; the fifth mesh, drawn only when there's something in the bin);
    - a birthday cake on the servery's tray slide, 19:30–21:30 on birthdays (a mesh and one instanced mesh of candles and flames; 0.29.12);
    - the menu board: its canvas (section 4b's `menuBoard`) is redrawn each day with the day's meals and the dinner's note, and its tap text is `galleyBoard()` (a getter on the item; 0.29.11). and the function exists. It's a function declaration, so it's there when the first deck is built at load. It marks the group, and `galleyVisuals` puts the items on the shelves at `ys`. Their material joins the shelf's fade list, so they fade with it.

  Each mesh is at most 1 draw call.
- **Numbers (days 1–7, before the schedules use the rota).** About 150 people come to eat each day: about 140 hot portions and 7–14 grab items. 30 portions are ready at 06:00 and 40–50 at 17:00. Nobody waits. Cooked 150–160, wasted 0–9. The cold store dips to about 140. All plates are washed by 21:15.
- **food_check.py.** `python3 food_check.py BUILD.html [DAY] [DAYS]` (defaults 1 and 7, about a minute). It checks:
    - food is ready at each opening (10 portions or more), and the grab shelf isn't empty at 06:00;
    - nobody waits more than 5 minutes, and nobody gives up;
    - portions and grab items taken equal the crew who came to eat;
    - no dirty plates and none still out at midnight;
    - the cold store never runs dry;
    - a chef up by 04:45 and cooking by 05:00, with an early and a late hand every day;
    - no station double-booked;
    - the same days twice (with the caches cleared and other days planned first);
    - the live galley matches the report;
    - on Deck 3: pans and covers follow the servery, `takePortion`, save and load, draw calls under 450;
    - once section 14 uses the rota, galley staff are at the rota's stations mid-block;
    - nothing spoils with the chiller as new; with a stand-in broken chiller for a week, food is binned every day, the bags show at 09:30, Mail gets a note, and the cold store never runs short (0.29.11);
    - busy windows over 28 days: longer waits only inside them (up to 25 minutes there, 5 elsewhere), nobody gives up, a rush makes a queue and the servery's text says so at some 5-minute step from 17:00 to 18:30 on the first rush day (0.29.12, 0.29.16);
    - a birthday cake, with a stand-in birthday until ROSTER has them: baked once, shown only from 19:30 to 21:30 with its 10 candles and flames, on the board, in the text and in Mail (0.29.12);
    - section 18's events, with a stand-in `eventsOn` when it isn't there: an event cake is baked, shown and on the board, a harvest day's dinner has salad, and with the real section 18 the birthday gathering's time is read (0.29.15);
    - ship-wide events with a stand-in `eventMod(kind, key, t)`: the ion storm's peak (storm stew, quicker pots, more on the grab shelf, 6 trays for the bridge sent by `cartTrays`) and a formal dinner (a batch of 12, 8 extra portions), with nobody missing a meal (0.29.18);
    - your cart run, with a stand-in `cartWaiting`: the meals wait past arriveAt and `galleyCartLand` lands them once; your birthday is worded for you (0.29.13);
    - two weeks of menus: no full stops in dish names, a pudding on Fridays, Sunday's dish is the top vote, Wednesday's favourite isn't on the week's menu already; at 18:00 the servery says the day's dinner and the board shows it (0.29.11).

## Deck 1, the Bridge (0.8.x)

Built from plans/deck1-plan.html (agreed) by the bridge architect, in parallel with Deck 2 (0.9.x). Builds 0.8.1 onward.

- **Shell (0.8.1).** `DECKS[1]`: 20 × 10, `lift: [8, 5]`, seed 10109. Rooms: `+` Bridge lobby (hall), `B` Bridge (plate for now), `O` Observation room (wood, windows), `w` Bridge washroom, `R` Ready room (locked, windows, `NO_FADE`), and sealed `k` Utility closet, `u` Relay room, `c` Sub-floor crawl. `sealed` maps each shut door to its room's tap text, as on Decks 5 and 6. The ladder hatch is `h,7,7`.
- **Hull pieces by facing (`hull.byFace`).** In `buildDeck`, the hull run splitter wrote piece numbers back into `part[]` while still comparing facings against it, so a run was split almost everywhere: Deck 1's small round hull came out as 128 one-segment pieces (589 draw calls for an empty shell). A deck whose `hull` has `byFace: true` compares the facings themselves (`fc[]`) and gets one piece per facing (Deck 1: 10 pieces, 256 calls). Decks 3–6 don't set it and keep their old pieces exactly (Deck 6 has 234, Deck 4 119). Turning it on for them would save draw calls but changes their hull-run table, so it needs its own regression pass.
- **Viewscreen (0.8.2).** `bridgeViewscreen(rk, a0, a1, y0, y1)` (Deck 1 block) walks the hull segments on the bow ellipse between bearings a0 and a1 (0 dead ahead, measured on the ellipse), keeps those belonging to room rk's hull pieces (matched by segment midpoint against `hr.edges`), and adds a `hullStrip` per piece to that piece's `g`: the picture from y .55 to 2.35, just inside the hull's inner face, with bezels above and below. Being in the piece's `g`, it squashes with the hull for the cutaway and shows as a lit band at knee height. The picture, `BRG.sky`, is a 1024 × 256 canvas drawn at load (stars from a fixed hash, not `R()`); its texture offset is pushed onto `SPINS` as `{ o: { rotation: offset }, ax: 'x' }`, so it drifts with no engine change. No lamp. The tap target in front of it is `tapSpot`, an empty kit piece.
- **Bridge floor (0.8.2).** New floor type `bridge` in the floor painter (blue-grey carpet tiles; it draws the same one `R()` per tile as other floors). New deck hook `floorArt(g, PX)`, called after hazards and before wall shading: Deck 1 paints the command ring from `ring: [15.4, 5, 2.0]` (tile centre and radius): a darker well shaded under its edge, a brass step edge, a teal light line outside it, a faint inner circle.
- **Command ring (0.8.3).** Tile coordinates (B starts at column 10, so room-local x is tile x − 10). Ring centre (15.4, 5), the step edge at r 2.0: a brass `curveStrip` (−.01 to .03 m) and a teal light strip just outside it, built in `dress` into `staticRoot`. The rail is the plan's `bar` curve at r 2.2, trimmed to 140°–220° so the tiles at its ends stay open (the plan's 120°–240° sealed the aft pocket off). Captain's chair (15.2, 5), seats at z 3.85 and 6.15; helm (17.45, 4.45) and ops (17.45, 5.55), each .9 wide so it blocks one row and column 18 stays reachable; their seats at x 16.55. The ring's centre sits on a tile line, so the chairs pass explicit `block` tiles.
- **Rear stations (0.8.3).** Watch (11.45, 3.4) and Survey (13.45, 3.4) against the ready room wall, its door (column 12) left clear; Comms (11.45, 6.6) on the washroom wall; Works (12.4, 8.5) against the washroom's east wall, facing the floor hatch at tile (13, 8) (painted by `floorArt` from `floorHatch`; the real hatch is the edge `h,13,9` behind it). Row 7 is the way round the south of the ring, so nothing stands in it. The plan put two stations in front of the glass beside the bridge doors; they moved so the glass stays clear.
- **Observation room, ready room, washroom (0.8.4).** O (room-local x = tile x − 1): `confTable` at (2.5, 5), 4.8 long along z, blocking column 3 only; ten seats (`commandChair` small, `noBlock`) at x 1.35 and 3.65, z 3–7; `briefingScreen` on the lobby wall (`placeWall('O', ..., 'e', 2.0)`); `windowSeat` (3.6 long) across the stern; `teaSideboard` at (4.67, 7.9); a floor lamp, the deck's one real lamp, at (.55, 2.45); two big plants. R (locked, `NO_FADE`, every item `see`): `readyDesk` at (1.6, 1.5) facing the door, the Captain's chair behind it, two visitors' chairs, `readyShelf` on the east wall, the `aquarium` (the Admiral) on the west wall. w (2 × 2): `compactToilet` in the far corner, `smallBasin` by the door; the door tile stays clear.
- **Draw calls.** Deck 1 by the lift: 315 (Decks 3–5: 326–332). Zoomed right out over the whole deck: about 364, of which doors are about 130 (see below). The cabins' `bookshelf` makes a material per book (about 40 calls), so the ready room has its own `readyShelf` on five shared materials. Each hull piece's viewscreen strips are merged (`mergeMeshes`) into two calls.
- **Deck 1 is done and waiting for the user's review** (0.8.4).
- **Scaled up (0.12.1, plan Sketch 2).** The bullets above give Sketch 1's positions; these replace them. 22 × 12, `lift: [8, 6]` (car rows 4–6, trunks row 3, ladder row 7, ladder hatch `h,7,8`), hull `bowFrom: 15`, world x −12…10, z −6…6. Standable tiles: bridge 85 (was 54), observation room 50 (was 40), ready room 12, washroom 6 (was 4).
    - **Rooms.** O cols 1–5; lobby cols 6–9 rows 2–8; k rows 0–1 and u rows 9–11 over cols 6–9; R cols 10–12 rows 0–3 (door `h,11,4`, plate `h,12,4`); w cols 10–12 rows 8–9 (door `v,10,8`); c cols 10–15 along the starboard edge (floor hatch `h,14,10`, painted at tile (14, 9)); B everything forward, with the vestibule cols 10–12 rows 4–7 inside the bridge doors (`v,10,5`, `v,10,6`).
    - **Command ring** `ring: [17, 6, 2.0]`, rail `arc: [17, 6, 2.4, 130, 230]`. Column 13 stays clear behind the rail, rows 3 and 8 either side of it. Captain's chair (16.8, 6), blocking its own tiles and the two behind it inside the rail; seats at z 4.85 and 7.15; helm and ops (19.4, 5.45 / 6.55) with seats at x 18.5; column 20 stays open in front of the viewscreen.
    - **Rear stations** against the bridge-side walls of R and w, facing the bow: Watch (13.4, 1.5), Survey (13.4, 2.5), Comms (13.4, 8.5), Works (13.4, 9.5) beside the floor hatch; seats at x 13.92.
    - **O:** table at z 6, seats z 4–8, briefing screen at 2.5, window seat at z 6, tea at z 8.9, plants at z .6 and 11.4. **R:** desk (1.5, 2.1) facing the door, chair (1.5, 1.3), visitors at x .6 and 2.4 (the door tile stays clear), aquarium (.36, .9), shelf (2.72, .9). **w:** basin (2.5, .32) and toilet (2.62, 1.45, facing west) down the far wall, reached from the middle.
    - **Draw calls (0.12.1):** 299 by the lift (0.10.1: 315), about 378 zoomed right out (was 364), 210–330 in room views.
- **Mood light:** `light: { sky #d4e2ff, sun #dfe8ff, hemi .96, sunI .9 }`, a little cooler and dimmer than the decks below.
- **Kit:** the Deck 1 block sits just above `DECKS[1]`: materials in `BRG`, pieces added with `Object.assign(KIT, ...)` (and `WALL_KIT`). Nothing there calls `R()`.
- **Doors cost a lot of draw calls on every deck** (about 120 of Deck 1's 256): each leaf is a box with six material groups. Grouping the four edge faces and the two leaf faces would cut that by two thirds with identical pixels, but changes the call counts the tests compare, so it's left for the lead.

## Places on Decks 1 and 3 (0.18.x, Pillar 1 workstream 3b)

The bridge architect's places, for the sim engineer's schedules (see plans/pillar1-plan.md, "Places"). Data only: nothing in the game reads or draws them yet.

- **Where:** `places: [...]` in `DECKS[1]` (just before its `dress`) and `DECKS[3]` (just after its `curves`). One place per line.
- **Fields:** the plan's `id`, `type`, `at: [c, r]`, `face`, `pose` and `room`, plus two more:
    - `xy: [x, z]`, the exact spot in tile units. A seat can sit off its tile's centre, and one tile can hold two seats (the mess benches seat four along 3.6 m), so `at` alone would stack people.
    - `walk: [c, r]`, the tile a person walks to before taking the place. It's `at` when that tile is walkable; otherwise the nearest tile in the same room, within 2.5 m, that can be reached from the lift. 122 of the 203 places need one: seats, booths, benches, toilets. Some can't be reached from a tile next door: the mess benches against the hull, which you slide into from the table's end, and the middle booths in the lounge.
- **How they were made:** a script logged every `place()` call's real position and rotation, and put seats where each kit piece draws them (the long tables' benches at ±.62 m, the round tables' chairs at .8 m, booths, bar stools, high tables, sofas, benches, planter islands), plus a few spots by hand: the servery queue, the galley pass and the lift waits. The output is kept as fixed data, so it only changes when someone means it to. If furniture moves, regenerate it or fix the lines by hand.
- **ids:** `<deck>-<what>-<n>`. Deck 1: `1-command-1` is the Captain's chair, `-2` the first officer's seat, `-3` the guest seat; `1-helm-1`, `1-ops-1`, `1-watch-1`, `1-survey-1`, `1-comms-1`, `1-works-1`; `1-conf-1..10` (the conference table), `1-tea-1`, `1-window-1..4`, `1-toilet-1`, `1-basin-1`, `1-lift-1..4`. Deck 3: `3-mess-1..44`, `3-queue-1..4` (in queue order, trays first), `3-grab-1`, `3-galley-1..8` and `3-galley-pass`, `3-infirmary-1..6` (5: ward round, 6: supplies shelf, 0.18.4), `3-visit-1..2`, `3-toilet-1..4`, `3-basin-1..2`, `3-wbench-*`, `3-bench-*`, `3-garden-*` (bench seats), `3-tend-*` (beds and racks), `3-gym-*`, `3-callhome-1..4`, `3-lounge-*`, `3-store-1`, `3-shop-*`, `3-lift-1..6`.
- **Types beyond the plan's list:** `work:command` (the command ring), `work:store` (the crew store counter) and `shop` (browsing the shelves and the drinks fridge). The conference seats are `common-room`.
- **Counts:**
    - Deck 1 (31): `work:command` 4, helm 1, ops 1, watch 1, survey 1, comms 1, works 1, `common-room` 11, `lounge-seat` 4, toilet 1, basin 1, `lift-wait` 4.
    - Deck 3 (175): `mess-seat` 44, `servery-queue` 4, `grab-shelf` 1, `work:galley` 9, `work:infirmary` 6, `lounge-seat` 34, `bench` 22, `garden` 23, `gym` 9, `callhome` 4, `shop` 6, `work:store` 1, toilet 4, basin 2, `lift-wait` 6.
- **Ready room (0.18.3):** `1-ready-1`, the Captain's desk chair (`work:command`), walk tile (10, 1). Its door `h,11,4` is a closed door, which crew can open; `deck_check` counts closed doors as passable for crew.
- **Left out:** the infirmary's four patient beds (no type for them yet), and showers (none on these decks).
- **Held back in 0.18.2, back in 0.18.3:** `deck_check` didn't yet take `walk` or `shop`, so 14 Deck 3 places sat out one build. The lead has since made `xy`, `walk` and `shop` part of the plan, and `deck_check` (from `claude/deck-architect`) checks the walk rule.
- **Check:** every place is checked the way the plan says: `walk` is walkable, in the place's room, reachable from the lift, and equal to `at` or within 2.5 m of `xy`. ids are unique ship-wide, and `face`, `pose` and `room` are valid. The script is in the session scratchpad. `deck_check.py`'s own place check comes from the Deck 6 architect.

## Pillar 2 on Decks 1 and 3 (0.31.x, workstream A5b)

- **Grab shelf hook (0.31.2):** `grabShelf` with `o.live` calls `galleyShelfRows(g, ys)` for the wraps and rolls rows when the galley's section 16 defines it as a function declaration (so it exists when the first deck is built at load); `ys` are those rows' shelf heights in the shelf's frame. Without it, the shelf draws as before.
- **Store (0.31.2):** staffed by the roster's storekeepers. The counter stands at room-local (2.85, 2.5), out from the east wall; the storekeeper's place `3-store-1` is behind the till at (35.55, 14.85) facing west, and the shopper's `3-shop-7` is in front at (34.15, 14.85).
- **Activity furniture (0.31.1):** `till` and `basketStack` (Deck 3 kit block, just above `DECKS[3]`; no `R()`). The till stands on the self-service counter at room-local (3.6, 1.85); the baskets are at (.4, .5), just inside the store door (`h,33,13`). The gym already had a weight rack and bench, so no new weights were needed. Nobody's roster job is `work:store`: the store is self-service.
- **`anim` on places:** see the 0.31.1 row. Places without `anim` take it from their type, as in the Pillar 2 plan.
- **Utility rooms (0.31.3):** built as planned in the deck plan pages' "Utility rooms" sections (updated to the built positions). Kit in the Deck 3 kit block: `recyclerTanks`, `filterBank`, `pumpSkid`, `uvUnit`, `cleanTank`, `risers`, `sump`, `extractorUnit`, `chillerUnit`, `nutrientTanks`, `irrigationPump`, `headerPipes`, `relayRack` (`o.w`), `cabinet` (`o.breakers`), `dataBus`, `floorCableTray` (named `cableTray` until 0.31.8), and wall kit `relayBox`. They use only materials these decks already draw (Deck 3 by the lift: 329 draw calls; day_check peak 417).
    - Rooms: R, g, i (Deck 3) and k, u, c (Deck 1) have `floor` and `still: true` instead of `void`; their hatches are listed in `service`, so the player and Works crew pass. Deck 1 gains the hatch `v,10,10` (relay room to crawl).
    - `machines` (Deck 3, 12): `3-oven-1`, `3-oven-2`, `3-range-1` (galley), `3-galleyplant-1` (the extractor), `3-chiller-1`, `3-recycler-1`, `3-filter-1`, `3-pump-1`, `3-uv-1`, `3-lights-1`, `3-nutrient-1`, `3-irrigation-1`. (Deck 1, 5): `1-relay-1`, `1-relay-2`, `1-junction-1`, `1-lights-1`, `1-databus-1`. Each has a place `<id>-panel` (`work:works`, `machine: '<id>'`) on its panel tile, standing a little towards the machine.
    - Kinds (chiller, filter, pump, uv, lights, nutrient, databus) are all in `MACHINE_KINDS` since 0.33.2, so the registry builds every one.
    - Section 14 (shared, one line): `schedData` skips places with `machine`, so a panel is never handed out as a desk.

## Places for Pillar 3 (C9, the deck architect, 0.38.x)

One architect now owns all six decks' data (branch `claude/decks-p3`, builds 0.38.x). Plan: plans/pillar3-plan.md, "Gatherings of more than 70 crew", "Moments", Part 4 and Part 5.

- **Gathering venues (0.38.0).** In one block just after `DECKS[1]` (search `Big gatherings`): `DECKS[n].gathers = { <venue>: { name, focus, rooms, seats, also, lead, at } }`.
    - `focus: [x, z]` (tile units) is where the crowd looks; `rooms` the venue's own room letters; `seats` the place types in those rooms that also count (the mess seats, lounge seats, benches); `also` other venues to spill into.
    - `lead: [[x, z, face], ...]` is the front: lead 1 the speaker (the Captain, Calder, whoever arrives), the rest beside them. `face` in degrees from south toward east.
    - `at: 'c,r c,r ...'` are the standing spots: the venue's rooms nearest the focus first, then the rooms next door. Generated by a scratch script: walkable, reachable from the lift, not a doorway or lift-door tile, not any existing place's tile; fixed data from then on.
    - At load each becomes a place `{ id: '<deck>-<venue>-crowd-<n>' | '<deck>-<venue>-lead-<n>', type: 'event', gather, lead?, at, xy, face, pose: 'stand', room }`. The crowd's `xy` is a fixed few cm off centre (from c and r, no `R()`) and `face` points at the focus. No `event` tag, so section 14 never hands them out day to day (crew plans are byte-identical with and without them).
    - `gatherSpots(venue)` returns them in the order to fill, each with `deck`: leads, then the venue's spots and seats nearest the focus, then its spill rooms, then each `also` venue. `gatherVenue(venue)` gives the table entry with its deck. For the sim engineer's `gathering()` and the events engineer's meetings (`where: '<venue>'`).

  | Venue | Deck | Leads | Spots in fill order | Spill |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `plaza` | 3 | 1, east of the lift facing down the promenade | 87 (70 standing, 16 benches) | — |
  | `mess` | 3 | 1, by the servery | 153 (44 seats, 22 standing, then the plaza's) | plaza |
  | `bow-lounge` | 3 | 1, the open floor between the bar and the windows | 181 (34 seats, 60 standing, then the plaza's) | plaza |
  | `shuttle-bay` | 5 | 3 (0.38.5): the Captain, Envoy Saruth, and the Speaker or whoever arrives at the foot of the Tern's ramp, facing them | 124 (about 95 in the bay, then the haulway and lobby) | — |
  | `works-bay` | 5 | 3: Calder beside the job board, two techs either side (the storm briefing) | 21 | — |
  | `observation` | 1 | 2: the Captain by the wall screen, the XO beside her (the all-clear) | 44 (15 seats, then the lobby) | — |

- **Event dressing and traces (0.38.1).** Just after the gatherings block (search `Event dressing and traces`). Props that appear only while an event asks; nothing calls them yet, so the game is unchanged.
    - `DECK_DRESS[id] = { deck, what, make(g, o) }`. The six, named after section 18's trace names: `signupMess` (a sign-up sheet beside the mess menu board), `signupWorks` (one pinned to the job board's corner), `stormRosterTeaCorner` (the storm crew's framed, signed roster in the Works tea corner), `relayScorch` (a scorch mark on Deck 1's relay room floor), `tapedPanel` (an access panel taped shut, low on the relay room's west wall, under the calendar), `partyLounge` (bunting, three bunches of balloons and a two-sided banner in the bow lounge), and (0.38.5) `veyliCordSculpture` (the diplomatic visit's gift, on the bow lounge's west wall between the booths).
    - `dressShow(list)`: list = ids, or `{ id, title, lines, head }` (a sheet's title and names; the banner's text is `title`, default WELL DONE, WREN!). Shows exactly those on the current deck, removes the rest, does nothing when the list hasn't changed, and puts them back after a deck change. **For the events engineer:** call it from `eventsTick` once a ship minute with the current event's dressing and the saved traces. `dressingOn()` lists what's drawn.
    - Each is tappable (a line of text), merged per material (sheets 3 draw calls, roster 2, scorch 3, panel 3, party 5), no shadows, no `R()`, colours through `col()`, not in the collision grid. Wall pieces lower with their wall (`WALL_DECOR`). Hiding disposes their geometry and canvases; repeated show/hide leaves memory where it was.

- **Visitable rooms (0.38.2).** After the gatherings block (search `Visitable rooms`). `DECKS[n].visits = { <id>: { name, room, door, host, places } }`: `door` is the edge the sim engineer's `doorOpenFor` opens for the appointment; `host` the host's place; `places` the guests' places, yours first. `visitSpots(id)` returns them as place objects with their deck.
    - `ready-room` (Deck 1): the Captain at her desk (1-ready-1); you stand in front of the desk (1-ready-visit-1), two visitors' chairs by the door (-2, -3), and a spot looking into the Admiral's tank (-4). Type `event`, `visit: 'ready-room'`: nobody uses them day to day.
    - `captain-table` (Deck 2): the Captain's existing table seats; she takes the north end (2-captable-5), you the south end (2-captable-6), then the other four.
    - `conference` (Deck 1, 0.38.3): the observation room's table for a briefing (Part 4): the presenter stands by the wall screen (1-observation-lead-1); you at the far end (1-conf-10), then 1-conf-1..9. `door: null`: the room's glass doors are open.
- **Pillar 4, the arrival evening (0.38.9).** In the C9 block: `DECKS[4].keepsakes` (shelf slots above berth 4's bunk, x and z in tiles, y in metres; place `4-B1-4-keepsake`), `DECKS[5].mugRack.yours` (the free hook's mug spot), `CALM_TYPES` / `placeCalm(p)`. Wall kit `keepsakeShelf` and `mugRack` (before the gym kit), placed last in Decks 4's and 5's `dress()`.
- **Pillar 4, Sowing morning and Tiles (0.38.10).** In the C9 block after `placeCalm`: `DECKS[3].seedlings` (the stand's 12 pots, `yours`, place `3-seedlings-1`), `DECKS[3].tiles` (`station` places, the station `board`, Tiles night's eight `boards` as `[x, y, z, turn, size]`) and `tilesSeats(k)`. In `DECK_DRESS`: `seedlingYours` (a trace: section 18 or onboarding pushes `{ id: 'seedlingYours', day }` to `SHIP.traces` when you plant on Sowing morning; it regrows to its stage whenever Deck 3 is built) and `tilesNight` (for section 18 to list while Tiles night is on). Kit `seedlingStand` (after `growRack`), `tilesTable` (after `stool`), `TILES_KIT` / `tilesBoardGeo()` (after `GD`). The station's places are inert for now; when the Tiles game is built, its engineer can make them `lounge-seat` (which changes crew plans, to be explained then). No hooks into other sections.
- **Pillar 4, Letters evening, the tasting table and the Lantern walk (0.38.11).** Places `3-postbox-1` (`letters`), `3-lantern-1..12` (`lanterns`). `DECK_DRESS.tastingTable` (`{ id, title: the dish, lines: up to five }`), `DECK_DRESS.lanternWalk`: for section 18 to list during their windows. Kit `postbox` (after `tilesTable`). Calm exceptions are `calm:` on the place lines (3-wbench-1/2 false; 2-obsnight-1/6/7 and 3-tiles-1/2 true).
- **Long-table seats (0.38.20, 0.38.23).** `3-longtable-1..14` (`event: 'long-table'`): four end chairs in the mess and a trestle on the promenade, drawn by `longTables` / `harvestTables` (`messExtra`, `MESS_EXTRA`).
- **The walk-in Tern (0.38.18).** Kit `shuttle` (rebuilt), `DECKS[5].tern`, places `5-tern-*` (`tern`), `ternRamp(open, secs)`, `ternRampOpen()`, `ternInside()`, `ternClear()`; the shuttle-bay venue's focus and leads moved to the new ramp.
- **Tiles, approved design (0.38.17).** `TILES_KIT` repainted; taps 'Tiles station' and 'Tiles board' (one per Tiles-night board through `dressShow`'s `more`); trace `tilesTrophy` (Deck 4, `DECKS[4].keepsakes.trophy`).
- **The traditions' places (0.38.14).** Tagged with the tradition ids: `2-open-lab-1..8`, `1-bridge-hour-1..10`, `4-music-1` (`late-piano`), `3-lantern-1..12` (`lantern-walk`). `DECK_DRESS.mugYours` (trace). Decor ids agreed with the traditions engineer: `longTables`, `harvestTables`, `cupBoard`, `talentStage`, `yearsEnd`, `openLab` (0.38.16; mending day and rummage day were cut by the user, so no `mendingTable` or `rummageCrates`). The Deck 3 ones are in 0.38.15; `MESS_LONG` lists the mess's long tables (room-local).
- **The working garden (0.38.6).** `3-gardenwork-1..5` (`work:garden`, standing and watering at the racks and beds, on tiles that aren't tend spots); machines `3-growlight-1/2` (kind `growlight`: dropped by `machineList` until section 17 registers the kind) with `3-growlight-1-panel` and `-2-panel` (`work:works`); `3-crates-1` (`storage`) at [9,12] by the glass doors (moved from [10,12] in 0.38.7). 0.38.7 adds three root beds (`raisedBed` crop and `live: 'roots'`: local (8.1, 8.9) and (8.1, 13.15) turned north-south, (5.7, 17.45)) and a stern trellis (`live: 'beans'`, local (.3, 14.8)), so all of the galley's 15 beds have a place in the garden.
- **Patient beds (0.38.4).** `3-ward-1..4` (type `patient-bed`, pose `lie`) on the infirmary's ward beds, west to east. Not anyone's bed (type `bed` is what the sleep plan reads), so nobody sleeps there unless the sim sends a patient.
- **Gym kit (0.38.2).** Last in Deck 3's `dress()`, no `R()`, existing GYM materials: a second pair of rowers by the mirror wall (3-gym-11/12, `anim: 'row'`, seats slide), a punch bag on a stand east of the weight bench (3-gym-13, `anim: 'punch'`; the bag is one unmerged mesh that swings while someone punches, via `MOVERS`), and a mat in the middle of the room (3-gym-14, `anim: 'stretch'`, standing at the mat's head). Poses from the performance engineer (8b, 0.27.15). +1 draw call on Deck 3.

## Places for 0.60.7 (deck architect 4, 0.38.24+)
