Pictures: `plans/shots-0607/`.

The names first (the hook, 2026-10-18), so every seat can code against them; the deck architect builds them in this order and marks each **built** here when it lands. Rules as before: every new place is inert (type `event` with an `event` tag, or a new type nobody's pool reads) unless it says otherwise, so crew plans don't change until the seat that owns the behaviour uses it. Item tags are fields on the tappable item (`ITEMS[k].<tag>`), so a tap handler can test `it.bar`, `it.shelf` and so on.

## Places for Pillar 5 Step 3 (seat A, deck architect 5, 0.47.3+)

## The rec deck (R, the rec deck architect, 0.80.x)

The plan is `plans/recdeck-plan.html` v3 (on `claude/recdeck-plan`); build branch `claude/recdeck-build`. Section `// 4e. THE REC DECK` sits after section 4d's dressing code.

- **Key and label.** `DECKS[7]` (`num: 7`), shown as **R**. `DECK_ORDER = [1, 2, 3, 4, 7, 5, 6]` is the order anything shown to the player lists decks in (the lift panel, the device's Maps buttons); `deckLabel(n)` gives `'R'` or the number; `deckWord(n)` gives `'Rec deck'` or `'Deck n'` for the HUD, the arrival toast, the Maps heading and the goal arrow's "Take the lift to the rec deck". Never let "Deck 7" reach the screen. `DECK_NAMES[7] = 'Recreation'`.
- **Phase 1: the player's only** (the user's rule). `playerOnly: true` keeps the deck out of the crew's schedules (`schedData`'s places), `placesOf`, the meeting places (`apptPlaces`), the job finder's where lookup and the blackout's deck pick. Nothing else on the ship mentions it (the other decks' directories still say six decks). Lift it in the phase that brings the crew (the plan's R7).
- **Taller walls.** `tall: 1.3` (1.6 until 0.80.7; the user: it hid too much) on a deck makes `H` (now a `let`, 2.8 × `tall`, set by `buildDeck`) and `WALL_K`. `writeWalls` stretches walls, posts and headers to the full height when up and brings them down to the usual low wall when cut away (`ts`); with `WALL_K` 1 every number is the same as before. Hull strips, void blocks and anything a kit builds from `H` are built at the tall height.
- **Floors without R().** Every rec room has `rec: true` and a `recFloor` type (`sport`, `tile`, `pooltile`, `court`, `stand`, `violet`, `pad`, `river`, `svc`), drawn from a fixed hash, so changing one room's floor never shifts another's. The neon stripes run along the hall's walls, cyan to port (north) and magenta to starboard (south).
- **Kit.** The deck's own materials are made by `recMats()` at the start of its `dress`, so they're freed with the deck; no rec kit is made at load.
- **Doors.** The plan's DOORSR, except `v,19,10` and `v,21,10`, which are solid wall so the lift's call panels sit beside the doors as on every deck. The kit hire counter and the court's rail are `pass` (sill, no pane). The four service rooms are `service` hatches (the player and Works pass).
- **Opening hours.** `open: [6, 24]` on the deck; the activities say "The rec dec's closed till six." outside it.
- **Your activities (section 15r, 0.80.6; on RA1's mover from 0.80.7).** `recTap` (pushed onto section 15's `PLAYER_TAPS`, Deck 7 only) turns a tap on the Pool, the rivers or the Kayak dock / rack / wave gate, the Climbing wall, the Float room, the auditorium's Seats or the Stands into a section 15 activity (`PLAYER_ACTS` kinds `recSwim`, `recKayak`, `recClimb`, `recFloat`, `recSeat`, `recWatch`, `recShower`; the change is 8c's `recChange`). The moving ones pass section 8c's `move` (see "Rec deck moves"): a swim of 4 lengths in the lane nearest your tap (`REC_LANES`), 2 laps of the river (`REC_LOOP`, fast on the white water), a climb on the route you tapped (`top` from `H`, the bell's toast in `onRing`), 4 float bounces. Each gives leisure; `also` adds side gains as the minutes pass (a swim: hygiene and fitness). **The wet way:** in uniform, a pool or kayak tap first walks you to a locker (`recChange` with `kit: 'rec'`), then on to the water; `recAfter` then sends you to a shower in the changing rooms (a towel after, `recTowel`) and back to the locker (`kit: null`). Walking away ends the chain (tap the Lockers to change back); leaving the deck puts your uniform back on. First pool or kayak tap: the tip `recChange`. The court, the scoreboard and the Ask someone to play panel (by the gate, the user's: Lightdisc comes later) say it's coming soon.

### Rec deck moves (section 8c, the rec deck animation engineer, 0.81.x)

Phase 1: the player only. The rec deck builder wires these to the rooms' places; this section owns the poses, props and kit.

- **Poses** (8b's `ACTS`, any rig): `swim` (front crawl, face down, the back at the water, the point is the chest), `tread` (chest-deep, sculling), `kayak` (seated in the hull: kayak on the body bone, paddle and helmet on the chest; `o.fast` rocks harder), `climb`, `ring` (one hand ringing), `lower` (sitting back in the harness, walking down the wall), `float` (`o.spin` turns a somersault about the middle, `rig.star` 0-1 opens a star), `cheer`, `clap`, `groan` (sit at a seat: `ACT_SEATED`), and `+towel` (a HOLDS with `one: 2`, so both arms swing). The water hides what's under it: nothing is cut.
- **Whole-body moves.** An activity's `root(o, root, s, t)` runs after `CrowdPerson.update` places the root (raise, lower, tilt, offset), by its blend weight. `animateActivity` sets `o.rootOn` the first time someone does one; nobody else runs `recRoot`, so other decks are untouched.
- **Rec kit** (`REC_KIT`, `makeCharacter` `kit: 'rec'`): teal T-shirt, dark teal shorts with a coral stripe, coral collar, chest band and sleeve hems, bare forearms, shins and feet; no badge, pips or belt. `socks: true` adds white grip socks with coral soles (also on a uniform). Same joints and meshes: no extra draw calls. `playerKit(k)` rebuilds your Walker (and the stand-in) and keeps the grubby tint off rec kit; not saved, a reload finds you in uniform.
- **The mover.** `actStart(kind, placeId, { move: M })`: walk to the place as usual, then in the 'do' phase `recMoveStep(A, dt, t)` drives the stand-in and the activity ends when the move does (`A.mv.done`). Deck coordinates like a place's `xy`, heights in metres: `swim { path: [a, b], y, lengths, speed }`, `tread { at, y, face }`, `kayak { path: [[x, z, fast?], ...], y, laps }`, `climb { at, face, top, onRing }`, `float { at, r, top, bounces }`. Without a count a move runs to the activity's minutes, then stops at its next natural end. Float puts the stand-in in grip socks.
- **Changing:** `PLAYER_ACTS.recChange` (2 ship minutes of the wardrobe's `browse` at a locker, then `playerKit` toggles, or `o.kit`). **Towel:** `recTowel(mins)` sets `player.towel` (a ship minute), and the Walker shows `+towel` until then.
- **Test spot:** `?recposes` (`REC_DEMO`) goes to R at 10:30 and lines up every pose on the concourse in rec kit with a swimmer, kayaker, climber and floater on stand-in water. Dev only; take it out with the rooms' own wiring.
- **Phase 2 (crew):** a crowd body's root isn't in the scene, so `recRope` draws no line for crew yet; give the crowd a line of its own when crew climb.
- **The auditorium's show (0.84.8, RB2):** `recShow(null | 'film' | 'talk' | 'allhands' | 'talent')`, called by RA's traditions engine; looks only (house lights while you're in the auditorium, the screen, the stage pools). See the 0.84.8 row.
- **The stage (0.84.9, RB2):** crew moves `perform { act: 'sing' | 'juggle' | 'piano' | 'bow', len? }` and `address {}`; poses `sing`, `juggle` (balls by `recCrewBalls`), `bow`, `address`. Spots in the 0.84.9 row.

## The rec deck's crew (section 14, RA, 0.83.x)

Brief: `plans/brief-recdeck.md` (claude/lead-pillar1), seat RA; plan v5 section 9. Everything waits for `REC_DECK`.

- **Places** (`REC_CREW_PLACES`, section 4e, concatenated into `DECKS[7].places`): see the 0.83.0 row. Crowd poses come from each place's `anim` (swim, kayak, climb, float, browse, wash); the moves themselves (lengths, the loop, up and down the wall) and the crowd's rec kit are rec engineer B's (section 8c). Visits' segments are tagged `rec-<kind>` (`rec-swim`, `rec-kayak`, `rec-climb`, `rec-float`, `rec-disc`, `rec-watch`, `rec-bean`), `rec-wait` at a waiting spot, `rec-change` at a locker and `rec-shower`.
- **Habits** (`REC_HABITS`, `recHabit(p)`, key 1030): never in HABIT_TAKE or HABIT_DROP, so the weekly habit changes are as before.
- **Visits** (`schedBuild`'s `recVisit` and `recSwap`, keys 1031-1034): only in place of rest, or of a block off the Commons (see the row). `REC_ODDS` (a habit 0.9, anyone 0.35, of a block off the Commons 0.6 of that), `REC_TURN` 20-40, `REC_Q` 10, `REC_CAP` 15 / 6 (`recCap(d, m)`).
- **The cap's count:** `shipDay` keeps `occ.rec` beside `occ.heads` (Deck 3's).

## Leave, missed shifts and being let go (the career engineer, 0.61.x)

The career section sits in section 20, after crew come and go (`movedOff`). It reads section 20's reputation, section 18's occasions and section 14's plans, and nothing in it calls `R()`. All the numbers are in `CAREER`.

- **Leave.** `SHIP.career.l` days held: 3 to start, +1 each time `floor((day - 1) / 28)` grows (days 29, 57...), at most 5 (`CAREER.leave`, `every`, `max`). An older save starts with 3 and no back pay. `careerCanBook(d)` -> `'book' | 'cancel' | null`: a workday by your rota, not sick, not let go, before 07:30 that day (`bookBy`). `careerBook(d)` books or cancels (cancelling gives the day back, capped at 5) and sends `simNote('leave', { day, booked })`. The Schedule shows a button under each day you can book ("Take this day as leave (N left)" / "Cancel this leave"), marks the day "Leave", and shows the leave tip once (a normal tip).
- **Days off.** `careerOff(d)`: a booked leave day, a sick day, or let go. Section 14's `playerShiftOn(d)` asks it first, so the shift, `shiftTick`'s start note, the Schedule and the jobs held for you (`leaveEnd`) all treat it as a day off with no other change. `careerSick(d, on)` is the hook for an illness system (the player can't fall ill yet): days in the infirmary never count.
- **Missed shifts.** `careerTick` (each ship minute from `tickShip`, and after a skip through `SIM_ADVANCE`) notes your start: in a Works room on Deck 5 (`shiftTick`'s test), a `shiftStart` note that isn't missed, or a repair of yours, from 07:00 to 12:30. A workday not started by 12:30 (`missBy`: over half of 08:00-17:00), with no leave, is missed: `repAdd('works', -6, { work: -15 }, 'missed')` (`hitDept`, `hitWork`; repAdd softens it below 50 and Sunday pulls work toward 50) and `simNote('shiftMissed', { day })`. Days 1-7 are a grace (`grace`: the first week never counts as missed). Being late stays section 20's small dip (20+ minutes: work -2). A skip over whole days counts each workday it passes.
- **The ladder** (0.61.4 and 0.61.6, the user's rules): work under 15 (`chiefLow`), or 3 workday shifts missed in a row (`chiefRun`; `ms` counts the run, days off, leave and sick days neither count nor break it) bring the Chief's talk, again after each worked shift (`ca` is set when it's taken for a run, cleared by a worked shift); then, once that talk has been held, `SHIP.rep.ship.work` under 12 (`captain`) brings the Captain's reprimand; `st` is the step reached (0, 1, 2) and `q` a step waiting to be booked (`qd` the day it fell due). Each is an occasion (`OCCASIONS.careerChief`: Calder's office, Deck 5, 15:30; `careerCaptain`: the ready room, Deck 1, 11:00; `due` is always false). `careerDue` books a waiting step with `occasionAdd` from 07:00 until 30 minutes before its time, on a day its person is on shift (or once it has waited `wait` 3 days), and the Captain's only after the Chief's latest talk is over (`careerHeld`): so the Mail (sent as it's booked) can say "today", and the order is always the Chief, then the Captain. It comes with the appointment, the Mail and the meeting, the warning, the worried friends and `simNote('careerStep', { step })`. Recovering re-arms: back to 40 (`rearm.chief`) clears both steps, to 25 (`rearm.captain`) the Captain's, with the tip `up`.
- **Warnings.** `careerTip(key)` puts a tip straight on the device's queue (`TIP_Q`), so it shows even with tips off, like the low-need warnings: the first late start, the first missed shift, each step, the bottom and the notice. After each step up to 3 friends (tier 2+, closest first) are asked to come and find you (`socialSeekAsk`, why `career:chief|captain|last|eve|day`); `seekShow` takes the why, and the chip opens the chat with their line (`careerSeekLine`: kinds `worried.*` and `farewell.*`).
- **Let go.** At the end of each workday (days off and leave neither count nor reset), once the Captain's reprimand has been held (kept or missed: so being let go always comes after both talks), work under 5 (`bottom`) is a day at the bottom (`n`); 3 in a row (`days`) and `careerLetGo` sets the notice day `go` (the next day) and the send-off friends `f` (up to 4 friends, plus Torres, Pell and Calder: `seeOff`), clears booked leave, books Calder's last words (`careerLetGo`, her office, 10:00 that day) and the send-off (`careerSendoff`, the shuttle bay, 14:30-15:00 the next day), and clears the plans' cache so the last day's plans pick it up. Notice day, 07:00: the notice Mail, the warning, friends come to say goodbye. The last day (`go + 1`): no shift; friends find you again; section 14's `gatherings` puts the send-off friends (`careerSendOff(d)`) in the shuttle bay 14:30-15:00 like a crew leaver's, and the send-off plays when you're there; at 15:00 (or when the send-off ends, by 15:30 at most) `end` is set, the game saves and the closing card covers the screen with New game (arrive or skip, as the HUD's button). A save that ended shows the card on load. New game clears it all (`onSim('newGame')`).
- **The time clock** (0.61.13): see the 0.61.13 row and the comment above `careerClockedIn`. `careerClock(now)` -> 'in' | 'out' | null; `careerClockTap`; `careerRemark(what, now, late)`; `careerBack(d)`. `k` bit 8 is the clock tip.
- **Being sick** (0.61.5): see the 0.61.5 row and the comment at `SICK` (section 20, after the career section): `sickDue(d)`, `sickStart`, `sickStage`/`sickFx`/`sickBent`, `sickCure(how)` (1 Rina, 2 a medic on duty, 3 slept it off), `sickExamTick`, `sickTick` (from `careerTick`), `sickChatLine`. `k` bit 4 is the sick tip shown.
- **The save.** `ship.career = { l, w, b, s, st, n, on, e, z, k, ci, co, rm, gs, md, mx, sd, sh, ss, sc, ms, ca, q, qd, fl, go, f, end }`, about 170-200 bytes (`md`: the last missed day, so a sick note can undo it; `sd`, `sh`, `ss`, `sc`: the last sick day, how it ended, the stage told, the day the card showed) (lists capped at 8). `careerClean` reads it defensively.
- **Words** (head writer 4): `content/meetings/careerChief|careerCaptain|careerLetGo|careerSendoff.meet`, `content/banks/career.lines` and `content/career/*.lines` (each voice's own; kinds `worried.chief|captain|last`, `farewell.eve|day`), and texts read as data (`careerText(kind, k)`, `{me}` filled): `career.tip.*`, `career.mail.*` as "subject | body", `career.card` as "title | body" (variant by the notice day). Written but not used yet: `career.missed`, `career.up`, `career.leave.booked`, `career.onleave`, `career.back` (crew remarks about your leave and standing).
- **Testing.** `leave_check.py BUILD.html` (about a minute) runs it on paper: leave earned, booked, cancelled; (a) a diligent and a casual player never get a summons; (b) all the leave and sick days never drop; (c) skipping everything: the Chief on day 10, the Captain on day 12, Calder's last words on day 19, the send-off and the end on day 20; two missed, a worked day and two more bring no talk, three in a row do, lateness alone never (the first week is a grace), the talks always in order; friends asked at every step and at the send-off; (d) each step comes again after recovering; (e) the save is about 110 bytes and loads back the same.

## Pillar 5: your Works career, section 24 core (the Works career engineer, 0.44.x)

Section 24 WORK sits between 23 TILES and 6 CAMERA (it reads 13, 14, 17, 18, 19, 20 and 21, all earlier). Built from `plans/mechanics/foundation.html` ("For the builder · section 24 core") on `claude/p5-design`. Nothing here calls `R()`; choices use schedU keys 850-854 (850: a training meeting's time).

- **Lazy state.** `SHIP.works` (saved as `ship.works`, `worksClean` on load) is made by `worksInit()` the first time anything asks from Day 1 17:00 (never at load; a save without it starts Tier 1, nothing trained, and the ladder opens from its day). Its fields are the page's, plus `cur`, the training under way (`{ id, st: ask|meet|watch|do|again|held, d, by, occ, j, w, hold?, told? }`), and a log row carries the job's kind as a 4th entry (and who noticed, `worksNoticed`, as a 5th) so the Work log can title jobs that have left the list.
- **The ladder.** `CAREER_LADDER` holds Steps 1-2's rows (m1, m4, m3d, m5, m3i, m7, p3, m6, m2, t2). `worksCareerTick()` (main loop) runs `ladderTick(h)` once a ship hour: on a workday of yours, a big row opens 06:00-10:30 and a small one 06:00-16:00, the first whose day has come, whose needs hold (`ladderNeeds`: intro shadow, a mechanic used N times, trained, a band, Pillar 4's work score, a flag) and whose teacher is on shift; at most one of each a day (`op[day]`); none on days off. **A row opens only if its sub-block registered `WORK_MECH[id]`**: in 0.44.0 none has, so nothing opens. Rows with `fixed: { wd, at }` (the drill, Wednesday; the inspection, Thursday) open only on that weekday.
- **Training (big rows).** The ask: `occasionAdd('works.<id>')` (an appointment and Mail from the teacher) at 08:30-15:00, a toast and the goal arrow; the meeting plays `MEETING_SCRIPTS['train.<id>']` (stand-in lines until the Works writer's). Then the teacher's training job (`jobReserve(row.job, ..., 10 + n, { train })`, or the sub-block's `reserve`) which they lead you to (`worksEscort`) and finish after you've watched 15 ship minutes nearby (`jobFinish(j, t, teacher)`); then the same slot again, held for you, the teacher following and saying a word at each step; turning it in trains you (`worksTrained`: `tr[id]`, the tool, `simNote('trained')`). Missed meeting, or a job released at the end of the shift: offered again on your next workday. Small rows train on their first use (`worksUse`).
- **Reputation.** `xp` from your jobs turned in (service 1, repair 2, overhaul 3, other 1; +1 each found by you, right first time from Day 5, clever), the drill and inspection (row `xp`), -3 a missed shift, -2 an abandoned job, never below 0. Bands `WORK_BANDS[1] = [0, 8, 18, 30, 40]` (the page's; career_check with section 17's real faults: steady 45-48 at the end of Day 8, a very busy player 35 at the end of Day 4). `worksBand()` is null before SHIP.works.
- **Agreed with the designer (0.44.1):** Torres is off on Fridays, so m7 and p3 are taught by the first on shift of Torres, Emeka Halloran, Calder (Emeka on Day 5); m2's "an oily job turned in" is `flag('works.oily')`, set by 24b for oily turn-ins from Day 8.
- **Other sections' tabs and words.** `worksTabAdd({ id, title, order, view() -> blocks, act? })` adds a Works app tab (24k). Words come from the head writer's keys (content/works/keys-p5.md) through `worksText(key, standIn, vars)`; meetings `train.<id>.<teacher>`; teachers' step words from their own `work` lines (`worksVoice`).

## Pillar 5 mechanics: rounds, your work uniform, the drill and the inspection, hauling (seat M1, 0.45.x)

Sub-blocks of section 24 after 24 core (`// 24a ROUNDS`, `// 24c DRILL AND INSPECTION`), each removable: without one, its ladder row never opens. Pages: `plans/mechanics/m1-rounds.html`, `m3-drill-inspection.html` (claude/p5-design). Nothing calls `R()`; keys 860-861 (the route, the sign) and 864 (the duty officer's rota).

- **Rounds (24a, ladder row m1).** `jobKindAdd('rounds', ...)` (section 17's 0.33.47), `hold: 'datapad'` (the clipboard, section 15b's 0.28.73). `roundRoute(day, train)`: the deck by day (4, 3, 5; a deck with fewer than 4 machines to look at is skipped, so today Deck 5 is never used), 4-6 of its machines at your tier with a panel place (`schedU(day, 0, 860)` for how many, `schedU(seed, day, 860)` for which), in nearest-next order from the lift; the training round is Deck 4, a washer first (Torres's first line is about one). Each stop is `how: 'look'` with `opts.text` = `roundSign(id, day)` (the writer's `sign.<kind>.<healthy|worn|faulty>`, picked by `schedU(machSeed(id), day, 861)`; a stand-in per state until the lines are baked) and three marks. `roundMark` (the kind's `stepDone`): worn or off on an unhealthy machine ("off" on a merely worn one counts too) → `machineFound(id, 'you')`; when `fresh`, +1 Works reputation and "Found by you: Washer 1." as the step's words (returned for section 15's after-step line); a mark on a healthy machine → "Looks all right to me."; a training round's teacher reacts with `worksTeach('m1', by, 'mark.right' | 'mark.okay' | 'mark.wrong')`. Never a penalty. `roundDone`: a round finished by someone else (the teacher's, the crew's) finds every unhealthy machine on it for them; yours is `worksUse('m1')`. The morning round: `def.post` on your workdays from the day after you're trained, at 07:00, `leave: 180` (held for you to 11:00, outside HOLD_MAX), then the crew's (`crew.mins` 25, Works crew). Training (since 0.45.9, playtest 2: Torres explains at the meeting, then your first round, the whole route, is on the board for you; `roundTrainStep()` drives U2's arrow and tips; the walk-along below is how it was): `WORK_MECH.m1.at` = 10:25 (so 24 core books Torres's meeting from 10:35, after the 10:30 tea: reviewer R2's FAIL on 0.45.0, fixed in 0.45.2); `WORK_MECH.m1.reserve` makes the teacher's round (the route's first two stops) and then yours (the rest), in 24 core's slot; Torres's `lead.look` lines once you're with him (`WORK_TICKS`). Saved on the job: `j.route`, `j.marks`. Not yet: stage 2 (your own route after 5 rounds) and stage 3 (a room from its doorway with the scanner): `worksStage('m1')` already reads 1 after 5 rounds.
- **The inspection (24c, row m3i, Thursday 06:30).** `inspectItems(d)`: the uniform (0.60.7's `SHIP.clothes.changed === d` and not grubby by section 15's `uniformState`, M2), the bed (`SHIP.player.bedMade === d`, section 15's 0.28.73 Make the bed), the locker (`lockerTidy()`, 0.28.73); either missing from the build counts as right. `inspectResult`: nod / uniform / bed / locker / two / none (the writer's `inspect.*` and `inspect.mail.*`). On `WORK_TICKS`: Thursdays on your workdays while you're a junior, once the row has opened; in your bunk room 06:30-06:59 the duty officer says it (`inspectOfficer`: Calder the first time, then an officer on shift by the week, key 864); else Mail at 07:00 (also after a sleep through it). Turning up, or a nod while you were out, is `worksUse('m3i')` (the row's +1, and the first is the training). `simNote('inspection', { day, uniform, bed, locker, result, by, here })`.
- **The drill (24c, row m3d, Wednesday 15:00).** `drillKind(d)` = fire, pressure, power by week (Day 3 fire). `jobKindAdd('drill', { side: true })`: posted for you at 14:50 on your Wednesdays as a junior (`reserved: true`, never the crew's), already yours when it's posted (0.45.5: never on the board, so neither you nor a check's `claimJob` can take it as a second job), with a toast and the arrow at 14:50; stops at `DECKS[5].drill` (the Pillar 5 deck architect's 0.47.x): fire = take at the bracket (`drillExtShow(false)`), stand 10 minutes at the hatch, put back; pressure = `turn` the bulkhead wheel four steps, then stand; power = the breaker cabinet. Over at 15:15 whatever (no lapse; `drillClose`, and a drill from an earlier day still open after a jump or a long sleep is closed quietly on the next tick: 0.45.6), `simNote('drill', { kind, mins, you })` (minutes from 15:00 to your first stop), Calder's `drill.time.fast | ok | slow` (3 / 6 minutes), `worksUse('m3d')` if you turned up. **Only with the drill places in the build** (`WORK_MECH.m3d` is registered when `5-drill-ext`, `-hatch`, `-door` and `-breaker` exist), so in 0.45.0 alone it never opens. The extinguisher in your hand between take and put-back (`def.hold`, section 15's 0.28.73; 8b's `HOLDS.extinguisher` and `PROP_KIT.extinguisher`, one small mesh in place of the toolbox: no draw calls added). The Calendar's fixed Wednesday line (section 19's `DEV_WEEK[2]`) reads, through a getter set from 24c (`drillCalendar`, no edit to 19), "Drill at 15:00: Fire drill. Muster at the Works bay" for the week's kind while the drill is yours. Calder's word at the Friday tea (works-tea or new-hands-tea, 18:45-19:30, on Deck 5) after a quick drill you turned up to (`drillTimeWord` fast): once, `simNote('drillTea')`. Not yet: the Works crew mustering (section 14, sim on call).
- **Hauling (24d, ladder row m4, Wednesdays).** Reads section 17's 0.33.50: `SHIP.manifest` (2-4 crates at the Wednesday delivery, only while this kind is registered: `haulOn()`), `manifestTake(id)`, `holdCrates()`. `jobKindAdd('haul', { junior: true })`: a run takes up to `CART_CRATES` crates (`haulCrates`: by deck from the hold upward, so the fewest lift rides); stops = a cart at `CART_AT`, a `load` per crate at `HOLD_AT` (`manifestTake` once each, `j.got`), a `deliver` per crate at its `dest` (the deck architect's `<n>-<room>-drop` once in, else the delivery's own place; `simNote('haul', { stop, id })`), the cart back; each stop's `hold` ('cart' / 'cartfull', section 17's 0.33.51 `stop.hold` for the crew's walk) and `def.hold(j)` for yours (section 15's 0.28.73). Posted once the unload is done (or from 13:00), at most one run open; a crate left over makes the next run. Before you're trained (or above junior) it's the crew's at once; once trained it's held for you to 16:00 (`leave`). The crew's run, or the teacher's, takes the crates it hadn't loaded (`done`). Training: `WORK_MECH.m4.at` = 11:00 (24 core's 0.44.8: the meeting after the delivery), `reserve` makes Yara's run (the cart and the first crate, `j.only = 'load'`; her `done` leaves that crate on the manifest, reviewer R2's 0.45.1 FAIL) and then yours (every crate, hers included: you load it again and deliver it); an ordinary run waiting that day stands down. Not yet: stage 2 (choose the order) and stage 3 (the stocktake: `SHIP.misplaced` is section 17's, ready).
- **Your work uniform (24b, ladder row m2, from Day 8).** Reads section 21b's 0.42.46 (onboarding 4): `SHIP.clothes.grime`, `clothesGrime(add)`, `uniformState()` ('grubby' from .6; a wardrobe change clears it, a shower doesn't; grubby drains hygiene like yesterday's clothes; `simNote('uniform')`). Each job you turn in from Day 8 adds grime by its machine: `OILY` kinds (oven, range, extractor, galley plant, cart dock, lift, pumps, heat pumps, fan, irrigation) .35 a repair or overhaul, .2 a service; anything else .05. So two oily jobs and you're grubby. The first oily one sets flag `works.oily`, which opens the small row m2 (24 core); `WORK_MECH.m2.open` plays Torres's word (the writer's `train.m2.torres-zeke` meeting through 24 core's `worksTrained`; his two lines in a bubble, or by Mail, while the meeting isn't baked) and is the row's use. Grubby, the player's body meshes (not props) swap to `UNIFORM_MAT`, one cached copy of `CHAR_MAT` tinted `col('#e1ded9')` (a shade duller), checked once a ship minute on `WORK_TICKS`: no new material per frame, no draw calls added. The inspection (24c) reads it. Registered only when `uniformState` is in the build. Not yet (the lead: stages stay out of Steps 1-2): stage 2, a worn washer's service held for you while your wash runs.
- **Held means held.** A round and a manifest run carry `j.shareSeen = true` from the start, so section 17's `shareOut` (every third small job to the crew at once) never gives them away early: the pages hold them for you to 11:00 and 16:00.
- **mech_check parts:** `PARTS['m1']` (Day 2's meeting with Torres at 10:35 or later; Day 3's round on paper, with a save and reload (the jobs through JSON and `jobsTidy`) after two stops: posted, held to 11:00, look stops, worn ones found by you with reputation, nothing for healthy ones, an untaken round done by the crew, stage 2 after 5, none on days off, the same twice), `PARTS['m3d']` (each kind's job and the all-clear, the same twice), `PARTS['m3i']` (five sets of items, in person and by Mail), `PARTS['m2']` (no grime before Day 8; .05, +.35, +.2 to grubby; the oily flag; the tint on and off), `PARTS['m4']` (Yara's training: every crate delivered; Day 10's run: posted after the unload, held to 16:00, every crate once, the cart in hand; five crates in two runs; the crew's run on the next delivery day when you don't; the same twice).
## Tuning and incidents (24e, 24f; Pillar 5 seat M2, 0.51.x)
`plans/mechanics/m5-tuning.html` and `m6-incidents.html`. Both are sub-blocks of section 24 after 24 core (and after M1's 24a-24d), removable on their own.
- **24e tuning.** Section 17 (seat S, 0.33.48) keeps the drift (`SHIP.machines[id].drift`, `machineDrift(id)`, `tuneSet(id, v)`) and posts a `'tune'` job (`jobMake('tune', { machine, ... })`) for a drift of 2+. 24e registers the kind (only when `tuneSet` exists): one stop at the machine's panel, `how: 'turn'` from the real drift (−3…+3), each press calls `tuneSet` so the machine's look follows at once; done at 0. The card shows words only (`tuneWord(kind, v)`; the writer's `tune.word.<kind>.<±n>`, `tune.card`, `tune.title`). The crew settle a tune job in 15 minutes (Works crew). Ladder row m5: opening it drifts Ovens 1 and 2 two steps hot; `WORK_MECH.m5.reserve` makes the training's two jobs (Oven 1 the teacher's, Oven 2 yours) in the teacher's slot; `watch: 10`. Yours turned in: `worksUse('m5')`, Brennan's `train.m5.do.done`. Stage 2 (scrubbers, two settings, Officer) and stage 3 (the scanner) come with Step 3. Saves nothing of its own.
- **24f incidents.** `INC_KINDS` burst (Decks 3-5), lift, alarm (the galley plant). A kind `'incident'` (urgency 2): burst = choose the valve at `n-valve-panel` (right: the last of `DECKS[n].valveLine`, the nearest upstream of the wet; a wrong one: "Still running", the stop stays undone with section 17's `def.veto`) then mop `n-wet-1`, `n-wet-2` (holds, 3 min each); lift = turn the brake at `5-lift-brake` to 3, then check the car at `4-lift-1`; alarm = hold at the alarm panel (`3-galleyplant-alarm` or seat A's `3-extractor-alarm`), wait 5 minutes at `3-vent-1` (vents open), look at the hood. Cleared (`def.done`): the burst's joint job (`jointJobPost`), or the lift's or galley plant's repair found by whoever cleared it (`machineFound`); yours held for you to the end of the shift. The start sets the lift or galley plant faulty (`machineFault(id, 40)`) and the lift stuck (`liftStuck(true)`, freed at the brake or after 20 minutes; set again after a reload). **The week's plan** (`incPlan(w)`, weeks from Day 15; week 1 has the training's): a weekday you work, not a ship-wide event day or a test job's day, 09:00-15:30 (before 11:30 on a tradition evening's day), the kind weighted by wear (keys 870 day and minute, 871 kind, 872 the burst's deck, 873 the crew's minutes). Held for you 30 minutes when you're trained and on shift (a toast), else the crew's (25-40 minutes). An incident in hours skipped (sleep, a skip) is cleared by the crew. **Training (m6):** an urgent call, no Mail: `WORK_MECH.m6.offer` (24 core's hook, 0.44.7) makes the meeting at 10:30; at 10:30 the burst on Deck 4 starts and Calder's call; her walk to the pipe (`watch: 1`), then the incident is yours; when it's cleared the training's job becomes the joint (`c.j` moved to the fix), so you're trained when the joint is turned in. The stuck lift is the page's fallback (nobody held in the car; "check the car").
- **Drawn:** a puddle (two discs, 1.2 cm off the floor, one instanced draw call) during a burst on your deck, and a smoke wisp (six quads, one draw call) by the galley vent during the alarm; made in `deckRoot` the first time they're needed on a deck, so the deck's unload frees them. +1 draw call each, only then.
- **Saved:** `ship.works.inc = { plan: { week: [kind, day, minute, deck, done] | [] }, open: null | { kind, deck, at, train, safe, j } }`; 24 core's `worksClean` keeps only its own keys, so 24f reads its part back itself (`incClean`: old weeks dropped, junk dropped). A save from before 24f has none: plans are made as their weeks come.
- **Tests:** `mech_check.py m5 m6` (the frame's training, then PARTS m5 and m6: settles only at 0, the look follows each press, words not numbers, the crew settle; the plan over four weeks, each incident's steps in order, the wrong valve, the fix posted, the lift freed, the crew clear one, a skipped hour's, the save read back, the same twice).

### Team jobs (24h, seat M2, 0.51.2)

See the 0.51.2 build row. Owners: the 'team' kind, the crew plans and the finish S2 (section 17), the ladder row m8 C2, team spots DA5 (`<machine>-team-N` or `4-washer-team-N`; until then the machine's panel), crew talk X (reads `j.team`, `j.start`/`j.done`, `j.seq`/`j.cur`, `simNote('team', { id, step, by, kind: 'start' | 'step' | 'turn' | 'late' | 'done' })`), lines W2 (`team.*`, `mail.team`, `works.ask.m8`, `train.m8.*`). Stage 2 (bringing the parts) is Step 4.

## Known issues

- **Breakfast seat share on day 3** (found by the traditions engineer at 0.34.56, there in 0.60.6a too): day_check day 3 finds '3-mess-42 walsh-esme/kovac-zainab from 07:38' settled in one seat (0.60.6a also '3-mess-16 dlamini-lukas/silva-bea from 18:24'). For the sim or galley seat; not held for 0.60.7 (lead 11).

- 0.5.8 against 0.5.7: paths, collisions, crew, taps and the random seed match; screenshots, hull runs and draw calls differ as expected. Later regressions compare against 0.5.8.
- From the default camera, the cutaway lowers the near-side lift doors, so you only see them slide from the far side.
- Rooms whose door faces away from the camera have their furniture at the camera end, so it's often faded. Rotate to see it.
- The tea corner is tight: everything is reached from its doorway tile.
- Washers, showers, the wash pod, the fabricator and the lathe are props until machines arrive in step 7. The job board's cards are static.
- A deck's plan must keep its flat sides on the map's top and bottom edges.
- A room needs to be deep enough for its template; bunk rooms need at least 4 tiles of depth.


## Next

**6d. Deck 3, the Commons.** The plan is agreed: plans/deck3-plan.html, 52 × 22, `lift: [20, 11]`. Built one turn at a time:

1. **The shell (done, 0.6.2).** Every room is walkable and named. Sealed: galley plant `g` (hatch `h,39,3`), water recycler `R` (hatch `v,17,14`), irrigation room `i` (hatch `h,3,17`), and the ladder hatch `h,20,13`.
2. **The mess hall, galley and cold store (done, 0.6.3).**
    - **Mess hall** (room-local, 12 × 9): long tables at (2.6, 1.0), (7.4, 1.0), (2.6, 4.0), (7.4, 4.0); round tables at z 6.9. Rows 2, 5 and 8 and columns 27–28 are left clear as aisles. The line runs down the galley wall (column 34) from north to south: drinks, tray return, trays and cutlery, the servery against the pass, grab-and-go; tile (34, 8) stays clear for the staff door. Customers queue in column 33.
    - **Galley:** ranges on the bow wall, ovens beside the cold store door, the prep island in the middle, the dish pit by the back door, Brennan's stool.
    - **Cold store:** three frosted shelf units along the hull, a chest freezer and crates.
    - **`pass` edges** (`v,35,4`–`v,35,6`) are glass edges with no pane: the sill (1 m) is the counter, open above, header on top. They block walking. `passShelf` puts a steel top through the wall and a heat lamp under the header.
    - New kit: `longTable`, `roundTable`, `servery`, `trayStack`, `trayReturn`, `drinksStation`, `grabShelf`, `ovenStack`, `prepIsland`, `dishPit`, `coldShelf`, `chestFreezer`; wall kit `menuBoard`, `passShelf`. Materials in `MESS`. None call `R()`.
3. **Curved inner walls, then the lounge (done, 0.6.4).** See "Curved inner walls" and "The bow lounge" above.
4. **The infirmary, exam room, garden, washrooms, gym, crew store and call-home booths (done, 0.6.5).** See "Deck 3, turn 4" above.
5. **Crew (done, 0.6.7).** Chef Brennan works the galley; Rina works in the infirmary. Deck 4's crew routes changed when they left.

**6e. Build the rest of the ship: Decks 6, 1 and 2 (next, agreed 2026-10-15).** The user's priority is getting the whole ship built. Crew, machines, supplies and jobs come after.

- **Who:** the deck architect session builds; the lead session reviews and merges (see TEAM.md).
- **Scope:** shell, rooms, dressing and furniture only. No crew, no shifts, no machines, jobs or supply runs, which are step 7 and later. Rooms that will later need crew (the engine watch room, the bridge stations) get furniture but no people.
- **Order:** Deck 6 (Engineering), then Deck 1 (Bridge), then Deck 2 (Command & Science, once its plan is final). After each deck: stop for the user's review and sign-off (CLAUDE.md).
- **Decisions:**
    - **Bridge command ring:** faked for now with a rail, a painted step edge and a curved step strip; no engine change. A real sunken floor can come later.
    - **Access:** the lift reaches Decks 6 and 1 from day one. Locking by tier or story comes later with the story work.
    - **Mood lighting per deck:** yes, warmer and dimmer on Deck 6, cooler on the bridge, as long as Decks 3, 4 and 5 stay pixel-identical.
    - **Bridge washroom:** keep it.
- **Progress, Deck 6:**
    1. **The shell (done, 0.7.1).** Every open room (lobby, overlook, watch room) is reachable; E, K and R are locked and stay solid (`NO_FADE`). About 230 draw calls by the lift.
    2. **The engine room (done, 0.7.2).** Room-local, `E` is 10 × 12 with the stern hull at x 0. Drive housing at (.55, 6) against the stern (`'w'`, 8.6 long); coils at x 2.6, 5.1, 7.6 on z 6, phases 0, −2.1, −4.2; copper feeds at y 2.4 between housing and coils; catwalks at z 3.1 and 8.9 (8.4 long) and a cross landing at x 9.35 by the stair door (`v,12,5`). All `see` taps. Overlook `O`: rails at x .2 either side of the stair door, benches at (2.3, 2.5) and (2.3, 7.5) facing the window.
        - **`PULSES`** (new): `{ m, key, base, amp, w, ph }`; the main loop sets `m[key] = base + amp * (.5 + .5 sin(t w + ph))`. Reset with each deck, like `SPINS`. Pulsing materials are made in the kit at dress time (owned by the deck, freed on unload), so each coil has its own phase. Don't pulse a material that a fading tall piece clones: `E` is `NO_FADE`, so its pieces never get cloned.
    3. **The reactor and engine control (done, 0.7.3).** Reactor `R` (room-local 9 × 12): `reactorDrum` at (4.4, 6), loop radius 3.0. Engine control `K` (5 × 4): `controlBoard` at (4.45, 2) against the reactor glass (`'e'`), chairs at x 3.4 facing it, `mimicPanel` on the north wall at 2.5. `trefoil` signs on `h,26,3` and `h,26,4`, in the alcove by the reactor door.
    4. **The watch room, lobby and mood lighting (done, 0.7.4).** Watch room `S` (2 × 3): armchairs at (.55, .55) and (.55, 2.45) facing each other, the kettle (`dispenser`) at (1.55, .4), a lamp (the deck's one real lamp) at (1.6, 2.6); tiles (0, 1) (the door) and (1, 1) (in front of the junction hatch) stay clear. Lobby: `coolantRiser` in its four corners, a bench at (2.5, 9.55).
        - **Mood lighting** (new): a deck definition may carry `light: { sky, sun, hemi, sunI }`. `applyLight` multiplies the hemisphere and sun colours by `sky` and `sun` and their intensities by `hemi` and `sunI`, after the day–night blend. Only uniforms change, so no shader recompiles; decks without `light` run exactly the old code. Deck 6: `sky #ffd2a6`, `sun #ffc48c`, `hemi .8`, `sunI .78`.
    - **Deck 6 is done and waiting for the user's review** (0.7.4).
    - **Review fixes (0.11.0):** the user couldn't get into the engine room ("the back room, going left on screen; stuff is in the way") and found the watch room too small.
        - **Engine room `E`, now walkable:** `v,12,5` is a door; the catwalks are removed; `overlookRail` at z 4.85 and 7.15 (7.6 long) either side of the coils; `markings: [[3, 4, 8, 4]]`. The coils block room-local rows 5–6, x 1–8; rows 1–4 and 7–10 and column 9 (inside the door) are clear. `E` is no longer `NO_FADE`, so the coils fade when they'd hide you; `driveCoil`'s core mesh is `noMerge` so the fade's material copies don't stop it pulsing.
        - **Watch room `S`, 4 × 4 at the overlook's south end:** room-local armchairs at (.9, 1.1) and (.9, 2.9) facing the window (`'e'`), tea table (.7, 2.0), kettle (3.55, .35), lamp (3.6, 3.6), heater on the south wall, log shelf on the north wall. 13 of its 16 tiles are free. The overlook is now rows 1–6 with one bench.
        - **The old corner (cols 22–23, rows 8–10) is lobby** now, with a bench at lobby-local (7.0, 7.3) and the fourth coolant riser moved to (7.65, 9.65).
- **Deck 1's empty shell draws 593 calls.** Bring that down before dressing it, without changing the hull output on Decks 3, 4 and 5.
- **Plans:**
    - plans/deck6-plan.html and plans/deck1-plan.html are agreed.
    - plans/deck2-plan.html is agreed at Sketch 8 (approved by the lead for the user, 2026-10-15). It's 52 × 22 on Deck 3's outline, with the lift at [20, 11].
        - Aft of the lift: a looped carpeted senior corridor, and 15 bedrooms (12 crew plus 3 visitor rooms), each with its own door. The Captain's quarters across the stern are the biggest bedroom (60 tiles).
        - Forward of the lift: science, off a four-wide corridor. The sensor and comms array is a sealed room in the nose (106 tiles).
        - The lift's stern door opens to the quarters and its bow door to science.
        - Open, with defaults until the user says otherwise: the six invented officers are placeholders, and the 15th room is a visitors' cabin.

**7. The Living Ship (next big phase, agreed 2026-10-15).** Crew and simulation as one phase. It replaces the old step 7 (machines, supplies and the shared job list), which is now Pillar 2. The full design is in **design-doc.md** ("The living ship", "Crew simulation", "Social life", "Ship-wide events" and "Milestones"): no fail state, a 30-minute ship day, all 75 crew with varied routines, ship systems the crew tend, relationships and gossip, and ship-wide events as the main missions. Built in four pillars, each reviewed when it's done:
1. Foundation: clock, rota, the 75-crew roster, simulation of crew on other decks.
2. Ship systems and jobs.
3. Social.
4. Events, starting with the ion storm.


## Agreed ideas waiting for their milestone

- **You first arrive by shuttle** in the Deck 5 bay. The Tern is there with its ramp down, ready for it. Until the arrival is built, the game starts with you waking in Bunk 1. Still to decide: which milestone builds it, and who meets you off the shuttle.
- **Hygiene is a fourth need,** and showers restore it (milestone 2).
- **Uniforms get grubby.** You drop yours at the laundry and take a clean one from your locker. A water recycler fault on Deck 3 means weak showers on Deck 4.
- **Hover hand carts** carry supplies from Deck 5's cargo hold up the main lift to Deck 3's cold store and wherever else they're needed. A job for step 7; the carts charge in Deck 5's Cart bay.
- **Shifts will move crew between decks.** Pell then sleeps in Bunk 2 and works on Deck 5.
